The P2.9 tests that read `checks[0]`/`would_run[0]` as `static.complexity` now find it by ID or pass `--only`. 979 tests in all; the integration added 18 (`cupel-checks` 12 metric, measurement, and `combine_metric` tests; `cupel-core` `scheduler` +1, `next_actions` +2; `cupel-cli` `gate` +3), beside the changed assertions above.
- **P2.9 — integration — open items.** A `[gate.tree]` rule on `tests.weakening` can never pass (rejecting it at load is a follow-up). The README still has no check table.

---
- **P2.10 — scoping.** Cupel's `cupel.toml` (from `cupel init --write`, then edited) excludes `crates/cupel-fixtures/**` through the new `[scope] exclude` table ([ADR-065](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_065.md)): gitignore-style globs (a pattern without `/` matches a name at any depth), applied to root detection (which never descends into an excluded directory), the file walk, and `--changed` lists. `vendor`, `node_modules`, `target`, and `.cupel` are always excluded. So the dogfood grades the six product crates.
- **P2.10 — initial findings** (`cupel check --depth quick`, binary at the start of P2.10, product crates): 610 — `test.logic` 162, `clone.pair` 74 and `clone.cluster` 44, `test.weak-assertion` 115, `test.no-assertion` 66, `function.long` 55, `test.duplicate-setup` 35, `complexity.high` 34, `params.many` 17, `dep.cycle` 6, `nesting.deep` 2.
- **P2.10 — Cupel bugs fixed, each with a fixture or regression case.** One `checks[]` entry per check when every language skips ([ADR-064](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_064.md); scheduler regression test). `ModuleGraph` v2 tags each edge `import | declaration | re-export` in all five languages and `static.deps` cycles ignore declarations ([ADR-068](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_068.md); `deps_tree` fixtures, conformance inventories, goldens) — this removed the Rust parent/child `mod` cycles. A loop over a table written in place is parametrization, not test logic ([ADR-069](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_069.md); a table test and a computed-loop test in every language's quality fixture), and in Rust a loop over a SCREAMING_CASE constant too ([ADR-071](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_071.md); `LISTED`). Rust `assert!(a == b)`, `assert!(x.is_none())`/`is_empty()`, `assert_…` helpers, and `assert_cmd`'s `.assert()` are assertions of the right strength ([ADR-070](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_070.md)), and an `assert_…` helper called with a turbofish counts too (`an_assertion_helper_called_with_a_turbofish_is_an_assertion`). `static.complexity` counts a `match`/`switch` once in cyclomatic complexity ([ADR-072](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_072.md); a 12-arm `month` dispatch in every fixture). `static.crap` without a `CoverageMap` passes with a null score and no findings instead of guessing coverage ([ADR-067](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_067.md), superseding ADR-048's no-coverage mode; `no_coverage` setting removed; unit test on a hand-built `CoverageMap`). A `[gate.tree]` rule on a check that needs a range (`tests.weakening`) is a configuration error at load, exit 2, pointing to `[gate.changed]` ([ADR-066](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_066.md); binary test).
- **P2.10 — code fixed.** Every `dep.cycle` (module layering in `cupel-core`: leaf `fact.rs`, `result.rs`, `status.rs`, `config/error.rs`; cycles in `cupel-store`, `cupel-cli`, `cupel-lang`), every `complexity.high`, `function.long`, `params.many`, and `nesting.deep` (long verbs run through `Invocation`; scheduler, scope, schema, store, resolver, and extractor functions split), and every test-quality finding (weak assertions became exact comparisons of whole values; loops and branches moved into named helpers; repeated arrange steps became shared helpers in each crate's `tests/support`). `NormNode::new`/`leaf` replace hand-built trees.
- **P2.10 — final findings** on the same command: 99, all `static.clones` (69 `clone.pair`, 30 `clone.cluster`; 213 similar pairs). `static.complexity`, `static.deps`, `static.dead-code`, and `tests.quality` report nothing; `static.crap` passes with a null score; `tests.weakening` skips on a tree.
- **P2.10 — suppressions.** None: `.cupel/suppressions.toml` does not exist. The only two deferred limitations it may hold (below) produce no warn finding on the tree today (`dead.export` is info and there are none), so nothing needed suppressing.
- **P2.10 — gates.** `[gate.changed]`: `tests.weakening`, `static.complexity`, `static.clones`, `static.deps`, `static.dead-code`, `tests.quality` all `none`, plus `static.complexity.cyclomatic_max <= 10` and `static.deps.cycles == 0`. `[gate.tree]`: the same without `tests.weakening` (ADR-066), but `static.clones` as a ratchet, `static.clones.pairs <= 213` and `static.clones.clusters <= 30`, because the 99 remaining groups (cross-language test scaffolding and same-shape data variants, some across crate boundaries that share no test-support crate) were not all removed in this step. `static.crap.max` joins both gates with coverage (P3.2). `cupel check --gate` exits 0.
- **P2.10 — changed gate open.** `cupel check --changed --gate` against `main` exits 1: `main` is the end of P1, so the range is all of P2. Beyond the clone groups it reports `tests.weakening` findings that are P2's deliberate test changes: 19 `snapshot.rewritten`, 7 `test.deleted`, and 54 `assertion.removed`, almost all where several weak assertions were folded into one exact comparison of a whole list (the weakening check counts assertions and cannot see that one strong assertion replaced several weak ones). The gate is expected to pass once P2 merges to `main`; the count-based `assertion.removed` is a follow-up below.
- **P2.10 — make dogfood and CI.** `make dogfood` runs `cupel check --changed --gate --format text` (`DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` drops `--changed`); the CI `dogfood` job checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`, runs the tree gate on `main` and the changed gate on branches (documented in the Makefile comment). Until P2 is on `main`, `make dogfood` on this branch fails for the reason above; `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` passes.
- **P2.10 — tests.** 1013 in all; `make check` green.
- **P3 follow-up list.** (1) A `References` fact so `static.dead-code` decides reachability per call, not per module ([ADR-058](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_058.md)). (2) A raw-token digest on `NormTree` so `static.clones` can tell type 1 from type 2 ([ADR-050](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_050.md)). (3) Bring `static.clones` on the Cupel tree to zero and replace the ratchet with `none`. (4) `tests.weakening`'s `assertion.removed` should weigh strength, not count, so folding weak assertions into one exact one is not a weakening. (5) `static.crap.max` in both gates once coverage exists (P3.2).
- **P2 — close-out** (2026-10-14; each DoD line checked against the release binary, `cargo build --release`, and `make check`). (1) *Goldens in all five languages:* met. `crates/cupel-fixtures/golden/` holds one envelope per (check, language), 35 in all; `describe matrix` marks all 35 cells `supported`, so no skip assertion is needed; the matrix test (`every_pair_has_its_golden_or_its_skip_and_no_golden_is_stale`, `every_check_runs_given_only_its_declared_facts`) passes. (2) *`cupel survey --depth quick` on the Cupel repo in < 5 s:* met. 0.64 s wall (five warm runs, 0.64–0.66 s) and 0.69 s on a fresh clone with no `.cupel/`; 216 files, 2816 symbols, exit 0. (3) *`cupel check --changed --gate` passes in CI on the Cupel repo:* not met on this branch, by construction, and met by the mechanism that replaces it. Against `main`, which is still the end of P1, the range is all of P2 and the changed gate exits 1: `tests.weakening` 80 (54 `assertion.removed`, 19 `snapshot.rewritten`, 7 `test.deleted`; P2's deliberate test rewrites, see "P2.10 — changed gate open") and `static.clones` 96 (67 pairs, 29 clusters, the groups the tree ratchet holds), plus 2 `dead.export` at info. The tree gate passes (`make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exit 0; clones at the ratchet, 213 pairs and 30 clusters). After the merge, CI runs the tree gate on `main` and the changed gate on every branch against the new `main`, which is where this line is satisfied. (4) *`describe` covers every shipped check and finding kind:* met. `describe matrix` lists the seven checks and `describe <id>` exits 0 for each; the 23 finding kinds in the manifests are exactly the `valid` list of `describe finding <unknown>`, and `describe finding <kind>` exits 0 for each and names its check. The new `every_finding_kind_in_the_check_table_is_described` test in `crates/cupel-cli/tests/readme.rs` makes the second half mechanical. `describe --all` carries the CLI, verbs, and schemas but not checks; the check registry is reached through `describe matrix` and `describe <id>`. (5) *README:* met. Rewritten from the real binary: usage for every built verb, captured output (a `check --changed --gate` envelope as JSON and text, `survey`, `explain`, `baseline diff`, `suppress`) from a small Python demo repository with a branch that adds a complex function, a copied function, a loosened assertion, and a skipped test; gate configuration and rule grammar; the agent loop and exit codes. The exit-code table, check table, and support matrix are generated by `crates/cupel-cli/tests/readme.rs` from `cupel describe` and `make readme` rewrites them; `make test` fails while they are stale ([ADR-073](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_073.md)). This closes the "no README check table" items in the P2.3, P2.6, and P2.9 notes.
- **P2 — close-out: found while writing the README, not fixed.** (1) `cupel describe --agent` is P8.3 and exits 2 with `cli.not-implemented`, so the README's Agents section is built on `describe --all`, `describe <check>`, `--describe`, and `--dry-run`. (2) `--since-baseline` is described as `pending P2.1`, but P2.1 is complete and did not build it; the flag is accepted and ignored (a tree scope runs, exit 0), as are the other pending scope flags (`--budget`, `--oracle`, `--cases`). Its owning step needs choosing, and a pending flag should arguably be a `cli.not-implemented` usage error as `--format sarif` is. (3) Under `--changed` or `--paths`, `static.clones` sees only the symbols in scope (facts are produced for the scoped files), so a new function copied from an unchanged one is not reported by `check --changed`; `survey` and tree-scope `check` find it. This is the clones form of the P2.8 narrowed-scope item, and ADR-050's "a group is reported when any member is in scope" holds only when every member's file is in scope. (4) `explain <check-id>` still returns only the stored score (P2.9 open items), and `explain` of a `clone.pair` shows only the first member's excerpt (P2.4 notes).
- **P2 — close-out: tests.** 1015 in all (two new in `readme.rs`); `make check` green; `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exit 0.

### P3 — Coverage and mutation

**Goal:** Tier 2–3 evidence: real test execution, coverage ingestion, per-test maps, impacted-test selection, coverage-gated differential mutation testing, flakiness detection, and the mandatory exit-4 refusal when the baseline suite is red.

**Status:** complete (2026-10-28; P3.6 closed by P9.0's gate, [ADR-129](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_129.md))

External tools used here (`cargo-llvm-cov`, `cargo-nextest`, `go`, `pytest`, `pytest-cov`, `vitest`, `jest`) are exercised for real in integration tests when present and reported as skipped when absent. Parsers are always tested against captured real output committed as fixtures.

#### P3.0 Decentralized registration and registry-derived tests

**Status:** complete (2026-10-14, 5b5a13b) · [ADR-074](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_074.md)

P3 fans out to parallel worktrees, each adding a check or a producer. Before it does, remove every line that two such additions would both edit: the `register()` list in `cupel-checks`, the generic-producer table in `cupel-lang`, hand-maintained lists of check IDs, finding kinds, fact kinds, and producers in tests, goldens that render every check at once, and duplicated test helpers. Afterwards, adding a check is literally one module plus goldens (§2.8), and adding a tool producer is one module in `cupel-adapt`.

- Checks register themselves from their own module (`inventory::submit!`); `cupel_checks::register` is derived from the inventory, sorted by check ID, and still panics on a duplicate ID. Write **ADR-074** (supersedes ADR-001's registration consequence): the mechanism, the trade-offs, and what a new check may touch.
- The same mechanism for producers: `cupel-lang`'s generic producers submit themselves from their fact modules; `cupel-adapt` gains `register(&mut ProducerRegistry)` over its own inventory, which every registry builder (CLI catalog, matrix harness) calls now, so a P3 tool producer is one file.
- Tests derive their expectations from the registries: every registered check and finding kind describes; every `supported` cell has a golden; `cells.len() == checks × languages`. No test lists check IDs, finding kinds, fact kinds, or producers.
- One golden directory per check: everything a golden pins about one check lives under `golden/<check-id>/`; goldens that render the whole registry (survey, describe matrix and language) keep only what no single check owns.
- Test helpers duplicated across test files (facts, contexts, fixture paths, temporary git repositories, running the binary) move into one shared module per crate, or `cupel-fixtures` where several crates use them. Clone counts before and after go in Notes; the `static.clones` tree ratchets may only go down.

*Tests first:* a test that `lib.rs` names no check type (fails while `register()` lists them); a duplicate submission panics with "native check IDs are unique"; a producer submitted from a test binary reaches `cupel_adapt::register`; registry-derived describe coverage fails when a registered check is not described.

*Done when:* a new check touches only its module file, its `mod` line, its fixtures, and `golden/<check-id>/`; a new tool producer touches only its module file and its `mod` line; `make check` and `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exit 0.

#### P3.1 Fact: `TestResults` — runner producers and baseline test run (exit 4)

**Status:** complete (2026-10-14, 53b7405) · [ADR-075](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_075.md) · [ADR-076](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_076.md)

- `Runner: Producer<TestResults>` trait (design §8.7); producers for `cargo test`, `cargo nextest`, `go test -json`, `pytest`, `vitest`, `jest`, registered through the P1.9 toolchain table; `list()` and `run(selection, env, timeout)` → `TestResults` (per-test outcome, duration). Each producer = a format parser (tested on committed real output) + an invocation.
- Baseline test run executed once in the real tree before any producer that needs it; red baseline → `status: error`, exit 4, no scores produced. `test_results` index table populated from the fact.
- `doctor` gains tool presence/version checks for every producer the configured checks' needs resolve to.

*Tests first:* parser fixtures for each runner's output format; a fixture with one failing test → `check --only tests.mutation` exits 4 and emits zero mutation findings; `[lang.python] runner = "unittest"` selects that producer.

#### P3.2 Fact: `CoverageMap` — coverage producers, `tests.coverage`, full `static.crap`

**Status:** complete (2026-10-14, 575fa0e) · [ADR-079](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_079.md)

- `Coverage: Producer<CoverageMap>` trait; format parsers for LCOV, Cobertura XML, Istanbul JSON, go coverprofile, JaCoCo XML → `CoverageMap` (line + `branches: Option<_>`). Invocations for Rust (`cargo llvm-cov --lcov`), Go (`go test -coverprofile`, `branches: None`), Python (`pytest --cov --cov-report=lcov`), JS/TS (`vitest --coverage`, `jest --coverage`, `c8`). Ingest existing reports when fresher than sources (mtime + content hash).
- `tests.coverage` check: `needs = [Symbols, CoverageMap]`; `coverage.uncovered-symbol` always; `coverage.uncovered-branch` only when `branches` is present, else the check result notes `branches: unavailable` — it learns this from the fact, not from the language.
- `static.crap` gains `wants = [CoverageMap]`; `--no-coverage` retained.

*Tests first:* each parser against committed real reports; `CoverageMap` property (line hits are non-negative and symbol coverage ∈ [0,1]); CRAP golden with known coverage; Go golden shows no branch findings and the documented note.

#### P3.3 Fact: `PerTestMap` — per-test coverage and `tests --impacted`

**Status:** complete (2026-10-14, ab8f054) · [ADR-006](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_006.md) · [ADR-077](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_077.md)

- `PerTestMap` producers: nextest + llvm-cov per-test profiles (Rust), `go test -coverprofile -run '^TestX$'` per test (Go), pytest-cov `--cov-context=test` (Python). None registered for `ecma` — `describe matrix` shows `PerTestMap`-needing behavior as unsupported for JS/TS, and `tests --impacted` falls back to `ModuleGraph` impact with `per_test_map: false` echoed. Write **ADR-006**.
- Inverted index `line → tests`; `tests --impacted` returns `{id, file, reason}` plus a ready-to-run `command` per runner; says when it fell back.

*Tests first:* a fixture with two tests covering disjoint functions — changing one function yields exactly one impacted test; the JS fixture takes the fallback path and says so.

#### P3.4 Fact: `MutationOutcomes` — the mutation engine and `tests.mutation`

**Status:** complete (2026-10-14, afabf89) · [ADR-080](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_080.md) · [ADR-004](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_004.md)

`cupel-mutate` is `Producer<MutationOutcomes>` with `needs = [MutationSites, CoverageMap, TestResults]`, `wants = [PerTestMap, ModuleGraph]`, obtaining a `Runner` from the registry. `tests.mutation` is a check with `needs = [Symbols, MutationOutcomes]`, `wants = [TestConventions]`, that only scores and writes findings. Built in sub-steps, each with its own failing test first:

1. **Sites → mutants**: from `MutationSites`, generate mutant source for each; `mutants` index table.
2. **File-copy overlay**: reflink (APFS/btrfs/XFS) else hardlink tree + private copy of mutated file plus every file `ModuleGraph` marks as a relative importer of it. Mutants of one file run concurrently, files sequentially.
3. **Coverage gating**: uncovered sites are recorded `uncovered` and never executed.
4. **Per-test selection**: only tests covering the mutated line run when `PerTestMap` is present; full suite otherwise.
5. **Timeouts**: `timeout_factor × baseline_duration(test)`, floor 2 s → `timeout` outcome.
6. **Store-based differential reruns**: outcome reused when `(symbol_hash, site_hash, suite_hash, toolchain_hash)` unchanged; scores recomputed from the store.
7. **Ordering**: last run's survivors → sites in changed symbols → rest.
8. **`tests.mutation` check**: `mutant.survived` (with `from`, `to`, `operator`, `covering_tests`, `nearest_test_file` from `TestConventions`), `mutant.timeout`, `mutant.uncovered`, `mutant.equivalent?` per **ADR-004**. Score = killed / (killed + survived).
9. Depth mapping: `standard` = changed symbols, `deep` = touched files, `exhaustive` = tree — applied by the engine's `plan()`.

*Tests first:* a fixture per language with a function and a deliberately weak test suite where the surviving set is known exactly; golden asserts the exact survivors; a second run with no changes executes zero mutants (differential); a change to one test re-executes only the sites it covers; `tests.mutation` run against a hand-built `MutationOutcomes` fact (no engine) produces the expected findings — proving the check is independent of how outcomes were produced.

#### P3.5 `tests.flaky`

**Status:** complete (2026-10-14, 71a9e28) · [ADR-078](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_078.md)

- A flake engine (`Producer<TestResults>` variant producing repeated runs, registered as `engine.flake`) reruns impacted tests N times (default 3 at `standard`) with shuffled order, varied seeds, varied parallelism. `tests.flaky` check consumes the repeated results and `TestFrames`. Kinds `test.flaky`, `test.order-dependent`; action `investigate_flake`.

*Tests first:* a fixture test that depends on a global mutated by another test → `test.order-dependent`.

#### P3.6 Dogfood: mutation gate

**Status:** complete (2026-10-20, 6c254ce; [ADR-129](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_129.md)) · `[gate.changed] "tests.mutation.score" = ">= 0.80"` is on the repo since 55280e4 (P9.0) and `make dogfood` runs the changed gate at `--depth deep` on every push (CI's `dogfood` job); the initial score, 0.875 (21 of 24 killed), and the fixes are in P9 Notes "P9.0 — dogfood". The per-push cost that blocked this step is remeasured in P4 Notes "P4 — Definition of Done, remeasured" and stays an open item there.

- `[gate.changed] "tests.mutation.score" = ">= 0.80"` on the Cupel repo. Fix Cupel's own tests until it passes. Record the initial score and what was fixed in Notes.

#### P3.7 Tool installation: `--install`, `--no-install`, `doctor --fix`

**Status:** complete (2026-10-15, 476c2b1)

Added during P3.1, which the P0.3 and P0.8 notes named as the step that installs tools; P3.1 delivered the runner producers and their `doctor` checks but installs nothing (see P3.1 Notes).

- `MissingTool.install` filled per tool producer (`cargo install cargo-nextest --locked`, `python -m pip install pytest`, a dev dependency through the root's package manager); `doctor --fix` runs it after asking, `--install` without asking, `--no-install` never (the default unless stdout is a terminal, design §6.1 rule 10); every install a dry run would make is listed in `would_install`.

*Tests first:* `doctor --fix --install` on a fixture whose runner is missing installs it into a scratch prefix and then exits 0; `--no-install` leaves the `doctor.tool-missing` finding.

ADRs: [081](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_081.md) (where install commands live, who may install, what is reported).

#### P3 Definition of Done

- All goldens pass; real-tool integration tests pass on a machine with the tools installed (CI installs them).
- `cupel check --changed --depth standard` on a 10-symbol change in the Cupel repo completes in ≤ 3 min (record in Notes).
- Exit 4 is enforced for every check that depends on test execution.
- README: coverage/mutation usage, `tests --impacted`, exit 4, the mutation performance stack.

#### P3 Notes

- **P3.0 — mechanism** ([ADR-074](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_074.md)). `inventory` 0.3.25 (`inventory::submit!` in the module, `inventory::iter` in the entry point). Checked before choosing, on the pinned 1.92 toolchain: it builds under the workspace's `unsafe_code = "forbid"` and collects every submission across crates in debug and in a release build with `lto = true`. `linkme` was rejected because its `#[link_section]` statics expand at the use site, which `forbid(unsafe_code)` refuses ("declaration of a static with `link_section`"); a build script generating `mod` lines through `include!` was rejected because `rustfmt` never visits modules declared that way, so check files would leave `make lint`'s format gate. Three submission types, each with one entry point: `cupel_checks::NativeCheck` → `cupel_checks::register` (sorted by check ID; still panics "native check IDs are unique"); `cupel_lang`'s crate-private `generic::GenericProducer` → `generic::register{,_except}` (sorted by fact kind; the `GENERIC` table in `generic.rs` is gone, each fact module submits its own); `cupel_adapt::ToolProducer { id, register }` → `cupel_adapt::register` (sorted by ID; panics "tool producer IDs are unique"), which `Catalog::builtin` and the matrix harness's `Registries::builtin` call now, with no submissions yet. The language list (`LanguageRegistry::builtin`) and each language's own `register()` in `langs/<id>/mod.rs` stay as they are.
- **P3.0 — the `mod` line.** It is the one shared line; `.gitattributes` gives `crates/{cupel-checks,cupel-adapt,cupel-lang}/src/lib.rs` git's built-in `union` merge driver, so two branches that each add a `mod` line merge with both (checked on a scratch repository; `make fmt` restores the order if they land out of it). `crates/cupel-checks/tests/registration.rs` reads `lib.rs` through `include_str!` and fails if any code line other than its `mod` line names a registered check's module (module name = check ID with `.` and `-` as `_`), and that `register` lists checks in ID order; `crates/cupel-lang/tests/registration.rs` fails if `generic.rs` registers a producer itself. Duplicate IDs are tested by submitting a second `static.complexity` (`cupel-checks/tests/duplicate_ids.rs`) and a doubled `tool.twin` (`cupel-adapt/tests/duplicate_ids.rs`), each its own test binary so the submission reaches no other test; `cupel-adapt/tests/registration.rs` submits a probe producer from the test binary and finds it registered for its language only.
- **P3.0 — what a new check touches.** `crates/cupel-checks/src/<module>.rs` (and its `<module>/` submodules, if any) with one `inventory::submit!`; `mod <module>;` in `crates/cupel-checks/src/lib.rs`; `crates/cupel-checks/tests/<module>.rs`; fixture code, preferably in its own `crates/cupel-fixtures/repos/<check-id>/<lang>/`, which the matrix harness runs the check on instead of `repos/<lang>/` (`matrix::fixture_repo`); and `crates/cupel-fixtures/golden/<check-id>/` (`<lang>.json` per supported language, `describe.json`, `survey-<lang>.json` per language), written by `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 make test`. Plan §2.8's "adding a check later is one module plus goldens" is now literally true, with the `mod` line and the check's own fixtures. The README's generated check table and support matrix gain its rows through `make readme`; they are the one whole-registry artifact left, and a merge conflict there is resolved by rerunning `make readme`.
- **P3.0 — what a new tool producer touches.** `crates/cupel-adapt/src/<module>.rs` with one `inventory::submit!(crate::ToolProducer { .. })`, `mod <module>;` in `crates/cupel-adapt/src/lib.rs`, and its tests and captured-output fixtures. A generic language producer is its fact module in `crates/cupel-lang/src/` with its `GenericProducer` submission and `mod` line; a language-specific one goes in that language's `register()`.
- **P3.0 — lists deleted.** In `crates/cupel-cli/tests/describe.rs`: the seven-check list and the all-supported assertion in the matrix test, the `checks × 5` cell count, the seven-check column in the Rust language test, the `norm-tree` consumer list, five hard-coded lists of the five language IDs, `languages.len() == 5`, and `languages[4]` for TypeScript; all now come from `cupel_checks::register` and `LanguageRegistry::builtin` through `support::{registered_checks, check_ids, language_ids}`. New registry-derived coverage: every registered check describes with exactly its manifest and its matrix row, every registered finding kind describes with its check and action (this replaces `readme.rs`'s `every_finding_kind_in_the_check_table_is_described`, which derived the set from `describe matrix` rather than the registry), the matrix has exactly one cell per registered check and language. Both fail when a check is registered but `describe` leaves it out (checked by making `describe` skip `tests.quality`). The `describe matrix` text snapshot is replaced by a row-per-registered-check assertion; the TypeScript language text snapshot no longer holds check rows (asserted from the matrix) or facts no language producer makes. In `crates/cupel-fixtures`: `describe/matrix.json` lost `checks` and `cells`, `describe/language-<lang>.json` lost `checks` (and keeps only language-layer producers in `facts`), `describe/languages.json` keeps only language-layer fact kinds, `describe/fact-symbols.json` lost `consumers`; each removed part is asserted from the registries in `tests/describe_goldens.rs`. `survey/<lang>.json` keeps only what no check owns; each check's results, findings, and ranking dimensions are `golden/<check-id>/survey-<lang>.json`, and the totals, severity counts, and `next_actions` are asserted against the findings (`cupel_core::next_actions`). Reassembling the slices reproduces the P2 survey goldens exactly. The matrix's stale-golden check claims `describe` and `survey-<lang>` in each registered check's directory.
- **P3.0 — goldens.** Every check's goldens are under `golden/<check-id>/`; no golden file holds two checks' content. The golden loader has no index: a name maps to `golden/<name>.json`, and `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` writes per file.
- **P3.0 — shared test helpers.** `cupel-checks/tests/support`: `check(id)`, `recorded(check, facts, scope, config)`, `setting_error`, `score`, `hash_of_line`, `decided_facts`/`decided_scope` (the `Symbols`+`Decisions`+`NormTree` world of the complexity and CRAP tests), `imported_file`/`imported_package`; the seven test files' copies of registry lookup, plan-and-run, and settings-error matching are gone. `cupel-cli/tests/support`: `described`, `Repo::git`, `usage_argument`, `finding_fields`, `finding`, and `cli_basics.rs` uses the shared `cupel()`. `cupel-core/tests/support`: `lang`, `manifest`, `Declared` (a manifest-only check) for the scheduler, extension, selection, config-registry, and rankings tests. `cupel-lang/tests/support`: one parameterized `sites_of`, site-row `summary`/`site_row`, `site_hashes`, `relaid`, and `assert_every_replacement_parses` replace the per-language copies in the four `*_mutations.rs`; `normalize_at` is shared by Go, Rust, and ECMAScript. The fixture-repository path is `cupel_fixtures::repo_path` everywhere, so `cupel-adapt`, `cupel-lang`, and `cupel-cli` dev-depend on `cupel-fixtures`. Left in place: the small cross-crate copies between `cupel-core` and `cupel-store`/`cupel-cli`/`cupel-lang`/`cupel-checks` tests (a temporary git repository's `write`, a file walk, a `CheckResult` builder, a `ProduceCtx` builder, a `Span` builder); sharing them through `cupel-fixtures` would make `cupel-core`'s and `cupel-store`'s tests depend on the language layer.
- **P3.0 — clones** (`cupel check --depth quick` on the tree, `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree`). Before (896984c): 99 groups (69 `clone.pair`, 30 `clone.cluster`), 213 similar pairs. After: 85 groups (66, 19), 168 similar pairs. `[gate.tree]` ratchets lowered to `static.clones.pairs <= 168` and `static.clones.clusters <= 19`. The registry-coverage tests iterate in helpers that return what they found, so `tests.quality` reports no `test.logic`.
- **P3.0 — crate graph.** `cupel-fixtures` depends on `cupel-adapt` (ADR-074: the matrix harness must see every producer the binary sees). `cupel-adapt`, `cupel-lang`, and `cupel-cli` dev-depend on `cupel-fixtures` (integration tests link the same library instance; the dev-dependency cycle is what `cupel-fixtures` exists for).
- **P3.0 — open items.** (1) `make dogfood` (the changed gate CI runs on branches) fails on this branch, as it did on P2's: `static.clones` reports the pre-existing groups in every file the step touched (each check module gained its submission), and `tests.weakening` reports the moved and rewritten tests (`readme.rs`'s finding-kind test now lives in `describe.rs`, the language text snapshot was rewritten). It passes once the step is on `main`. (2) Checks added before P3.0 keep their fixtures in the shared `repos/<lang>/`; their code shows up in every check's fact scope there, so editing a shared fixture still ripples into other checks' goldens. (3) The TypeScript language text snapshot aligns its facts column to the longest fact name; a producer of a longer-named fact (`mutation-outcomes`, P3.4) realigns it. (4) Language-layer producer additions still change `golden/facts/` and the `cupel-lang` tests that pin fixture contents, as before.
- **P3.0 — tests.** 1024 in all (+9 over P2's 1015); `make check` green at every commit; `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exit 0.
- **P3.1 — `TestResults`** ([ADR-075](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_075.md)). `cupel_core::TestResults` v1 (`test_results.rs`): `runner` (producer ID), `invocations[{dir, command, exit_code, completed, output}]` (one per project root; `completed` false for a build that failed, a crash, or no readable report; `output` the tail when not completed), `tests[{id, file, name, outcome: passed|failed|skipped|errored, duration_ms, output}]` sorted by ID (`output` only for failed/errored, at most `OUTPUT_TAIL_LINES = 40` lines), and `suite_hash` (blake3 of the sorted IDs and the content of every file they name). `is_green()` = every invocation completed and nothing failed or errored; `failures()`, `incomplete()`. IDs per runner: `cargo test` `<binary>/<file>::<path>`, `cargo nextest` `<binary-id> <path>`, `go test` `<package>/<Test>`, `pytest` node IDs, `unittest` dotted IDs, `vitest`/`jest` `<file> > <titles> > <title>`; a root other than `.` prefixes `<dir>: `. Version rule: bump on a renamed/removed/retyped field, an outcome added or redefined, or any runner's ID formation changing. `describe fact test-results` now shows the schema and version 1.
- **P3.1 — what `ProduceCtx` and the producer contract gained** ([ADR-076](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_076.md)). `ProduceCtx.upstream: Upstream` (the facts the producer's `needs` named, produced first for the same language; `upstream.get::<F>()`) and `ProduceCtx::new(ctx, writer, cancel)`, which the six construction sites outside the scheduler now use. `Ctx.lang` (the `[lang.*]` tables) and `Ctx::pin(kind, lang)`; `FactKind::pin_key()` (`TestResults` → `runner`). `Producer::detected(&Ctx)` (default true) and `Producer::tool(&Ctx) -> Option<ToolProbe { name, version }>` (default none), forwarded by `RegisteredProducer`. `ProducerRegistry::candidates(kind, lang, ctx)`: only the pinned producer (`runner = "unittest"` or `"tool.unittest"`; a pin naming nothing is `Err(key)`, which the matrix reports `unconfigured` and doctor `doctor.producer-unknown`), else detected producers first, in registration order, then the rest; both `SupportMatrix` and the scheduler's fact store select through it. `cupel_adapt::register` applies tool producers in toolchain-table order (`toolchain::position`), and runners are detected by their row's rules at the repository root (`toolchain::detects`).
- **P3.1 — processes.** `cupel_core::Process` (`process.rs`): program, arguments, directory, extra environment, `merged()` (stderr into the stdout pipe, in order, through `std::io::pipe`); output read on threads; the child (in its own process group on Unix) polled every 10 ms against the run's `Cancellation` and killed with its group when it fires, returning `Error::Cancelled`, so `--timeout` is exit 5 as everywhere else. `find_program` searches `PATH`. A program that cannot start is `Error::Process`.
- **P3.1 — runners** (`cupel-adapt`, one module each: `cargo_test`, `cargo_nextest`, `go_test`, `pytest`, `unittest`, `vitest`, `jest`; shared `runner.rs` and `report/`). A `RunnerSpec` names the tool, its version command, the command for a root, the parser, and the exit codes that mean "finished with no failure"; `Runner` runs it once per `ProjectInfo` root of its language (`needs = [ProjectInfo]`; the repository root when there is none) and writes reports and nextest's tool profile under `.cupel/cache/runners/<id>/<n>/`. Formats: libtest text (`cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`, merged output), JUnit (`pytest -p no:cacheprovider -o junit_family=xunit1 -o junit_logging=all --junitxml=…` with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`; `cargo nextest run --profile cupel --tool-config-file cupel:<profile>`), test2json (`go test -json <packages>`), unittest text (`python3 -m unittest discover -v -b`, merged), Jest JSON (`jest --ci --json --passWithNoTests --outputFile=…`, `vitest run --reporter=json --passWithNoTests --outputFile=…`). Availability is the program on `PATH` (`cargo`, `cargo-nextest`, `go`, `pytest`, `python3`, `vitest`, `jest`), and for `vitest`/`jest` also the root's `node_modules/.bin`. `roxmltree` 0.21.1 reads JUnit.
- **P3.1 — captures** (`crates/cupel-fixtures/captures/<runner>/<case>/`, each with `capture.toml`: tool version, directory, command, exit code). All seven runners have real `green` and `red` captures of `repos/test-results/<lang>/` (plus its `red/<lang>/` overlay), and `go-test/broken` (a file that does not compile). Tools: cargo 1.92.0, cargo-nextest 0.9.148 (the prebuilt binary, downloaded into the session scratchpad, not installed), go1.23.2, pytest 8.4.2 and Python 3.13.1, jest 30.5.2 and vitest 4.1.11 (npm-installed into the scratchpad). No capture is missing. On this machine `cargo-nextest`, `vitest`, and `jest` are not on `PATH`, so their real-run tests print `SKIPPED: <tool> not installed` in `make check` here; they passed with the scratchpad tools on `PATH`, and CI installs them.
- **P3.1 — fixtures.** `repos/test-results/{rust,go,python,javascript,typescript}/`: one small passing suite each with one skipped test (Rust also an integration test); the Python suite is written for `unittest` so both Python runners run it, and its `pyproject.toml` names pytest so pytest is detected; JavaScript depends on jest, TypeScript (with `tsconfig.json`) on vitest with `globals` so a vitest installed outside the fixture runs it. `repos/test-results/red/<lang>/` adds one failing test that prints a line first.
- **P3.1 — the baseline and exit 4** (ADR-076 §5–8). `Scheduler::run` produces `TestResults`, after planning and before any check, for every language where a selected task's needs reach it (through check needs or any producer's needs: `reaches`); every production of `TestResults` passes `FactStore::gate`, which keeps it for `Outcome.test_results` and withholds a red one (`AbsentFact`), recording it; the run then returns at once with `Outcome.refused: Some(Unevaluable { lang, results })` and no check results. The refusal is an outcome, not a `cupel_core::Error` variant: an `Error` holding `TestResults` made `error → test_results → id → error` a module cycle, which `static.deps` reported. `check.rs` (`schedule`) keeps the outcome's test runs and maps a refusal to `CupelError::Unevaluable`: `status: error`, exit 4, empty `checks[]`, and the findings `baseline.test-failed` (one per failed or errored test; evidence `lang`, `runner`, `test`, `file`, `outcome`, `output`; action `fix_bug`) and `baseline.run-incomplete` (one per incomplete invocation) — the envelope's error list, since design §7.1 has no `errors[]` and error envelopes already carry their errors as findings (ADR-018). `Run::failed` takes `CupelError::findings`.
- **P3.1 — the store.** Migration 2 recreates the never-written `test_results` table as `(run_id, lang, runner, test_id, file, outcome, duration_ms NULL)` with an index; `Store::record_test_results(run, lang, &TestResults)` and `Store::test_results(run) -> Vec<StoredTestResult>`. `Run` keeps the test runs a verb made (`keep_test_results`, red ones included) and `app::record` passes them to `persist::record`, so they are indexed with the run record. The `facts` cache does not hold `TestResults` (no cache key or reuse yet).
- **P3.1 — `doctor`.** For the checks a default `check` run selects at the configured depth and the languages with a project root or a `[lang.<id>]` table, `doctor` resolves each need to the producer the scheduler would choose (first ready candidate, else the first), follows producer needs, and requires each chosen producer's tool (`required: true`, so a missing one is `doctor.tool-missing` at `fail`, exit 3); every probed producer tool's version (first line of its version command) goes in `run.toolchain`. A pin naming no producer is `doctor.producer-unknown` (`configure`, listing the valid producers). With no shipped check needing a tool fact, `doctor` output on real repositories is unchanged today.
- **P3.1 — how exit 4 is proven.** No shipped check needs `TestResults` (`tests.mutation` is P3.4), and the plan's `check --only tests.mutation` test cannot exist yet. `cupel-cli` gained a library target (`src/lib.rs` holds the modules; `main.rs` only reads the process and calls `cupel_cli::run`), and `crates/cupel-cli/tests/runner_baseline.rs` submits a test-only check `test.needs-test-results` (`needs = [TestResults]`) through `inventory::submit!(cupel_checks::NativeCheck(…))` and calls `cupel_cli::run` with an `Environment` it builds: `check --only test.needs-test-results` on a copy of the Python fixture whose `cupel.toml` says `runner = "unittest"` scores 1 of 2 and indexes 2 rows (exit 0); with the red overlay it is exit 4, `status: error`, `checks: []`, one `baseline.test-failed` naming `tests.test_red.RedTest.test_reports_a_wrong_total` in `tests/test_red.py` via `tool.unittest`, and the failed row is indexed; without the pin pytest runs it; `doctor` reports `run.toolchain.python3`; `runner = "nose"` skips the check as `unconfigured` and `doctor` exits 3 with `doctor.producer-unknown`. No hidden flag or variable was added. The unit-level proofs are `cupel-core/tests/baseline.rs` (synthetic runners: once per run, refusal before any check, transitive through a producer's needs, no run without a need, detection and pins, `unconfigured`/`unavailable` skips, upstream roots). The insta snapshots in `cupel-cli/src` were renamed `cupel__…` → `cupel_cli__…` (the unit tests now belong to the library crate); their contents are unchanged.
- **P3.1 — conformance.** Per-fact conformance inventories (`conformance/<fact>.toml`, design §8.3.7) are construct lists a *language* must exhibit and detect; the harness covers `FactKind::SYNTACTIC` only. `TestResults` is a tool fact with no constructs to map, so it has no inventory; its equivalent is that every runner's parser is tested on real captures of the same fixture (`cupel-adapt/tests/reports.rs`) and every runner runs that fixture green and red for real (`cupel-adapt/tests/runners.rs`).
- **P3.1 — CI.** The `check` job (ubuntu and macOS) installs cargo-nextest (`taiki-e/install-action`), Go (`actions/setup-go`, stable), Python 3.12 with pytest (`actions/setup-python`, pip), and Node 22 with vitest and jest (`actions/setup-node`, `npm install --global`), so every real-runner test runs there.
- **P3.1 — deferred.** (1) The design's `Runner` trait (`list()`, `run(selection, env, timeout)`): every runner runs the whole suite today; listing, selection, and per-run environment arrive with their first consumers (P3.3 impacted tests, P3.4 mutants). (2) Installing tools (`--install`, `--no-install`, `doctor --fix`), which earlier notes assigned to P3.1, is the new step P3.7; their `pending_step` now says so. (3) A JavaScript-and-TypeScript project in one root runs its runner once per language. (4) Detection looks at the repository root only, so a JavaScript root in a subdirectory falls back to table order. (5) Runners build their own command lines and do not run the `[lang.<id>] test` command `init` writes (doctor still probes its program). (6) `cargo test` and `unittest` report no durations (null); `cargo test` file paths are relative to each workspace member; nextest's JUnit report omits ignored tests. (7) `TestResults` is not cached in the `facts` table.
- **P3.1 — clones and gates** (`cupel check --depth quick` on the tree). Before (baf7649): 85 groups, 168 similar pairs, 19 clusters. As first written, the runner modules' command functions, the seven real-runner tests, and `TestOutcome::as_str` (shaped like `Format::as_str`) added 19 pairs; they were removed by building commands from `RunnerSpec` argv plus `env`/`merged`, one `jest_report_command`, an `Expected` table per runner, and outcome names by index. After: 84 groups, 167 pairs, 19 clusters; `[gate.tree] static.clones.pairs` lowered to 167. The same pass split `Scheduler::run` (`Execution::run_checks`), `FactStore::produce` (`FactStore::upstream`), the libtest and test2json parsers, `doctor::run`, and the doctor resolver's parameters, and replaced the `Error::Unevaluable` variant with `Outcome::refused` to break the `error → test_results → id → error` cycle. `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exits 0. The changed gate (`make dogfood`) fails on this branch as it did on P3.0's: `tests.weakening` reports the two `describe`/`format` snapshots rewritten for `pending P3.1 → P3.7`, and `static.clones` the 6 pre-existing groups in files the step touched.
- **P3.1 — tests.** 1077 in all (+53 over P3.0's 1024): `cupel-core` `test_results` 7, `process` 8, `baseline` 8; `cupel-adapt` `reports` 11, `runners` 11; `cupel-store` +1; `cupel-cli` `runner_baseline` 5 and doctor unit tests +2. The real-runner tests print `SKIPPED: <tool> not installed` (captured by the harness unless `--nocapture`) where a tool is missing, as the repository's other tool-dependent tests do; here `cargo-nextest`, `vitest`, and `jest` are skipped in `make check` and pass with them on `PATH`. `make check` green at every commit.
- **P3.3 — `PerTestMap`** ([ADR-077](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_077.md)). `cupel_core::PerTestMap` v1 (`per_test.rs`): `runner` (the `TestResults` producer whose IDs it uses), `tests[{id, file, covered[{file, lines}]}]` sorted by ID, lines the test alone executed. `PerTestMap::index()` is the inverted index `file → line → tests`; `LineIndex::covering(file, start, end)` gives each test with the first line it executes. Version rule: bump on a field change, a change of what `lines` means, or the runner's ID formation changing (with `TestResults`).
- **P3.3 — producers** (`cupel-adapt/src/per_test/`, cost `deep`, `needs = [ProjectInfo]`, reports under `.cupel/cache/per-test/<id>/<root>/`). `tool.llvm-cov-per-test` (Rust): `cargo nextest list`, one `cargo llvm-cov clean --workspace`, then per test `cargo llvm-cov clean --profraw-only` and `cargo llvm-cov nextest --no-clean --lcov -E '(binary_id(=…) & test(=…))'`, so the instrumented build happens once (about 0.3 s per test on the fixture). `tool.go-cover-per-test`: `go test -list ^Test -json`, then per test `go test -coverprofile -coverpkg=<packages> -run '^(Test)$' <package>`, import paths mapped to files through the root's `go.mod`. `tool.pytest-cov-per-test`: one pytest run with `--cov=. --cov-context=test`, a coverage rc in `.cupel/` (data file there, `show_contexts`), and the JSON report's per-line contexts (`<node id>|run|setup|teardown`). None for `ecma` ([ADR-006](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_006.md)). **For the integrator:** the private slicers `per_test::executed_lcov_lines` and `per_test::executed_coverprofile_lines` (`crates/cupel-adapt/src/per_test/mod.rs`) read only the executed lines per file of one test's report; replace them with P3.2's LCOV and coverprofile parsers (`cupel_adapt::coverage::parse_lcov` and its coverprofile sibling) at merge. `pytest_cov::tests_of` reads coverage.py's JSON contexts, which no `CoverageMap` format carries, and stays.
- **P3.3 — the `Runner` surface** (ADR-077 §3, deferred by P3.1). `cupel_core::Runner: Producer<TestResults>` with `list(ctx)`, `run(ctx, selection, env, timeout)`, `commands(ctx, selection)`; `TestSelection::{All, Tests(ids), Files(paths)}`; `RunnerCommand { dir, command }`. They live in `producer.rs` (a `runner.rs` beside it made a `producer ↔ runner` import cycle). `ProducerRegistry::register_runner` / `runner(id)`; every P3.1 runner registers through it. Narrowing per `RunnerSpec::select`: nextest and go test by ID (`-E` filterset, `-run` pattern), pytest by ID and file, jest and vitest by file, cargo test and unittest not at all (their `commands` is `None` and `tests --impacted` shows the whole-suite command with `narrowed: false`). `list` for nextest, go test, and pytest (parsers in `report/listing.rs`, tested on captures). A per-call timeout is `Cancellation::child`: it ends only that run, which reports an incomplete invocation "timed out after N ms"; the run's own `--timeout` still cancels. Display commands are the toolchain table's runner command for the root (`toolchain::runner_command`, launcher filled) plus the narrowing arguments.
- **P3.3 — `tests --impacted`** (`crates/cupel-cli/src/tests_verb.rs`, `tests_verb/impact.rs`). Resolves the scope as `check` does (`check::resolve` now returns the walk too), then produces facts through `Scheduler::production(job)`, a public face of the scheduler's fact store (ADR-077 §4) over the whole walk, so the import graph sees every importer. Lines in scope: `--symbols` spans; with a range, `Git::changed_lines` (`git diff -U0`) widened to the symbols each hunk touches (added files whole); else whole files. Per language: when `SupportMatrix::fact(PerTestMap)` is supported and the map is produced, the tests covering those lines plus tests whose `TestFrames` case the lines touch; otherwise (unsupported, tool missing, or the producer failed) the test files (`TestConventions`) in scope or importing a scoped file through `ModuleGraph`, with the reason. The envelope gains `tests: {per_test_map, impacted[{id, file, reason, lang}], commands[{lang, runner, dir, command, narrowed}], languages[{lang, per_test_map, producer, fallback}]}`, null for every other verb (ADR-013), so every golden gained `"tests": null`; **after merging P3.2 and P3.5, run `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 make test` once** so their new goldens gain the line too. `tests` without `--impacted` is a usage error. A dry run plans nothing (it lists no producer runs; producer planning is not modelled outside checks yet). `describe matrix` gains `verbs` (`tests --impacted` needs `per-test-map`, per language); `describe/matrix.json` leaves it out because Rust/Go/Python cells depend on installed tools, and the CLI tests pin JavaScript and TypeScript as `unsupported(per-test-map)`. `doctor` resolves `PerTestMap` for each language as an optional requirement (missing is a `warn`, not versioned in `run.toolchain` unless a check requires the same tool).
- **P3.3 — fixtures and proof.** `repos/per-test/{rust,go,python}`: `add` and `scale`, each covered by exactly one test; `repos/per-test/javascript`: `add.js`/`scale.js`, each imported by one test file. `cupel-cli/tests/tests_verb.rs` commits a fixture, changes `add`, and runs `tests --impacted --changed`: Rust, Go, and Python impact exactly the one covering test with a narrowed command (Rust skipped with the reason when `cargo-llvm-cov` is missing); JavaScript impacts exactly `add.test.js` ("imports add.js"), `per_test_map: false`, and the fallback reason names `per-test-map`; no changes impact nothing; `--paths` takes whole files. `cupel-adapt/tests/per_test.rs` runs each producer for real and checks the two maps are disjoint.
- **P3.3 — captures** (`captures/per-test/<tool>/<case>/`). Real: `cargo-nextest/list`, `go-test/list`, `pytest/list`, `cargo-llvm-cov/{adds,scales}` (LCOV), `go-cover/{TestAdd,TestScale}` (coverprofile), `pytest-cov/contexts` (coverage.py JSON). Tools: cargo-llvm-cov 0.9.1 (prebuilt, in the session scratchpad) with the 1.92.0 `llvm-tools` component unpacked there and found through `LLVM_COV`/`LLVM_PROFDATA`, cargo-nextest 0.9.148 (scratchpad), go1.23.2, pytest 9.1.1 with pytest-cov 7.1.0 (scratchpad venv) and the machine's pytest 8.4.2 (which has pytest-cov). None is missing. In `make check` here `cargo-nextest` and `cargo-llvm-cov` are not on `PATH`, so the Rust per-test and nextest selection tests print `SKIPPED`; they pass with the scratchpad tools on `PATH`. CI installs `llvm-tools-preview`, `cargo-llvm-cov`, and `pytest-cov` in a separate step.
- **P3.3 — open items.** (1) `PerTestMap` is not cached in the `facts` table, so every `tests --impacted` remeasures (one test run per Rust/Go test). (2) Go profiles never include `_test.go`, so a change to a Go test's own body impacts it only through `TestFrames` name matching. (3) The fallback for a language with a producer whose tool is missing selects by imports, which for Rust inline tests finds nothing (the test file is the source file). (4) `--symbols` that matches nothing is not a usage error for `tests` as it is for `check`. (5) A failing test still yields its coverage; `tests --impacted` does not run the baseline.
- **P3.3 — clones and gates.** First cut added 7 pairs and 1 cluster (`select` and `files` per runner, `placed_id`/`placed_file`, three per-test producer tests, a test helper), a `producer ↔ runner` cycle, and long or complex functions; all removed. After: 167 pairs, 19 clusters (unchanged), `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exits 0. The changed gate on this branch reports the snapshots rewritten from the pending `tests` verb to `oracle` (`describe`, `format`, `render`, `run_infrastructure`) as weakenings.
- **P3.3 — tests.** 1113 in all (+36 over P3.1's 1077): `cupel-core` `per_test` 4, `runner` 3, `git` +1; `cupel-adapt` `selection` 10, `per_test` 4, slicer unit tests 3; `cupel-cli` `tests_verb` 8, `describe` +3, doctor unit tests reworked. `make check` green.
- **P3.5 — `TestReruns`** ([ADR-078](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_078.md)). Repeated runs are a new fact kind, not a field of `TestResults`: one `TestResults` per (kind, language) is the baseline, every production of it passes the exit-4 gate (a rerun set that found a flaky test is red by construction), and the engine needs the baseline as input. `cupel_core::TestReruns` v1 (`flake.rs`): `runner`, `seed`, `runs[{order: declared|reversed|shuffled, seed, parallelism (null = runner default), executed (test IDs in the order they ran, when the runner reports it), invocations, tests}]`; `TestRerun::order_key()` is `executed` when present, else the order asked for (with the seed for a shuffle). `FactKind::TestReruns` (`test-reruns`) sits after `test-results` in the catalog; design §8.3.2 has its row and §8.5 says the flake engine produces it. No conformance inventory (an execution fact, as `TestResults`).
- **P3.5 — the seed reaches producers.** `Job.seed` and `ProduceCtx.seed` (0 from `ProduceCtx::new`); `check.rs` passes the run's seed (`--seed`, else random). `cupel-core/tests/seed.rs`.
- **P3.5 — `engine.flake`** (`cupel-adapt/src/flake/`: `mod.rs` the producer, `options.rs` `RunOptions` and the schedule, `reruns.rs` the per-runner table, `order.rs` Go's run order). `needs = [TestResults]`, `Langs::Any` (support follows from the runners), `Ready`. With no upstream baseline (the matrix harness's declared-facts run) it produces an empty `TestReruns` and runs nothing. Schedule from the run seed (`splitmix64(seed + k)` per rerun): reruns 0 and 1 declared and serial, 2 reversed (pytest node IDs, unittest dotted names, reversed from rerun 0's executed order) or the runner's seeded shuffle, 3+ shuffled alternating default parallelism and serial; `[lang.<id>] flake_runs` (default 3, 0 for none, anything but a non-negative integer is `Error::InvalidValue`). Per runner: `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast --tests [--config target.'cfg(all())'.runner = ["env", "RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1"]] -- [-Z unstable-options --shuffle-seed N] --test-threads=N` (the target runner reaches the test binaries only, so nothing rebuilds; doc-tests are not rerun); `go test -json -count=1 -shuffle=off|N [-p=N -parallel=N] <baseline packages>` with the order from test2json `run` events; pytest with the runner's JUnit flags plus node IDs, and unittest, both with `PYTHONHASHSEED`; `jest --seed --randomize --maxWorkers` and `vitest run --sequence.seed --sequence.shuffle --maxWorkers` with the Jest JSON report. Reports are read by the P3.1 parsers; `executed` is kept for serial reruns of runners that report execution order (libtest, test2json, JUnit, unittest), not for jest or vitest (vitest's report is in declaration order). Every process goes through `Process` against the run's `Cancellation` (`--timeout` → exit 5). Each rerun's tests are narrowed to the scope's files (tests with no file are kept); the whole suite reruns, since impacted-test selection is P3.3. `tool.cargo-nextest` is not rerun (each test is its own process, so order cannot matter): the engine returns `Error::InvalidValue` naming it and the check ends in `error`.
- **P3.5 — what P3.3's `Runner::run(selection, env, timeout)` must absorb.** The runner modules were off limits here, so `reruns.rs` repeats part of each runner's command line (pytest's JUnit flags, the Jest-JSON report flags, `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`, `go test -json` and the baseline's packages, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE`, `CARGO_TERM_COLOR`). `RunOptions { order, seed, parallelism, explicit }` is the hand-off: `explicit` is a selection in order; `order`/`seed`/`parallelism` become argv and environment per runner (the table above); a run must also return the order its tests ran in. Once `Runner::run` takes these, `reruns.rs` shrinks to the schedule and the engine calls the registered runner.
- **P3.5 — `tests.flaky`** (`cupel-checks/src/tests_flaky.rs`): tier 2, cost `standard`, `needs = [TestReruns, TestFrames]`, no settings. Per test over reruns where it passed, failed, or errored: two reruns with one order key that disagree → `test.flaky` (confidence 0.95; evidence `test`, `runner`, `reruns`, `passed`, `failed`, `outcomes`, `same_order`); otherwise disagreement across order keys → `test.order-dependent` (evidence adds `orders` and `preceded_by`: tests before it in every failing rerun and after it in some passing one; confidence 0.9 with a polluter, 0.7 without). Severity `warn`, action `investigate_flake`, ID `for_subject(check, kind, test id)`, `repro` `cupel check --only tests.flaky --seed <seed>`. Location: the `TestFrames` case with the runner's name (exact, or a suffix after a separator), in the runner's file when it names one, else the one case anywhere with that name (Go), else line 1 of the file. Score `unstable` (count) over the tests rerun; metrics `flaky`, `order_dependent`; summed across languages.
- **P3.5 — fixtures** `repos/tests.flaky/<lang>/` (the matrix's per-check convention, ADR-074, rather than `repos/flaky/<lang>/`): a balance in a module global, a test that passes only while it is zero declared before one that deposits (order-dependent), and a test in its own file that fails on every other run by toggling a `.settled` marker in the working directory (flaky: state outside the process flips the outcome between identical reruns, deterministically, so goldens are stable; a test failing at random would be reported flaky only when two same-order reruns happened to disagree). The Python suite is `unittest.TestCase`s so pytest and unittest both run it. The TypeScript fixture's config is `vitest.config.ts`: a `.mjs` file made JavaScript a language of the fixture, and a skip over two languages loses its `lang`. All five languages classify both tests correctly against real tools: pytest, unittest, go1.23.2, cargo 1.92.0, jest 30.5.2, vitest 4.1.11 (npm-installed into the session scratchpad; not on `PATH` here, so their tests print `SKIPPED` in `make check` and pass with them on `PATH`).
- **P3.5 — captures** `captures/flake/{cargo-test/shuffled, go-test/shuffled, pytest/reversed, unittest/reversed, jest/randomized, vitest/shuffled}` (each with `capture.toml`), read by `cupel-adapt/tests/flake_reports.rs`.
- **P3.5 — matrix and goldens.** `golden/tests.flaky/<lang>.json` for all five, made with `cupel_fixtures::matrix::SEED` (3), which every matrix run now passes as `--seed` (redacted, so no other golden changed); seed 3's shuffles put the depositing test first for Go and Rust. `matrix::verify` keeps the golden of an `unavailable` pair (supported where the tool is installed; CI installs every runner) and accepts an `unavailable` skip whatever tool the fixture's detection named, printing `SKIPPED`. `describe <check>` goldens no longer pin the support row, which depends on installed tools; `describe_goldens.rs` asserts it from the registries (every check's `describe.json` lost `languages`). The README's generated tables state `unavailable` as `supported`.
- **P3.5 — the default depth now runs tests.** `standard` is the default depth and `tests.flaky` is `standard`, so `cupel check` and `cupel survey` without `--depth` run the baseline. Repositories with code but no runnable suite are refused with exit 4, because P3.1's runners run at the repository root when `ProjectInfo` has no root (`cargo test` without a `Cargo.toml`; `unittest` finding no tests exits 5 on Python 3.12+). The CLI tests that build such repositories (`gate.rs`, `check.rs`, `survey.rs`) and the survey goldens over the shared fixtures (`survey_goldens.rs`, which now checks only selected checks) run at `--depth quick`; `survey/<lang>.json` record depth `quick` and `tests.flaky/survey-<lang>.json` are empty. Open: whether a language with no project root should have an empty, green baseline (runner modules, P3.3).
- **P3.5 — dogfood.** `make dogfood` passes `--skip tests.flaky`: on Cupel the engine reruns the whole workspace suite three times, two of them one test at a time, which ran past 10 minutes before it was stopped, and `make check` runs that suite on every commit. Revisit when P3.3 narrows reruns to impacted tests. The baseline itself still runs whenever a selected check needs it (P3.6's mutation gate).
- **P3.5 — clones** (`make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree`). As first written the step added 15 similar pairs and a cluster (the check's `new`, `plan`, `finding_kinds`, and `metrics` joining the per-check boilerplate clusters, the engine tests' registry helpers, parser tests per runner, two fact builders). Removed by building kinds and metrics inside the manifest, a `Rerunning` context for the engine, table-driven parser tests, the shared `Registries` in the engine tests, and one rerun builder in the check tests. After: 167 pairs, 19 clusters, unchanged; `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exits 0.
- **P3.5 — tests.** 1111 in all (+34 over P3.1's 1077): `cupel-core` `test_reruns` 3, `seed` 2; `cupel-checks` `tests_flaky` 9 (a hand-built `TestReruns` with no engine); `cupel-adapt` `flake` 14 (schedule, no baseline, nextest refused, `flake_runs`, cancellation, real reruns of all six runners, seed reproducibility), `flake_reports` 3; `cupel-cli` `flaky` 3 (findings, text line, `explain`, exit 4). On this machine Go test binaries were slow to start under load (about 25 s each, little CPU), which made the Go engine tests take minutes; they took seconds earlier in the session.
- **P3.2 — `CoverageMap` and the parsers** ([ADR-079](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_079.md)). The payload is ADR-067's version 1, unchanged; `CoverageMap::new` merges records of one file (hits add up), sorts files, lines, and branches, and keeps a file's branches when any record measured them; `line_hits`, `branch_hits` (`None` where branches were not measured), `branches_measured`, `FileCoverage::from_hits`. Version rule: bump on a renamed/removed/retyped field, a change in what `hits` or a branch means, or a change in path normalization. Parsers in `cupel_adapt::coverage`, format-only (no process or file access), each `fn(&str) -> Result<CoverageMap, ReportError>` with paths exactly as the report writes them: `parse_lcov`, `parse_cobertura` (`<source>/<filename>`; `condition-coverage="50% (1/2)"` → two branches, the first hit), `parse_istanbul` (statements by start line, largest count per line; branches by `line` or `loc.start`), `parse_coverprofile` (import paths; lines take their blocks' largest count; never branches), `parse_jacoco` (`<package>/<sourcefile>`; 1/0 hits; `cb` hit then `mb` missed branches; DTD allowed). `Format::of`/`parse` recognize the format from the text; `placed(map, &[ran_in…], dir)` makes paths repository-relative and drops files outside the root. An LCOV record has branches exactly when it holds a `BRDA` line (llvm-cov writes `BRF:0` without measuring). P3.3 can call `parse_lcov` per test profile and `placed` on the result.
- **P3.2 — captures** (`crates/cupel-fixtures/captures/coverage/<format>/<tool>/`, each with `capture.toml`), all real, of the coverage fixture: `lcov/cargo-llvm-cov` (cargo-llvm-cov 0.9.1 prebuilt and rustc 1.92.0's `llvm-tools` tarball, both downloaded into the session scratchpad with `LLVM_COV`/`LLVM_PROFDATA` pointing at it; nothing installed), `lcov/pytest-cov` and `cobertura/pytest-cov` (one run; pytest 8.4.2, pytest-cov 7.0.0, coverage.py 7.11.0), `lcov/jest` and `istanbul/jest` (jest 30.5.2), `lcov/vitest` and `istanbul/vitest` (vitest 4.1.11 with `@vitest/coverage-v8` 4.1.11), `lcov/c8` (c8 12.0.0 over a `node:test` copy of the JS tests), `coverprofile/go-test` (go1.23.2), `jacoco/jacoco-cli` (JaCoCo 0.8.12 agent and CLI on Temurin 21.0.12, a Java port of the fixture; JDK and JaCoCo downloaded into the scratchpad). Node tools were npm-installed into the scratchpad. No format is missing. Two formats of one run agree line for line (Cobertura vs LCOV for pytest-cov; Istanbul JSON vs LCOV for jest and vitest), which `coverage_reports.rs` asserts.
- **P3.2 — producers** (`cupel-adapt/src/coverage/`, one module per tool, IDs from the toolchain table: `tool.cargo-llvm-cov`, `tool.go-coverprofile`, `tool.pytest-cov`, `tool.coverage-py`, `tool.vitest-coverage`, `tool.jest-coverage`, `tool.c8`; shared `tool.rs` (`CoverageSpec`, `CoverageTool`) and `fresh.rs`). Each needs `ProjectInfo` and runs, per project root, the configured `[lang.<id>] coverage` command through `sh -c` with `{out}` filled by a cache path (or reads the tool's default `coverage/lcov.info`), recognizing the report's format. Availability: `Ready` with a fresh cached report (even without the tool); else `Unconfigured { key: lang.<id>.coverage }` without a command — so repositories that never configured coverage, including the shared fixture repositories, run no coverage and their goldens do not depend on installed tools; else `MissingTool` without the program (`node_modules/.bin` counts for vitest, jest, c8). `detected` follows the runner the table pairs it with (and a `runner` pin). Go maps import paths through `go.mod`. A command that exits non-zero or leaves no readable report emits `coverage.run-failed` (check `coverage`, `fix_bug`, evidence `producer`, `dir`, `command`, `exit_code`, `output`) and fails; exit 4 is not wired to coverage (ADR-079 §5). `runner.rs` gained `pub(crate)` `installed` and `probe` (shared with runners), `toolchain::companions`.
- **P3.2 — freshness.** A run writes `.cupel/cache/coverage/<producer>/<hash of the root path>/{report, sources.json}`; `sources.json` holds the root, where the tool ran, the report's hash, and blake3 of every file under the root (hidden entries, `.gitignore`s inside the root, `skip_dirs`, the tool's output directory, and `[scope] exclude` left out; parent, global, and `info/exclude` ignore files deliberately not read, so the answer is the same on every machine). Fresh = same file set, each file either older than `sources.json` (mtime) or hashing as recorded, and the report unchanged; a test-only edit makes it stale. A report at the tool's default location newer than every other file under the root is ingested without a record. The cache entry is per root, so JavaScript and TypeScript sharing a root run the tool once.
- **P3.2 — `tests.coverage`** (ADR-079 §6): needs `Symbols`, `CoverageMap`; wants `NormTree`, `TestConventions`; tier 2, `standard`. `coverage.uncovered-symbol` (warn) and `coverage.uncovered-branch` (info, only where the symbol's file has branches); with no branches measured anywhere the result carries `notes: ["branches: unavailable (<lang>)"]`. A symbol's lines are those after its declaration line (a Python `def` line runs on import), or the declaration alone for a one-line function; `static.crap` (now version 3) measures coverage the same way (`metrics::symbol_lines`/`symbol_coverage`). Score `line_ratio` over instrumented lines (combined weighted by lines); metrics `line`, `branch`, `lines`, `branches`, `uncovered`; ranking `uncovered_lines`. `CheckResult.notes` is new: omitted when empty, the one property exempt from ADR-013's all-required schema (`x-cupel-optional`); the scheduler suffixes each language's notes with ` (<lang>)`; the text renderer prints them as `note: …`.
- **P3.2 — fixtures and goldens.** `repos/coverage/<lang>/` (a `grade` module: `total` covered, `classify` partly — one branch never taken — `refund` never called, CC 6) with a `cupel.toml` coverage command and the committed `.cupel/cache/coverage/` a real run left (Rust also its `Cargo.lock`); `repos/tests.coverage` and `repos/static.crap` are symlinks to it. Every machine ingests the same real report, so the goldens hold with or without tools; `coverage_runs.rs` fails, with the regeneration recipe, when a committed report goes stale. CRAP golden evidence: in all five languages `refund` is `crap.high` with `{crap: 42.0, cyclomatic: 6, coverage: 0.0}`, score `max` 42 over 3 symbols; `classify` and `total` score below `watch`. `tests.coverage` goldens: Go and Rust report only `refund` uncovered and note `branches: unavailable`; Python, JavaScript, and TypeScript also report `classify`'s untaken branch. The matrix harness verifies each pair with its support in the pair's own fixture (`Registries::fixture_pairs`; `pairs` stays repository-free for the `describe matrix` comparison) and serves wants only from a ready producer, as the scheduler does. Shared-fixture survey slices show `tests.coverage` skipped `unconfigured` and `static.crap` unchanged. `explain` and text-evidence tests of `crap.high` (removed by ADR-067) are restored on the coverage fixtures, with the same for `coverage.uncovered-symbol`.
- **P3.2 — doctor and CI.** `doctor` requires the tool of the coverage producer chosen for every configured language whose checks need `CoverageMap` (`run.toolchain` gets its version); an `Unconfigured` producer needs no tool. CI's `check` job installs `cargo-llvm-cov` (`taiki-e/install-action`) and `llvm-tools-preview`, `pytest-cov`, and `@vitest/coverage-v8` (global npm, beside vitest and jest), so `coverage_producers.rs` runs every tool there; here they pass with the scratchpad tools on `PATH` and print `SKIPPED: <tool> not installed` without them (cargo-llvm-cov, jest, vitest are missing on this machine; go and pytest-cov are present). The dogfood job installs no coverage tool, so Cupel's own `tests.coverage` is `unavailable` there and `static.crap` still reports nothing for Cupel.
- **P3.2 — clones and gates** (`make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree`). As first written, the step added 21 pairs and 2 clusters, a module cycle (`coverage/mod.rs` ↔ the parsers), three over-complex parsers, a 70-line finding builder, and weak-assertion tests; removed by a shared `cupel-adapt/tests/support` (runners now use it too), table-driven real-tool tests, `FileCoverage::from_hits` for test maps, `coverage/error.rs` and `coverage/format.rs` as leaves, per-line LCOV and per-class/per-file XML readers, and split finding builders. After: 167 pairs and 19 clusters (the ratchets, unchanged), `cyclomatic_max` 10, no cycles; exit 0.
- **P3.2 — merge into `main`** (2026-10-14, after P3.3 and P3.5). Each branch held the clone ratchet at 167 pairs on its own; their union on `main` holds 169 pairs and 19 clusters (two new type-3 pairs between P3.1's and P3.2's `toolchain.rs` tests and between `metrics::largest` and `Check::combine_metric`), so `[gate.tree] static.clones.pairs` is raised to 169, recorded here against follow-up (3). The merge also: kept `CheckResult.notes` omitted when empty (ADR-079 option (c); the design's example envelopes carry no `notes`); re-recorded the Go coverage fixture's committed report, which the ADR renumber (077 → 079 in its `cupel.toml` comment) had made stale; made `doctor` expect P3.3's optional per-test requirement beside P3.2's coverage requirement; and made `Process` drop a coverage harness's variables (`CARGO_LLVM_COV*`, `__CARGO_LLVM_COV_*`, `LLVM_PROFILE_FILE`, and cargo-llvm-cov's `RUSTC_WRAPPER`) from every child, because Cupel's own `tests.coverage` runs `cargo llvm-cov` over its suite, whose `coverage_producers.rs` runs `cargo llvm-cov` on the Rust fixture, and the inner build inherited the outer wrapper's crate list and failed; a user's own `RUSTC_WRAPPER` stays.
- **P3.2 — Go in CI.** The first CI run of the merge failed `coverage_producers.rs` for `tool.go-coverprofile` alone: Go 1.27 (`setup-go` `stable`) emits different coverprofile blocks for `grade.go` than Go 1.23 (28 instrumented lines, 13 run, locally; 25 and 11 under 1.27), so the exact shape the test asserts, and the committed Go report, depend on the Go release. CI pins Go 1.23, the release the fixture reports were made with. Follow-up: assert coverage facts that hold across releases (`refund` unrun, `total` run) rather than block counts, or commit a capture per Go release.
- **P3.2 — committed reports on another machine.** The second CI run failed Go's `tests.coverage` goldens only: a cached report resolved its import paths through the `go.mod` in the directory the tool ran in (`sources.ran_in`), which exists only on the machine that made the report, so on CI every Go file dropped out of the map and `grade.Refund` went unreported. `CoverageTool::read` now resolves such names in the project root it is reading for and keeps `ran_in` only for the report's own absolute paths; `coverage_producers.rs` covers a committed report whose `ran_in` is gone.
- **P3.2 — open items.** (1) A red coverage run is `error` and `coverage.run-failed`, not exit 4 (needs `TestResults`, which would double the suite run and make goldens depend on runners; P3.4 can add it). (2) `cargo llvm-cov` measures no branches on stable (`--branch` is nightly), so Rust notes `branches: unavailable`. (3) The command runs through `sh`. (4) Runners still ignore `[lang.<id>] test` (P3.1 deferred (5)); coverage uses its configured command. (5) The cache is not in the store's `coverage` table yet. (6) JaCoCo paths are package-relative; no Java producer exists until P6.
- **P3.4 — `MutationOutcomes`** ([ADR-080](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_080.md), [ADR-004](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_004.md)). `cupel_core::MutationOutcomes` v1 (`mutation.rs`): `engine`, `runner`, `depth`, `per_test`, and `mutants[{symbol, symbol_hash, site_hash, operator, from, to, span, outcome: killed|survived|timeout|uncovered|unviable, tests, killing_tests, duration_ms, reused, equivalent, cache_key}]`, one per (site, replacement), sorted by file, position, replacement; `MutantOutcome::killed()` is `Some` only for the two scored outcomes. In `FACT_SCHEMAS` (15 entries) and `describe fact mutation-outcomes`; `SiteHash::refined(detail)` gives each replacement of a site its own finding ID.
- **P3.4 — what producers gained** (ADR-080 §2–3). `Producer::wants` (default none), made by the scheduler after the needs when it can (`FactStore::ensure_wants`) and handed upstream, but only when a producer of it costs no more than the run's depth (so a per-test map, `deep`, is made only at `deep` and `exhaustive`). A check result that ends in `error` now carries the error's message in `notes` (suffixed with its language), so a failing engine says why. `ProduceCtx::runners` (`ProducerRegistry::runners`, the registered runners behind `Arc`s) and `ProduceCtx::focus` (`Focus { depth, symbols, changed }`: the run's depth, the `--symbols` selection per language, and a range's changed lines, `ChangedLines`). `Job` gained `depth` and `changed` (`check::changed_lines`, moved from `tests_verb.rs` and shared). `Outcome::mutation_outcomes` and the CLI's `run::Executions` carry every produced `MutationOutcomes` to `persist::record`, which indexes it with the run record.
- **P3.4 — `Runner::run(ctx, selection, &RunOptions) -> TestRun`** (the P3.5 hand-off). `RunOptions { env, timeout, order, seed, parallelism }`; each `RunnerSpec` has a `vary: &Varying` (ordering, arguments, environment, where the run order comes from: `Executed::Report` for libtest/JUnit/unittest, `Events` for test2json, `Unknown` for Jest JSON) and `outputs` (`target` for both cargo runners). A seeded run of `go test` is uncached (`-count=1`); unittest now narrows to dotted test IDs (no `discover`), so `tests --impacted` gives it a narrowed command. `Runner::ordering()` (`Fixed`/`Seeded`/`Explicit`) and `Runner::outputs()`. The flake engine calls the registered runner through `ctx.runners` and wants `ProjectInfo`; `flake/reruns.rs` (the private per-runner table) and `flake/order.rs` are gone (`report::test2json::order` replaces the latter, still exported as `flake::go_test_order`); its `Planned` (was its own `RunOptions`) keeps the schedule. Every existing runner and flake test passes unchanged except that the engine tests hand the context the registry's runners.
- **P3.4 — the engine** (`crates/cupel-mutate`, a new workspace crate depending on `cupel-core` and `cupel-store`; the crate-graph line of §2.1 now says so through ADR-080). `engine.mutation`: `needs = [MutationSites, Symbols, CoverageMap, TestResults]` (`Symbols` added to the design's list for spans), `wants = [PerTestMap, ModuleGraph, NormTree, ProjectInfo, TestConventions, TestFrames]`; registered by `cupel_mutate::register`, which `Catalog::builtin` and the matrix `Registries::builtin` call. Modules: `mutant` (sub-step 1: a site's text replaced at its byte span, only where the span still holds it), `overlay` (2), `plan` (3, 4, 9, and the cache key), `execute` (2, 5), `history` (6, 7), `equivalent` (ADR-004), `settings` (`[lang.<id>] mutation = { operators, jobs, timeout_factor }`; anything else is `Error::InvalidValue`).
  - **Overlays.** One slot per worker under `$TMPDIR/cupel-mutate/<repo hash>/<lang>/<n>/`, outside the repository so no runner discovers it (vitest's default `include` would find tests under `.cupel/`). Synced at each production: clones on macOS (`std::fs::copy` is `clonefile`), hard links elsewhere; files with the slot's bytes kept as they are (so a restored file keeps its newer time); the tree's own symlinks as the same links; gitignored entries symlinked; any `.git`, the repository's own `.cupel` (not one deeper in the tree, such as a fixture's committed cache), and the runner's `outputs` neither mirrored nor removed (cargo names path-package artifacts by their path within the workspace, so slots sharing `target/` ran each other's builds: every Rust fixture mutant came out killed until each slot got its own). A mutant is written as a private copy (the link removed first); relative importers (`ModuleGraph`) are rewritten as private copies in each slot; the original is written back after each mutant. `overlay::claim` records the repository as its slots' owner and removes slots whose repository is gone (test repositories had left 385 MB behind).
  - **Timeouts.** Each slot's first act on a file is a reference run of that file's tests against the original plus one trailing newline (a new file to build caches keyed by time or content: cargo rebuilds, Go and Jest recompile), which must be green; mutants get `max(2 s, timeout_factor × reference)`. Before this, Go's `1 → 0` mutant timed out under load because the reference came from Go's build cache.
  - **Reuse.** Key = BLAKE3 of the symbol's source text (the structural hash ignores literals), site hash, replacement, suite hash (the covering tests' IDs and files with a per-test map, else the baseline's), and toolchain hash (engine version, runner ID, the runner tool's version line). Store migration 3 recreates `mutants` (`run_id, lang, engine, cache_key, symbol_hash, site_hash, replacement, outcome, duration_ms, mutant` JSON) with indexes by key and by site; `Store::record_mutants`, `mutants_by_key`, `last_mutant_outcomes`. The engine reads it read-only (`open_existing`).
  - **Ordering** (`History::order`): last run's survivors, then symbols with no stored outcome, then the rest; files take their first mutant's place, mutants of one file run concurrently across slots, files sequentially.
  - **Per-test selection** at `deep` and `exhaustive`, and only when the `PerTestMap`'s runner is the baseline's (Go always; Python with pytest-cov; Rust only with nextest as the runner, since the map's IDs are nextest's).
  - Every mutation run carries the run's seed.
