- **P3.4 — `tests.mutation`** (`cupel-checks/src/tests_mutation.rs`): tier 3, `standard`, `needs = [Symbols, MutationOutcomes]`, `wants = [TestConventions]`, no settings. `mutant.survived` (fail, 0.95, `write_test`, at the site, evidence `mutation{from,to,operator}`, `covering_tests`, `tests_run`; hints `covering_tests`, `nearest_test_file`), `mutant.timeout` and `mutant.uncovered` (info), `mutant.equivalent?` (info, 0.3, `review`, evidence adds `reason`); killed and unviable mutants report nothing. Score `killed_ratio` = killed / (killed + unmarked survived), 1 when nothing is scored; metrics `score`, `killed`, `survived`, `timeout`, `uncovered`, `unviable`, `equivalent`, `executed`, `reused`; ranked `survivors` per symbol. The `Mutation` evidence struct is `#[schemars(inline)]` (the evidence validator has no `$defs`, ADR-043). Exit 4 needs no code: its needs reach `TestResults` through the engine.
- **P3.4 — fixtures and goldens.** `repos/tests.mutation/<lang>/`: `discount(total, member)` (`if total >= 100 && member { return total - 10 } return total * 1`) and `refund(total) = total - 5`, two tests in two files (a large member order, a small order), each file's coverage report committed under `.cupel/cache/coverage/` (Rust also `Cargo.lock` and a `.gitignore` for `target/`); `coverage_runs.rs`'s freshness test covers them. Every language has the same exact outcome set: survived `>=` → `>` on the `if` line (the suite never tries 100); `*` → `/` survived and marked possibly equivalent; `&&`/`and` → `||`/`or`, `-` → `+`, and `1` → `0` killed; refund's `-` → `+` uncovered. Score 0.75 (3 of 4), findings `mutant.survived`, `mutant.equivalent?`, `mutant.uncovered`. Every test runs the `if` line, so the survivor's `covering_tests` are both tests whether or not a per-test map selected them, and the goldens do not depend on pytest-cov, go, or cargo-llvm-cov being installed beyond the runner. Goldens: `golden/tests.mutation/{go,javascript,python,rust,typescript,describe,survey-<lang>}.json` (the survey slices are empty: shared fixtures have no coverage command, so `unconfigured`). README tables: `tests.mutation` is `unconfigured` in the shared fixtures, as `tests.coverage` is.
- **P3.4 — proofs.** Engine on the Python fixture through the scheduler's fact store (`cupel-mutate/tests/engine.rs`, `stack.rs`): exact outcomes; uncovered never run; no baseline runs nothing; with a per-test map each mutant runs only its covering tests; an infinite-loop mutant times out (`timeout_factor = 3`); a second production with the store written runs no mutant; changing `tests/test_small.py` reruns exactly the four mutants of lines 6 and 8 and reuses line 7's; `standard` with a range on refund mutates only refund, `deep` everything. Through the binary (`cupel-cli/tests/mutation.rs`): the second run reports `executed 0, reused 5` and identical findings; editing uncovered `refund` runs nothing, a comment in `discount` reruns its five; a failing test refuses with exit 4, `baseline.test-failed`, no check, and no `mutants` row; the text line. `tests.mutation` on a hand-built fact with no engine (`cupel-checks/tests/tests_mutation.rs`, 10 tests).
- **P3.4 — integration from P3.3 and P3.5** (done in a parallel worktree, cherry-picked): the per-test slicers `executed_lcov_lines`/`executed_coverprofile_lines` are gone; `tool.llvm-cov-per-test` reads each test's report with `coverage::parse_lcov` and `placed`, `tool.go-cover-per-test` with `coverage::parse_coverprofile` (import paths still through `go.mod`), both through `per_test::executed_lines(&CoverageMap)`; unit tests on the same captures. A repository with no test suite no longer exits 4: when `ProjectInfo` names no root of a runner's language the runner runs nothing (`runner::suite_roots`); unittest's exit 5 ("no tests ran") is a finished run; and when a green baseline ran no test (`TestResults::ran_none`) the scheduler skips every check whose needs reach `TestResults` as `unavailable` (`fact: test-results`, no tool), not partial. `cupel-cli/tests/flaky.rs` proves it for Rust and Python code with no suite.
- **P3.4 — dogfood and clones** (`make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree`). The dogfood target skips `tests.mutation` beside `tests.flaky` (every mutant reruns Cupel's suite; the mutation gate is P3.6). As first written the step added 7 pairs and a cluster (176/20), a `lib ↔ mutants` module cycle in `cupel-store`, three long or complex functions, and eight `tests.quality` findings; removed by `Planner`/`Selection` in planning, one `note` builder in the check, `FactStore::ensure_wants`, the mutants index in the store's root module with shared `insert_rows`, `Limits::plan` for the three test checks' `plan`, `runner::as_arguments`, `Registries::shared`/`lang` in `cupel-fixtures`, `Repo::python_fixture` in the CLI tests, and helpers for repeated test setup. After: 169 pairs and 19 clusters (the ratchets, unchanged), `cyclomatic_max` 10, no cycles, `tests.quality` clean; exit 0.
- **P3.4 — timing** (DoD: `check --changed --depth standard` on a 10-symbol change in ≤ 3 min). Measured on this machine (8 cores, Apple silicon, every tool on `PATH`) in a clone of this branch with one commit adding a comment line inside `cupel-checks`' `metrics::weighted_mean`: `cupel check --base HEAD~1 --only tests.mutation --depth standard` took **2386 s (about 40 min)** for **one** function and its **5 mutants** (`+=`→`-=` twice, `*`→`/`, `==`→`!=`, `/`→`*`; all killed, score 1.0). Of that, ~290 s was the baseline (`cargo test --workspace`, ~180 s) and the coverage report (`cargo llvm-cov` over the suite, stale after the edit); the rest is the engine: 4 slots, each first building the workspace into its own `target/` (about 6 GB each) and running a reference, then each mutant an incremental rebuild of `cupel-checks` and its dependents plus the whole workspace suite (330–670 s per mutant with four running at once). A repeat run with nothing changed took **181 s**, of which the check took 1.9 s (`executed 0, reused 5`): the rest is the baseline. So the DoD's ≤ 3 min for a 10-symbol change is missed by more than an order of magnitude, and the 3 min is already spent by Cupel's own baseline. Why, and what closes it: every mutant reruns the whole suite because `cargo test` cannot narrow (per-test selection needs nextest as the runner, and the per-test map is `deep` cost and not cached); every mutant rebuilds (schemata, P4.1, compile once); and Cupel's suite itself is several minutes because many tests run real tools. The first attempts also found three engine bugs, now fixed and tested: a symlinked directory in the tree (the fixture repositories' `static.crap` → `coverage`) could not be mirrored; a `.cupel/` inside the tree (the fixtures' committed coverage reports) was left out, so the suite failed in the overlay; and wanting `PerTestMap` at `standard` made the engine measure one llvm-cov run per Cupel test (over 20 min before the first mutant), hence the depth rule for wants.
- **P3.4 — open items.** (1) Without schemata (P4.1) a Rust mutant rebuilds its crate and every dependent and reruns the whole `cargo test --workspace` suite; with `cargo test` as the runner there is no per-test selection for Rust. (2) `PerTestMap` is still not cached, so wanting it remeasures every test per run where its tool is installed (one per-test run per test for Go and Rust-with-nextest). (3) The reuse key is the mutated function's text: a changed callee or constant elsewhere reuses outcomes (design §8.8 accepts this). (4) `standard` without a range mutates every function in scope; repeated runs are cheap only through reuse. (5) Slots live in the system temporary directory; a slot's first production is a full build (for Rust, its own `target/`, so up to `jobs` copies). (6) Engine settings live in `[lang.<id>] mutation`, not `[checks."tests.mutation"]` as design §6.7's example has `timeout_factor`, because producers never see check settings. (7) No progress events per mutant yet (`check.start` has the check's symbol targets). (8) The `equivalent?` rule is lexical and covers only identity operands (ADR-004).
- **P3.4 — for the integrator.** Review first: `crates/cupel-core/src/producer.rs` (the `Runner` trait, `RunOptions`, `Focus`, `Runners`, `Producer::wants`), `crates/cupel-core/src/schedule.rs` (wants, focus, `mutation_outcomes`, and the cherry-picked no-test skip), `crates/cupel-adapt/src/runner.rs` and every runner spec (`vary`, `outputs`), `crates/cupel-adapt/src/flake/`, `crates/cupel-cli/src/{check,run,app,persist,tests_verb}.rs`, `crates/cupel-store/src/{lib,migrations}.rs`, the root `Cargo.toml` (new member), `Makefile` (`--skip tests.mutation`), and `crates/cupel-checks/src/{settings,outcome,finding,tests_coverage,tests_flaky}.rs` (shared helpers). P3.6 turns the mutation gate on: drop `--skip tests.mutation` from `make dogfood` for the changed scope and add `"tests.mutation.score" = ">= 0.80"` to `[gate.changed]`; expect each Cupel mutant to cost a rebuild plus the workspace suite (see timing).
- **P3.4 — tests.** 1255 in all, `make check` green with every tool on `PATH` (cargo-nextest, cargo-llvm-cov, go, pytest with pytest-cov, jest, vitest). New: `cupel-core` `producer_inputs` 4, `mutation_outcomes` 3, `scheduler` +1 (an error's note); `cupel-store` +2; `cupel-mutate` 28 (`mutants` 4, `overlay` 8, `engine` 4, `stack` 5, `settings` 3, unit 4); `cupel-checks` `tests_mutation` 10; `cupel-cli` `mutation` 4; `cupel-adapt` `selection` +2 (an explicit order run and reported, a seed handed over); the matrix's five `tests.mutation` pairs; and the cherry-picked integration's tests (`baseline.rs`, `runners.rs`, `flaky.rs`, per-test slicer units). `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exits 0.

---

- **P3.6 — measurement** (2026-10-15, release binary at ad87ead, a fresh clone, every tool on `PATH`, 4 workers). Two runs of `cupel check --base HEAD~1 --only tests.mutation --depth standard`, each on a one-commit change adding a comment inside two functions of `crates/cupel-checks/src/metrics.rs`. (1) `decisions_by_symbol` and `hashes_by_symbol`: no mutation sites, 0 mutants, 718 s wall (the check itself 382 s: the baseline suite, the stale coverage report re-measured, the per-worker builds). (2) `symbol_lines` and `symbol_coverage`: 4 mutants, 4 killed, 0 survived, score 1.0, 2242 s wall (37 min; the check 1973 s). With the agent's P3.4 measurement (`weighted_mean`, 5 mutants, 2386 s) the cost on Cupel is about 8 min per mutant on top of a 6–12 min floor, so a 10-symbol change (tens of mutants) runs for hours. **Initial score: 1.0 on the 9 Cupel mutants measured so far; nothing to fix yet.** The gate `[gate.changed] "tests.mutation.score" = ">= 0.80"` is not added and `make dogfood` keeps `--skip tests.mutation`: a rule the dogfood never evaluates would be a false claim, and running it would make every branch's CI run for hours. Unblocks when a Cupel mutant costs seconds, not minutes: P4.1 schemata (one build for every mutant of a file) and per-test selection at `standard` depth with a cached `PerTestMap`, so a mutant runs only its covering tests through nextest's filter.
- **P3.6 — the changed gate under `--only`.** The measurement runs above passed `--only tests.mutation` on a range without `--no-gate`, and the envelope came back `fail`, exit 1, with no findings: the changed gate was evaluated and every rule was `skipped` because its check did not run. First recorded here as a defect; it is [ADR-060](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_060.md) §4 and §6 as decided (a rule without a value fails the gate as `skipped`, so `--only` cannot satisfy a gate by leaving checks out, and iterating with `--only` says `--no-gate`), and the envelope says so (`summary.gate.evaluated = gate.changed`, every rule `skipped`). Not a defect: the runs should have passed `--no-gate`. Withdrawn from the follow-up list.
- **P3.6 — measurement with schemata and the cached map** (2026-10-15, release binary at 85dcab7 — `main` with P3.7, ADR-083, and P4.1 — a fresh clone, every tool on `PATH`, 4 workers, a quiet machine; `cupel check --base HEAD~1 --only tests.mutation --depth standard --no-gate`, each run on a one-commit change adding a comment inside one function of `crates/cupel-checks/src/metrics.rs`). (1) *Cold* (`symbol_coverage`, no caches): 2294 s wall, the check 1955 s, 3 mutants, all `schemata`, all killed, score 1.0; `--dry-run` showed `per-test-map(deep)`, so each mutant ran the whole suite (1316 tests, ~577 s each in parallel slots). A fresh clone has no per-test map and `check` does not build one at `standard` (ADR-083 §4), so a cold `check` never narrows. (2) *The map built once:* `tests --impacted --base HEAD~1` measured 1316 tests in one instrumented nextest run, 304 s. (3) *Cached map* (`cyclomatic`): `--dry-run` showed `per-test-map(refresh 129 stale)` (staleness is by file, ADR-083 open item (1)); 1098 s wall, the check 825 s, 2 mutants, all `schemata`, killed, score 1.0, **16 tests and ~130 s each** (the 16 include matrix tests that run every check). Against the first P3.6 measurement (2242 s for 4 mutants, ~560 s each) the per-mutant term fell from minutes to about two, and the per-mutant build to ~2 s (P4.1 timing). What remains is the floor: the baseline suite (nextest), the coverage report (`cargo llvm-cov`, stale after any edit), and the per-test refresh (129 tests under coverage) are **three runs of the same suite, each with its own build**, about 4–5 min apiece here, before the first mutant. **Initial score 1.0 on the 14 Cupel mutants measured so far.** The gate is still not added: `make dogfood` skips `tests.mutation`, a `[gate.changed]` rule whose check does not run is `skipped` and fails the gate (ADR-060 §6), and running it in CI means those three runs plus the mutants on every push on a two-core runner with no cache. Unblocks when one instrumented run yields `TestResults`, `CoverageMap`, and `PerTestMap` together and staleness is by function (P4.4), which puts the floor near one suite run; then the gate and the dogfood's `--skip tests.mutation` are revisited, with nextest and cargo-llvm-cov installed in the CI dogfood job.
- **P3.6 — measurement with one suite run** (2026-10-16, P4.4; release binary at d8ae1e6 on branch `p4-4-one-run`, a fresh clone, every tool on `PATH`, 4 workers, `cupel check --base HEAD~1 --only tests.mutation --depth standard --no-gate`, each run on a one-commit change adding a comment inside one function of `crates/cupel-checks/src/metrics.rs`). **The machine was loaded**: another agent's test suites ran throughout (load average 7 at the start, 32–56 during both runs), so wall times are contended. (1) *Cold* (`symbol_coverage`, no cache, no mutation slots): `--dry-run` planned `test-results+coverage-map+per-test-map(one run)`; **956 s wall**, the check 570 s; **one suite run** (the instrumented `cargo llvm-cov nextest` of all 1342 tests, about 385 s with the instrumented build from nothing; neither `.cupel/cache/runners/` nor `.cupel/cache/coverage/` was written); 3 mutants (`>`→`>=`, `0`→`1`, `/`→`*`), all `schemata`, all killed, score 1.0, **16 tests per mutant**, 192–196 s each in parallel slots. The per-test map came from the same run, so even the cold run narrowed each mutant (P3.6's cold run ran the whole suite per mutant, ~577 s each). (2) *Warm* (`cyclomatic`, the cache and slots from (1)): `--dry-run` planned `test-results+coverage-map+per-test-map(one run)` where P3.6's second measurement planned the baseline, a stale coverage report, and `per-test-map(refresh 129 stale)`; **734 s wall (12.2 min)**, the check 350 s; **one suite run**, about 384 s (incremental instrumented build of `cupel-checks` and its dependents, the whole suite, export of 1342 test processes, `cargo llvm-cov report`); 2 mutants (`1`→`0`, `+`→`-`), `schemata`, killed, score 1.0, **16 tests and 125–133 s per mutant**; the rest of the check, about 217 s, is reconciling the four slots with the tree, their incremental schemata builds, validation, and the reference runs. (3) *Unchanged rerun*: `--dry-run` planned `test-results+coverage-map+per-test-map(cached)`; **1 s wall**, no test run, both outcomes reused. Against P3.6's 1098 s (warm, three suite runs) the warm floor fell from three suite runs to one; **the P4 target of ≤ 10 min is missed by about 2 minutes on this loaded machine**: the remaining time is the one suite run (~6.4 min, of which the matrix tests that run every check on every fixture are the longest) and the slots' per-change work (~3.6 min). **Score 1.0 on the 19 Cupel mutants measured so far.** The gate is not added here (the integrator does, from these numbers).
- **P3.6 — measurement on a quiet machine** (2026-10-16, release binary at a993d7d — `main` with P4.1–P4.4 — a fresh clone, every tool on `PATH`, 4 workers, `CUPEL_NO_DAEMON=1`, load average 3 at the start; `cupel check --base HEAD~1 --only tests.mutation --depth standard --no-gate`, each run on a one-commit change adding a comment inside one function of `crates/cupel-checks/src/metrics.rs`; the breakdown is from the store's `durations` table, ADR-086 §2). (1) *Cold* (`symbol_coverage`, no cache, no slots): `--dry-run` planned `test-results+coverage-map+per-test-map(one run)`; **907 s wall**; the joint suite run 368 s; the engine 538 s for 3 mutants (`schemata`, all killed, score 1.0, the longest 171 s). (2) *Warm* (`cyclomatic`, the cache and slots from (1)): **630 s wall (10.5 min)**; the suite run 317 s; the engine 311 s for 2 mutants (`schemata`, killed, score 1.0; 141 s each in parallel slots, the remaining ~170 s the slots' sync with the tree, their schemata builds, and validation). (3) *Unchanged rerun*: `(cached)`, **2 s wall**, the suite entry read in 69 ms, both outcomes reused. **The ≤ 10 min line is missed by 30 s.** The two halves are equal: one run of 1377 tests under instrumentation, and the mutants, whose 16 covering tests take over two minutes because Cupel's matrix tests run every check over every fixture. **Score 1.0 on the 24 Cupel mutants measured so far.** The gate is still not added: a `[gate.changed]` rule needs its check to run (ADR-060 §6), so `make dogfood` would have to run `tests.mutation`, and the CI dogfood job runs that on every push to a branch, on two cores, with no suite cache (the cold path, 907 s here), with mutants in proportion to the change, which on an agent's branch is tens of functions. Unblocks when a Cupel mutant costs seconds (Cupel's own matrix tests split so a mutant's covering tests are small) and the CI job keeps the instrumented build and the suite cache between runs; both are work on the Cupel repository, not the product, carried as follow-up (13).
- **P3 — Definition of Done** (2026-10-15, against `main` at ad87ead; CI green on both runners and the dogfood). (1) *Goldens and real-tool tests:* met. 1255 tests; every (check, language) pair of the ten checks has its golden or its recorded skip; CI installs every runner and coverage tool (Go pinned to 1.23, see "P3.2 — Go in CI"). (2) *≤ 3 min for a 10-symbol change at `standard`:* **not met.** Measured 718 s with no mutants and 2242 s with 4 (see "P3.6 — measurement"); without `tests.mutation` the changed check on Cupel at `standard` is bounded by the baseline suite (~3 min) plus the coverage run (~5 min) when the report is stale, so even `tests.coverage` alone misses the line on this suite. The line is carried to P4 with P4.1. (3) *Exit 4 for every check that depends on test execution:* met for `tests.flaky` and `tests.mutation`, which end in `baseline.test-failed` / `baseline.run-incomplete` with an empty `checks[]`, proven through the binary, and skip as `unavailable` where there is no suite (cherry-picked in P3.4). Not for `tests.coverage`: a red coverage run is `error` with `coverage.run-failed`, not exit 4 (P3.2 open item (1); wiring it through `TestResults` would run the suite twice). Carried to the follow-up list. (4) *README:* met. Coverage, mutation testing and its performance stack, `tests --impacted`, flakiness, and exit 4 are documented from the real binary; the generated check table, matrix, and exit-code table are regenerated by `make readme` and checked by `make test`.
- **P3 — follow-up list** (carried from P2's, extended). (1) `References` fact ([ADR-058](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_058.md)). (2) Clone type 1 vs 2 ([ADR-050](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_050.md)). (3) `static.clones` on the Cupel tree to zero; the ratchet rose to 169 pairs in the P3.2 merge and must come back down before it is replaced by `none`. (4) `assertion.removed` weighs strength, not count. (5) `static.crap.max` in both gates now that coverage exists (CRAP max on Cupel is 20). (6) *Withdrawn:* the changed gate under `--only` is ADR-060 §4 as decided (see "P3.6 — the changed gate under `--only`"). (7) *Done where a joint run applies:* `PerTestMap` is cached per producer and root and refreshed incrementally, and a cached map is used at `standard` ([ADR-083](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_083.md), "P3 — per-test map cache"); `TestResults` is cached with the coverage and the per-test map under one freshness record by the joint suite runs (Rust with nextest, Python with pytest, Go; [ADR-084](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_084.md), "P4.4 — cache and the baseline"). Plain runners (`cargo test`, `unittest`, `jest`, `vitest`, and any runner whose coverage command is customized) still run their baseline every time. (8) The pytest-cov per-test producer probes `pytest`, not the plugin. (9) `--since-baseline`, `--oracle`, `--cases` are accepted and ignored; *`--budget` done* in P4.3 ([ADR-086](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_086.md)). (10) The real-tool Go coverage test asserts block counts that depend on the Go release. (11) `tests.coverage` on a red suite is `error`, not exit 4 (P3.2 open item (1)). (12) Mutation outcome reuse is keyed on the mutated function only, so a changed callee reuses stale outcomes (accepted by the design, worth a note in `explain`). (13) The P3.6 gate: a Cupel mutant's covering tests are the matrix tests (16 tests, over two minutes); split them per check so a mutant runs seconds of tests, and cache the instrumented build and the suite run in the CI dogfood job; then run `tests.mutation` in `make dogfood` and add `[gate.changed] "tests.mutation.score" = ">= 0.80"` (P3 Notes "P3.6 — measurement on a quiet machine").
- **P3.7 — install commands** ([ADR-081](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_081.md)). They live in the toolchain table beside the rows they belong to, where detection and default commands already are, not in the producers: a runner or coverage row's `install` is a template filled with the launcher detected at the repository root, whose new `add` value is `npm install --save-dev`, `pnpm add --save-dev`, `yarn add --dev`, `python -m pip install`, `uv add --dev`, or `poetry add --group dev`. Rows: `cargo install cargo-nextest --locked`; `cargo install cargo-llvm-cov --locked && rustup component add llvm-tools-preview`; `<add> pytest`, `<add> pytest-cov`, `<add> coverage`; `<add> vitest`, `<add> vitest @vitest/coverage-v8`, `<add> jest` (runner and coverage), `<add> c8`. A new `[toolchains]` table gives hints for `cargo`, `go`, and `python3` ("go is the Go toolchain, which Cupel does not install: …"). `cupel_core::Install::{Command, Hint}` replaces `MissingTool.install`'s `String` (only a `Command` is ever run or planned); `cupel_adapt::toolchain::install(producer, program, root)` is what `runner.rs` and `coverage/tool.rs` fill it from. `describe language` shows each producer's `install` template (`ToolDescription.install`; the five `describe/language-*.json` goldens and the run schema snapshot changed). Cupel chooses no prefix: the command runs with Cupel's environment, so `CARGO_HOME`/`CARGO_INSTALL_ROOT`, the `python` first on `PATH` (an active venv), and the root's `node_modules` decide where a tool lands.
- **P3.7 — who may install and when.** Design §6.3 makes `--install | --no-install` global, and §6.8 gives `check --dry-run` a `would_install`, so `check` (and `survey`, which shares its pipeline) install too, not only `doctor`. `install::Permission::of(install, no_install, stdout_is_terminal)`: `--no-install` never, `--install` without asking, neither asks when stdout is a terminal (a `[y/N]` question on stderr, read from stdin; a stdin that is not a terminal declines) and installs nothing otherwise. `doctor` installs only with `--fix`. `check`/`survey` resolve, before scheduling, the producers the scheduler would choose for their selected checks in the scope's languages (doctor's `Resolution`, required tools only) and install the missing ones with a command; with permission "never" they do nothing and report nothing (their `unavailable` skips already name the tool). Other verbs accept the flags and install nothing. `make dogfood` passes `--no-install`.
- **P3.7 — `doctor --fix`** (`crates/cupel-cli/src/install.rs`, `doctor.rs`). Installs run one at a time as `sh -c <command>` in the repository root under the run's cancellation, stdout and stderr captured separately; then every requirement is probed again, so `doctor.tool-missing` and `run.toolchain` reflect the state after installing. Findings (check `install`, subject the tool): `install.succeeded` (`info`, `command`, `version`), `install.failed` (`fail`, exit 3, `command`, `exit_code`, `stderr` tail; also when the command exited 0 and the tool is "still not found on PATH"), `install.skipped` (`info`, `reason`: `--no-install`, "stdout is not a terminal; pass --install …", or `declined`). `doctor.tool-missing` gained `install` evidence (the command or hint, or null) and its fix names the command and `cupel doctor --fix --install`. `doctor --fix --dry-run` fills `would_install` under the same permission (empty on a pipe without `--install`); `doctor --dry-run` without `--fix` stays an empty plan. ADR-022 §6 (`doctor.fix-pending`) is superseded; the flags lost `pending_step: P3.7`.
- **P3.7 — tests.** `cupel-adapt/tests/installs.rs` (4): commands per launcher (npm, pnpm, yarn, pip, uv, poetry) and for cargo, the hints for `go`, `cargo`, and `python3` pinned, no install for a producer the table does not list, and every registered runner and coverage producer has a command or a hint for its program. `cupel-cli` `install` unit tests (6): permission precedence and the terminal default, `Never` runs and asks nothing, `Ask` runs only what is accepted, `Granted` asks nothing, a failing command and a tool still missing are failures, `would_install` per permission, one finding per attempt. `cupel-cli/tests/install.rs` (9) on the new fixture `repos/doctor/runner-missing` (a Python suite with `runner = "pytest"`) with a `PATH` the test builds (`sh`, `git`, and any stand-in scripts): `doctor` names the command; `--fix --no-install` and `--fix` on a pipe leave `doctor.tool-missing` at `fail` and run nothing; a stand-in `python` that fails gives `install.failed` with its stderr; one that exits 0 without providing pytest fails as "still not found"; `doctor`, `check`, and `survey` dry runs list `would_install` only with `--install`; `check --install --only tests.flaky` reports the failed install and skips the check as `unavailable`. The real-tool test makes a venv in a temp directory with `python3 -m venv`, puts only its `bin` (plus `sh`, `git`) on `PATH`, and runs `doctor --fix --install`: `python -m pip install pytest` installs pytest into the venv from PyPI and doctor exits 0 with `install.succeeded` and `run.toolchain.pytest`; about 3 s for the doctor run and 5 s with the venv on this machine (pip's cache warm). It is skipped with a reason without `python3` or when `venv` fails; it sets `PIP_CONFIG_FILE=/dev/null`, because this machine's `~/.config/pip/pip.conf` says `user = true`, which pip refuses inside a venv. `cupel-cli/tests/doctor.rs` rewritten: `--fix` with nothing installable adds no findings; `--fix` is no longer pending. Goldens changed by evidence or description shape only: `baseline/diff.json`, `cli/describe.json`, `describe/language-*.json`, and the CLI and describe text snapshots.
- **P3.7 — dogfood and clones.** `make check` green with every tool on `PATH`: 1275 tests (+20 over P3.4's 1255). `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exits 0: 165 similar pairs and 18 clusters (under the 169/19 ratchets, which are not lowered here), `cyclomatic_max` 10, `tests.quality` clean. As first written the changed gate found `app::configured` (61 lines) and `check::run` (65) too long, a clone pair in the new adapt tests, `test.logic`/weak-assertion/duplicate-setup findings in the new tests, and two pre-existing clone groups in files the step had to touch (`registry.rs`'s argument refinements, two `extension.rs` tests); removed by `app::invoke`, installing inside `check::schedule`, one `With::with` behind every `Refine` method, a shared `get_error` in `extension.rs`, and table-driven, value-pinning install tests. `make dogfood` (the changed gate against local `main`) still fails on `tests.weakening` alone, as P3.0's and P3.1's did: five snapshots rewritten because the CLI's description and the run schema changed (the flags lost `pending P3.7`, `ToolDescription` gained `install`), and two `doctor.rs` tests whose subject no longer exists (`fix_reports_that_installing_arrives_in_p3_1`, `doctor_describes_fix_as_pending_p3_1`, replaced by `fix_with_nothing_installable_reports_no_installs` and `doctor_describes_fix_as_implemented`). Every other changed rule passes; no suppression was added.
- **P3.7 — open items.** (1) The `PerTestMap` producers (`cupel-adapt/src/per_test/`, another agent's area during this step) still report `install: None`; doctor installs their programs through the runner and coverage rows when those are required, but a per-test-only requirement (`tests --impacted`) gets no command. One line in `per_test/mod.rs` (`toolchain::install(&self.id(), missing, &ctx.root)`) closes it. (2) `uv add`/`poetry add` install into the project's environment, which is not on `PATH` unless activated, so doctor can report "still not found" after a successful install. (3) `tests --impacted` accepts the flags and installs nothing. (4) The terminal question is tested through an injected answer; the stdin prompt itself has no automated test (it needs a pseudo-terminal). (5) An install needs the network; offline it is `install.failed` with the package manager's stderr. (6) Bench producers have no `install`; none is a registered producer yet.
- **P3 — per-test map cache** ([ADR-083](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_083.md); follow-up (7), half of what unblocks P3.6). (1) *Cache.* Each per-test producer keeps one entry per project root, `.cupel/cache/per-test/<producer>/<hash of the root>/{report, sources.json}`, the report `{version, tests}` relative to the root, the record ADR-079's (`fresh.rs`, moved from `coverage/` to `cupel-adapt/src/fresh.rs` and shared: `Sources::changes` names added, changed, and removed files; `since` marks a file edited while the tools ran; `cache_dir`, `entry_name`, `entries`, `load`, `store`, `skip_dirs` serve both caches). (2) *Refresh* (`per_test/cache.rs`, `Staleness::refresh`): by file, a test is stale when it covers a changed or removed file or owns one (its own file; for Go, any `_test.go` of its package); a change to build or test configuration makes every test stale; new tests are measured and gone ones dropped (Go and Python list; Rust runs `-E 'not (<kept>)'`); a change touching no covered, owned, or test-holding file measures nothing. `PerTestMap` v2 adds `measured` and `reused`. (3) *One run for Rust.* `tool.llvm-cov-per-test` runs `cargo llvm-cov nextest --no-report` once with a cargo target runner (`CARGO_TARGET_<HOST>_RUNNER`, a script in the cache entry) that gives each test process its own profile directory, keyed by process ID and inherited by its children, recording `NEXTEST_BINARY_ID`/`NEXTEST_TEST_NAME`; each directory is merged with `llvm-profdata` and exported with `llvm-cov export` on all cores (children's profiles against the programs the build made). Python stays one pytest-cov run, Go one run per test. (4) *Estimate.* `Producer::estimate(ctx) -> Estimate { cost, note }` (cupel-core); `FactStore::ensure_wants` compares it, not `cost()`, with the depth. Per-test producers stay `deep` cold and estimate `standard` with a cache whose refresh is one suite run (Rust, Python) or at most 32 stale tests (Go). A dry run lists `per-test-map(cached)`, `per-test-map(refresh N stale)`, or `per-test-map(deep)` in the check's `needs`. The engine is unchanged: at `standard` with a cached map each mutant runs only its covering tests. (5) *`tests --impacted`* reports `measured`/`reused` per language and `(cached)` in text. (6) *Cupel's runner:* `[lang.rust] runner = "cargo-nextest"`, `test = "cargo nextest run --workspace"`; `make check` keeps `cargo test`. CI's `check` job installs nextest on both runners; the `dogfood` job installs none of the test tools (it skips every check that runs tests), so P3.6 must add nextest and cargo-llvm-cov there. (7) *Bugs found on the way:* nextest exports `NEXTEST_PROFILE` to its tests, so under Cupel's own runner (`--profile cupel`) every nested nextest run in Cupel's suite failed ("profile `cupel` not found") and the baseline was red; the runner's listing and the per-test producer now pass `NEXTEST_PROFILE=default` (`cargo_nextest.rs`, outside this step's area). And `.cargo-lock` (no extension) was handed to `llvm-cov export` as an object; only executable, unhidden files now are.
- **P3 — per-test map cache: tests.** 1279 in all in `make check` (+24 over P3.4's 1255). New: `cupel-core` `per_test` +1 (counts), `producer_inputs` +2 (a want made by a cheap estimate; dry-run needs); `cupel-adapt` `fresh` +2, `per_test::cache` 4, `llvm_cov` 2 (the runner script, the executables), `per_test` +8 real-tool tests (reuse, a test-file edit, a source edit, a new and a deleted test, another machine's cache, a Rust refresh running only the new test, a Go package's test file, the estimate); `cupel-mutate` `stack` +2 (a cached map selects at `standard`; without one, the whole suite); `cupel-cli` `tests_verb` +2 (Python and Rust second runs cached). Snapshots: `schemas__run_schema` and the four render snapshots (`measured`, `reused`). No golden changed. Dogfood: the changed gate passes with six suppressions until 2026-11-15 in `.cupel/suppressions.toml` (the five snapshots that gained `measured`/`reused` and the version test renamed for v2, reported by `tests.weakening`); the tree gate passes at 169 pairs and 19 clusters.
- **P3 — per-test map cache: measurement** (2026-10-15, release binary at 89fb0ad, a fresh clone, every tool on `PATH`, 8 cores, Apple silicon; other agents' test suites were running on the same machine throughout, so every wall time is contended). *One run versus one per test:* on Cupel, the instrumented build plus the whole suite took 336 s and attributing 1278 test processes 13 s (258 MB of profiles, 38 tests with child processes) in the first experiment; the old producer ran over 20 minutes before it was stopped (P3.4 timing). On the per-test fixture the maps are identical. *`tests --impacted --changed`* after a comment inside `metrics::symbol_coverage`: cold **542 s**, 1279 tests measured, 16 impacted; second run **2 s**, `measured 0, reused 1279`; `--dry-run` of the mutation check said `per-test-map(cached)`. *Refresh* after a second one-function change (`metrics::cyclomatic`): **1066 s**, 128 measured, 1151 reused. Slower than cold because the mutation check before it ran the configured coverage command, `cargo llvm-cov --lcov`, which cleans `target/llvm-cov-target` by default, so the refresh rebuilt the instrumented workspace, and the 128 include the matrix tests (about 150 s each). *Mutation* (`cupel check --base HEAD~1 --only tests.mutation --depth standard --no-gate`, the `symbol_coverage` change): **2479 s wall**, check 1938 s, 3 mutants (`>`→`>=`, `0`→`1`, `/`→`*`), all killed, score 1.0, `per_test` used: **16 tests per mutant** instead of the ~1255 of the whole suite, yet each mutant's run took 562 s. Against P3.6's 2242 s for 4 mutants this is no faster: a Cupel mutant's cost is now its rebuild (every slot builds the workspace, then each mutant rebuilds `cupel-checks` and every dependent) and the two `cupel-fixtures::matrix` tests every check module's mutant selects, which run every check on every fixture. So per-test selection works but does not by itself unblock P3.6; schemata (P4.1) remove the rebuild, and the matrix tests remain a floor of about 150 s per mutant of any check.
- **P3 — per-test map cache: open items.** (1) Staleness is by file: a one-function edit in `metrics.rs` re-measures the 101–128 tests that cover the file, not only those that ran the function. (2) Holes that leave a map naming too few tests (ADR-083): changes to files no test covers (fixture data, `[scope] exclude`d paths such as `crates/cupel-fixtures/**`, which the record leaves out, types and consts without counters, `inventory` registrations). (3) The configured coverage command (`cargo llvm-cov`) and the per-test producer share `target/llvm-cov-target`, and the former cleans it; giving the per-test build its own target directory would keep refreshes incremental. (4) `TestResults` is not cached. (5) The NEXTEST_PROFILE fix has no permanent test: it shows only when the test process itself runs with `NEXTEST_PROFILE=cupel` (`NEXTEST_PROFILE=cupel cargo test -p cupel-adapt --test selection --test per_test` fails without it). (6) The per-test producers construct `Availability::MissingTool { install: None }`, whose type changed on `main` (P3.7); expect a conflict there at merge. (7) A Rust repository whose baseline runner is `cargo test` refreshes a nextest map cache at `standard` that the engine then discards.

### P4 — Schemata, daemon, budget scheduler

**Goal:** Compile-once mutation for Rust, a long-lived daemon with byte-identical output, and value-ordered scheduling under a wall-clock budget.

**Status:** complete (2026-10-28) · every step complete; DoD line (4) is not met and stays an open item (P4 Notes "P4 — Definition of Done, remeasured")

#### P4.1 Rust mutant schemata

**Status:** complete (2026-10-15, 9ffa7d7) · [ADR-082](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_082.md) · [ADR-005](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_005.md)

- Rust schemata strategy registered with the mutation engine: rewrite each site as `if cupel_mutant_id() == N { mutated } else { original }` preserving expression types; compile once; run each mutant with `CUPEL_MUTANT=N`. Validate the schemata build compiles before running; fall back to file-copy on failure (recorded in the check result). Write **ADR-005** on Go timing.

*Tests first:* for every Rust fixture, outcomes under schemata == outcomes under file-copy (the design's schemata validation test); a fixture that defeats the type-preservation rule triggers fallback cleanly.

#### P4.2 Daemon (`serve`)

**Status:** complete (2026-10-15, 24e2c95) · [ADR-009](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_009.md) · [ADR-085](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_085.md)

- `cupel-serve`: holds parsed trees (content-hash keyed), store connection, coverage map, last plan; `notify` watcher for incremental reparse and re-hash; Unix socket at `.cupel/serve.sock`; CLI auto-connects when present, `CUPEL_NO_DAEMON=1` bypasses. Write **ADR-009**.
- Parity test: every fixture run cold and via daemon is byte-identical after redaction.

#### P4.3 Budget scheduler

**Status:** complete (2026-10-16, 477b4d5) · [ADR-086](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_086.md)

- Cost from store history (p50 of last 10 runs of the task shape) else manifest default; value = `tier_weight × novelty × (changed ? 2 : 1)`; greedy knapsack with dependency closure; skipped tasks reported with `reason: budget` and `est_ms`. `--jobs` concurrency cap; CPU-bound on rayon, process-spawning on tokio.

*Tests first:* scheduler unit tests on synthetic DAGs (budget respected, dependencies honored, highest value chosen, deterministic order given a seed); `--budget 1s` on a fixture skips mutation and says so.

#### P4.4 One suite run for `TestResults`, `CoverageMap`, and `PerTestMap`

**Status:** complete (2026-10-15, ef916d3) · [ADR-084](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_084.md) · the ≤ 10 min line of the P4 DoD is missed by 30 s (630 s warm on a quiet machine; P3 Notes, "P3.6 — measurement on a quiet machine")

Added after P3.6's second measurement (P3 Notes "P3.6 — measurement with schemata and the cached map"): a changed run on Cupel runs the suite three times — baseline, coverage report, per-test refresh — each with its own instrumented build, before the first mutant.

- Where the runner and the coverage tool are one toolchain (Rust: `cargo llvm-cov nextest`; Python: `pytest --cov` with contexts; Go: `go test -coverprofile` per package), one run yields the three facts: the per-test producer's run already holds every test's result, and the union of its per-test profiles is the coverage report. Decide how a producer yields several facts (`Runner::outputs` from P3.5 is the precedent) and how the scheduler prefers the shared run to three separate ones; write **ADR-084**. Cache `TestResults` under the same freshness record (follow-up (7), second half). Make per-test staleness by function (symbol spans), not by file (ADR-083 open item (1)), and give the per-test build its own target directory so the coverage command's clean does not discard it (open item (3)).

*Tests first:* on the Rust per-test fixture the three facts from one run equal the three from separate runs; `--dry-run` on a changed Cupel plans one suite run where it planned three; a one-function change refreshes only the tests that ran that function.

#### P4 Definition of Done

- Schemata parity holds for all Rust fixtures; warm daemon `check --depth quick` < 1 s on the Cupel repo.
- `--budget` never exceeds its wall clock by more than one task's timeout.
- README: `serve`, `--budget`, performance characteristics.
- `cupel check --changed --depth standard` including `tests.mutation` on a one-function change in the Cupel repo, with a warm cache, completes in ≤ 10 min (record in Notes), and the P3.6 gate is added.

#### P4 Notes

- **P4.2 — the daemon** ([ADR-085](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_085.md)). A new crate, `cupel-serve` (→ `cupel-core` only; `cupel-cli` and `cupel-fixtures` → `cupel-serve`), holds the transport: the protocol (ADR-009), the server loop, the client, and the `notify` watcher, generic over a `Handler`. `cupel-cli`'s handler (`serve.rs`, `Requests`) rebuilds the client's `Environment` and calls the same `cupel_cli::run` a cold process calls, so there is one code path; `Environment` gains `stderr` (a `Stream`: the process's standard error, or the client's connection), `session` (a `Session`: what outlives an invocation), `cancel` (the token a run's cancellation sits under, via the new additive `Cancellation::under`), and `path`. `main` calls `cupel_cli::invoke` (`connect.rs`): it resolves the root from `--cwd` in the raw arguments, and when `<root>/.cupel/serve.sock` exists it sends the invocation, writing each streamed `stderr` line as it arrives; it runs locally for `CUPEL_NO_DAEMON=1`, `serve`, non-UTF-8 arguments, a run that could ask on the terminal, no socket file, or a stale one, and with one stderr line (`cupel: ran without the daemon: <reason>`) when the daemon refuses (another build: version plus the executable's size and modification time; another protocol; another `PATH`) or breaks off. `cupel serve [--watch] [--socket PATH]` serves in the foreground until `serve --stop`, `--timeout`, a signal, or the socket file going (checked every 200 ms); in-flight runs are cancelled and answered on stop; a client's hang-up cancels its run; connections are answered concurrently; the socket is `0600`; `serve` loads no configuration. Findings: `serve.stopped`, `serve.not-running` (info), `serve.running`, `serve.failed` (exit 3); `doctor.daemon-running` (info), `doctor.daemon-mismatch`, `doctor.daemon-stale` (warn) when `.cupel/serve.sock` exists. `--mcp` stays pending (P7.7, `cli.not-implemented`).
- **P4.2 — what the daemon holds.** The parse cache (`cupel_lang::ParseCache`, shared through `LanguageRegistry::with_cache` and `Catalog::with_cache`): trees by content hash and grammar, now also by path, so `reparse(path)` starts from the file's previous tree (one `InputEdit` spanning the longest common prefix and suffix, cut at character boundaries, then tree-sitter's incremental parse) and `sweep()` after each request drops trees no existing file last parsed to (old contents, a range run's base copies). Each tree also keeps what extractors derived from it (`Parsed::derived`, by file, result type, and extractor): the query producers (`QueryProducer`: symbols, decisions, normalized trees, mutation sites, test frames, API surface, roots) and the import reader use it, so an unchanged file is neither parsed nor queried again; this, not the parse, is most of the saving (below). Every request still reads and hashes every file it uses, so correctness never depends on the watcher. Not held: the store connection (`Store::open` per use, five call sites in two crates; a warm run's whole store work is inside its 233 ms), the coverage and per-test maps (their producers in `cupel-adapt`, P4.4's area, decide freshness by hashing every file under the root, which is most of `static.crap`'s 135 ms warm; holding a decoded map needs a hook there), and the last plan (nothing reads it; P4.3's budget scheduler would).
- **P4.2 — protocol** ([ADR-009](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_009.md)): JSON Lines over a Unix stream socket, one request per connection, every request carrying `protocol: 1` and the client's build; replies are zero or more `stderr` lines then one of `done`, `status`, `stopping`, `refused`, `error`; a message is at most 64 MiB; unknown members are ignored; `status` and `stop` are answered for any build. `nc -U .cupel/serve.sock` can drive it.
- **P4.2 — parity proof.** `make parity` (CI runs it after `make check`) sets `DAEMON_PARITY=1` for the fixture suite: every `run_cupel` copies its directory before the cold run, starts `cupel serve --socket` outside the copy (so the run sees no daemon of its own), serves the same command as a `--dry-run` (warming the trees), then for real, and panics with a unified diff unless the exit code, the redacted stdout (ADR-003 for envelopes; for text, the `run` line and each `N ms`), and stderr (event lines with run ID and timings masked) match. Every one of the 139 CLI goldens (`baseline`, `cli`, `describe`, `doctor`, `init`, `survey`, and the 11 per check) is asserted on such a run, plus every other `run_cupel` in the suite: all 71 `cupel-fixtures` tests pass that way, with every tool on `PATH` and nothing skipped (`make parity` 390 s wall; 362 s of test time against 146 s cold). The other 50 goldens (`facts/<lang>/<fact>`) are produced in process, without the CLI; `every_fact_golden_holds_for_trees_reparsed_from_edited_ones` makes them hold for trees reparsed from edited ones (every fixture file edited and reparsed, the originals swept, the original text reparsed back, every fact read from those trees alone). `tests/parity.rs` proves the harness catches a difference. The first parity run found one: a text rendering's check durations.
- **P4.2 — measurement** (release build at fbd83d5, this worktree: 333 Rust files, 78k lines, 4133 symbols; `cupel check --depth quick --no-gate --format json`; 8-core Apple silicon with other agents' builds running, load average 33–67). Without coverage tools on `PATH`: cold 0.97–1.06 s (`run.duration_ms` 957), daemon's first request 0.94 s, **warm 0.28–0.31 s** (233 ms; checks: clones 30, complexity 7, crap 15, dead-code 55, deps 7, quality 7). With every tool on `PATH` and a fresh cached coverage report: cold 1.07–1.20 s, **warm 0.42–0.47 s** (403 ms, of which `static.crap` 135 ms: the coverage producer re-hashing every file to decide the cached report is fresh, and reading it). `describe`: 30 ms cold, 36 ms through the daemon. Before `Parsed::derived`, warm was 0.8–2.5 s against 1.5–2.3 s cold on a busier machine: a sample of the warm daemon put most of its time in tree-sitter query cursors (symbols 4523 samples, roots 3710, decisions 2596, normalization 2526, the Rust import graph 2200) and file reads and hashes (`ParseCache::parse` 2475, `open` 2033), so trees alone saved little. What a warm run still does: walk and hash every file once per producer, run the checks, ask git for `HEAD`, write the run record and the store. **The P4 DoD target holds** (warm < 1 s). Where the time can go instead: with `cargo llvm-cov` installed and Cupel's `[lang.rust] coverage` configured, `static.crap` wants a coverage report at every depth (a check's `wants` are made whatever the depth; only producers' wants are filtered), so after any change the next `quick` run, cold or warm, measures coverage with the whole suite: 785 s here.
- **P4.2 — bugs found on the way.** (1) The fact store: concurrent first opens of a new store failed with `database is locked` (SQLite does not wait on the busy timeout to switch to WAL); reproduced with four cold `cupel check` processes on a fresh repository (4 of 100 exit 2) and by the daemon's concurrent-clients test; the switch now happens under the writer lock (`cupel-store`, with a test). (2) `CUPEL_*` variables of a `cupel` process reach the tools it runs: a `CUPEL_NO_DAEMON=1` cold run's coverage measurement passed it to the test suite, where it kept a test's client from the daemon (the test now clears `CUPEL_*`; producers still pass them on).
- **P4.2 — gates.** `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` passes with clones at 165 pairs and 18 clusters (ratchets 169/19 left as they are). The first cut added two clone pairs (test helpers), a `app → serve → app` import cycle (broken by moving `Environment`/`Output` to `environment.rs` and handing `serve::verb` the `run` function), a 61-line `doctor::daemon` and a 62-line `run_verb` (split), and 13 `tests.quality` findings in the new tests (loops, shared setups, weak assertions; all rewritten). The changed gate (`make dogfood`) fails on this branch the way P3.0's, P3.1's, and P3.3's did: `tests.weakening` reports the three CLI snapshots rewritten from `serve … · pending P4.2` to the built verb's summary (`describe`, `format`, and the text renderer's CLI description), and `static.clones` the pre-existing pair `exit_2_for_a_misspelled_flag_suggests_the_right_one` / `exit_2_for_conflicting_flags_names_both` in `tests/exit_codes.rs`, which this step touched to drop `serve` from the pending verbs; everything else in it passes. A trial merge of this branch with local `main` at bfa7608 (P4.4 merged) is clean, clippy-clean, and passes all 1379 tests with every tool on `PATH`.
- **P4.2 — tests.** 1353 in `make check` (1317 on `main` at 5b9a608), all with every tool on `PATH` and no skips. New: `cupel-serve` 17 (`tests/protocol.rs` 5: round trips, shapes, end of input, malformed and cut-short and oversized lines; `tests/server.rs` 12 over real sockets with a scripted handler: status and runs, streaming order, refusals by build/protocol/`PATH` with status and stop still answered, malformed requests, stop, stale sockets, a second daemon refused, concurrency, hang-up cancels, stop cancels in-flight runs, socket removal, the watcher); `cupel-cli/tests/serve.rs` 10 with the real binary (answers equal to `CUPEL_NO_DAEMON=1`, stale socket, another build refused with its line and silent under `--quiet`, four concurrent clients, `--events stderr` order equal to cold, `serve --stop` and `serve.not-running`, `serve.running` and the start line, `--mcp` pending, `--watch` reparse then a run equal to cold, `doctor` running and stale); `cupel-lang` 5 (incremental reparse equals a cold tree over three edits, reparse of unknown/unchanged/gone files, sweep, derivation once per file and content, the edit computation); `cupel-core` 1 (`Cancellation::under`); `cupel-store` 1; `cupel-fixtures` 2 (`tests/parity.rs`, the reparsed fact goldens). Every test owns its socket in a temporary directory and stops its daemon (drop guards kill one that does not stop; daemons start with `--timeout`).
- **P4.2 — open items.** (1) A repository whose root path is longer than about 85 bytes cannot have a daemon: a Unix socket path is limited to 104 bytes on macOS (108 on Linux); `serve` exits 3 with `serve.failed` saying so, and clients only look at `.cupel/serve.sock`. A fallback location under the temporary directory, keyed by the root's hash, would lift it. (2) The coverage and per-test maps are not held (above); the coverage producer's freshness check is 135 ms of a warm run here. (3) A warm run still reads and hashes each file once per producer (seven producers); one read per request would need a per-request view in the parse cache. (4) `PATH` is the only variable compared; other environment differences between client and daemon go unnoticed. (5) A client's hang-up leaves a `partial` run record that a killed process would not. (6) A panic message in the daemon is recorded in one process-wide slot, so two runs panicking at once can swap messages. (7) A stale socket from a killed daemon is ignored by clients, reported by `doctor`, and removed by `serve --stop` or the next `serve`, but never by a client. (8) `--socket` serves only programs that speak ADR-009; the CLI has no way to name another socket. (9) `check --depth quick` on a repository with a configured coverage tool measures coverage after every change (above): a P4.3/P4.4 question.
- **P4.2 — for the integrator.** Review `crates/cupel-serve/` (new), `crates/cupel-cli/src/{serve.rs, connect.rs, session.rs}` (new), `app.rs` (`Environment` members, `run_verb`'s `serve` arm, `serve` without configuration), `run.rs` (`Run::within`, `Invocation::session`), `events.rs`, `catalog.rs`, `check.rs`/`tests_verb.rs` (the session's cache), `doctor.rs` (the daemon row), `cli.rs`/`registry.rs` (`ServeArgs`, `--stop`, `--mcp` pending), `error.rs` (`pending_flag`). Outside the stated area: `crates/cupel-lang/src/{parse.rs, langs/mod.rs, lib.rs, producer.rs, imports.rs}` and `tests/cache.rs` (reparse, sweep, `with_cache`, `Parsed::derived`); `crates/cupel-core/src/cancel.rs` and `tests/run_controls.rs` (`Cancellation::under`, additive; `schedule.rs` untouched); `crates/cupel-store/src/lib.rs` and `tests/store.rs` (the WAL race); `crates/cupel-fixtures/{Cargo.toml, src/lib.rs, src/run.rs, src/parity.rs, src/conformance.rs, tests/parity.rs, tests/fact_goldens.rs, golden/cli/describe.json}`; `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` (`notify` 8.2.0, `cupel-serve`, `nix`'s `poll` feature in `cupel-serve`); `Makefile` (`parity`); `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (`make parity`); the CLI's snapshots and `tests/{exit_codes.rs, runner_baseline.rs}`. `cupel-adapt` and `schedule.rs` are untouched. These notes sit at the top of P4 Notes so they do not collide with P4.4's, which `main` added after P4.1's; reorder them at will.
- **P4.1 — the rewrite** ([ADR-082](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_082.md)). `cupel_mutate::schemata`: the engine asks `schemata::rewriter(lang)` (`Some` for `rust`, `None` elsewhere) unless `[lang.<id>] mutation = { strategy = "file-copy" }` (default `schemata`). The Rust rewriter (`schemata/rust.rs`, `tree-sitter-rust` in `cupel-mutate`; no new edge between Cupel crates) gates each mutant's smallest whole expression — the binary or compound-assignment expression of an operator token, else the site's own node — as `if cupel_mutant_id() == N { variant } else { original }`; mutants of one expression share one chain; inside a variant every other site keeps its original text (only mutant `N` is live there), so text grows linearly with nesting, and each branch is exactly the code a file copy would build, which keeps single evaluation, `&&`/`||` short-circuiting, borrows, moves, and inferred literal types. Parentheses only where the parent needs them (operands, receivers, casts, indexes, statements, assignment left sides, let chains, `let … else` values; rustc's `unused_parens` warns elsewhere). A multi-line variant is folded onto one line so no line moves. `N` is the mutant's 1-based position in its file's run. The reader `fn cupel_mutant_id() -> u32` (`CUPEL_MUTANT` read once into a `OnceLock`, 0 when unset) is appended once at the end of the file; a gate inside `k` inline modules calls it through `super::` × `k`. Every engine run now sets `CUPEL_MUTANT` explicitly (`0` for references and file copies), so a variable inherited from an outer mutant run never selects a fixture's mutant.
- **P4.1 — exclusions** (run as file copies, reason in `fallback`): patterns (match arms but not guards, `let`/`if let`/`for`/parameter patterns, closure parameters), constant contexts (`const`/`static` items, `const` blocks, `const fn`, array lengths and types, enum discriminants, const generics), macro token trees and definitions, attributes, tuple field indexes, constants the compiler checks (a literal that is an index, a divisor or shift amount, or beside another literal, and an operator between two literals: deny-by-default `unconditional_panic`/`arithmetic_overflow` reject such a file copy as `unviable` but cannot see the constant behind a switch, which would make it `killed`), a multi-line expression holding a line comment or a multi-line literal, and a site not where its span says.
- **P4.1 — validation and fallback.** Before any mutant of a file runs, every slot it uses writes the schemata source and runs the file's selected tests with `CUPEL_MUTANT=0`; all must be green. On failure the gates on the lines the compiler's `error` diagnostics name in the file (`Rewriter::culprits`, `--> path:line:col`) leave the schemata (`did not compile under schemata (line L)`) and the file is rebuilt, at most twice more; if the errors name no gated line, or tests fail, or it still fails, the whole file runs as file copies with `the schemata build of `<file>` failed: <first error>` (or `… still failed after 2 rebuilds`) on every mutant without its own reason. A failed validation is never a production error. Gated mutants run first with no write (no rebuild); excluded ones then run as file copies in the same slots. A slot's timeout is `timeout_factor ×` its validation run (build included), the limit a file copy gets.
- **P4.1 — payload and check.** `MutationOutcomes` version 2: `Mutant.strategy` (`schemata` | `file-copy`, `null` when not run here) and `Mutant.fallback` (why a mutant of a language with a rewriter ran as a file copy), both `#[serde(default)]` so version-1 rows in the store still read; reuse copies them from the stored mutant; the cache key is unchanged (the strategy is not part of it, ADR-082 §8). `tests.mutation` gains metrics `schemata` and `fallback` (mutants run this time each way) and one note per fallback reason (`N mutants ran as file copies: <reason>`). Goldens: the five language envelopes gain the two metrics (`schemata` 5 for Rust, 0 elsewhere; `fallback` 0), `describe.json` the two metric specs, the README check table the two keys. The Rust survivor set is unchanged (`>=`→`>` survived, `*`→`/` equivalent?, `&&`→`||`, `-`→`+`, `1`→`0` killed, `refund` uncovered, score 0.75).
- **P4.1 — parity proof.** `cupel-mutate/tests/parity.rs` runs the engine twice on each Rust fixture with a suite and a coverage report, once per strategy (a `--config` file outside the fixture, so its committed coverage report stays fresh), and compares every mutant's position, replacement, outcome, tests run, killing tests, and equivalence mark exactly: `tests.mutation/rust` (5 run, 1 uncovered) and `coverage/rust` (4 run — `0`→`1` and `+=`→`-=` killed, two `>=`→`>` survived — 9 uncovered) agree, every schemata mutant with strategy `schemata` and no fallback. Fallback, with cargo-llvm-cov to re-measure coverage: a `String::from("hi ") + name` added to the fixture makes `+`→`-` leave the schemata on the compiler's line (unviable as a file copy under both strategies, outcome sets still equal, the other five gated); a function named `cupel_mutant_id` in the file makes every build fail on the reader's line, so the whole file runs as file copies with `error[E0428]` as the reason and the same outcomes. `tests/schemata.rs` compiles a 16-mutant program with rustc once as schemata and once per mutant as a file copy, and every `CUPEL_MUTANT=N` run prints what its file copy prints (short-circuit side effects, borrows of `String`s, a guard, a folded multi-line closure, a `let … else`, an inline module). The other Rust fixtures (`per-test`, `test-results`, `tests.flaky`, `rust`, `detect/*`) have no coverage configuration, so the engine makes no mutants there.
- **P4.1 — Go** ([ADR-005](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_005.md)): Go stays on file copies in P4. Rewriting the Go fixture by hand (immediately called `func() bool` literals for comparisons, generic `cupelAddSub[T number]`-style helpers for arithmetic, `cupelPick` for literals) compiled and reproduced every outcome, but untyped constants become typed run-time values silently (`0.1 + 0.2` is 0.3 as a constant, 0.30000000000000004 through the helper), and generic inference falls back to default types (`var half float64 = cupelMulDiv(1, 1, 2)` does not compile); a rebuild of the fixture costs 0.07 s over a run with only `CUPEL_MUTANT` changed.
- **P4.1 — timing** (release binary at 9ffa7d7, a fresh clone of this branch with one commit adding a comment inside `cupel-checks`' `metrics::weighted_mean`, every tool on `PATH`, `cupel check --base HEAD~1 --only tests.mutation --depth standard --no-gate`). **Run with 2 workers, not 4** (`mutation = { jobs = 2 }` through `--config`): the disk had 32 GiB free and four slots of Cupel plus the clone's own builds need more. **The machine was heavily loaded** throughout by the other agents' builds and measurements (load average 45–77 on 8 cores at start and end, 176 just after), and it appears to have slept: the shell's wall clock read 30 246 s while the run's own `duration_ms` (a monotonic clock) is **8356 s**. Result: 5 mutants (the same five as P3.4: `+=`→`-=` twice, `*`→`/`, `==`→`!=`, `/`→`*`), all `schemata`, no fallback, all killed, score 1.0; the check itself 6798 s; by file times, ~4 h of wall for the baseline and ~2.3 h for the stale coverage report, then ~6300 s for the engine (both slots building the workspace and validating, then the mutants); each mutant's run 433–1788 s (`/`→`*` 433, `*`→`/` and `==`→`!=` 756, the two `+=`→`-=` 1788), i.e. about 1670 s per mutant over the whole run against P3.4's 2386 s / 5 = 477 s on a quieter machine with 4 workers. These numbers measure the machine's contention more than the engine. The controlled comparison, back to back in one of the run's slots under the same load: `cargo test --workspace --no-run` after rewriting `metrics.rs` (what every file-copy mutant costs) took **37 s and 40 s**; the same with only `CUPEL_MUTANT` changed (what every schemata mutant after the first costs) **2 s and 2 s**. So schemata remove the per-mutant build as designed (about 38 s per Cupel mutant in `cupel-checks`, more for a file deeper in the crate graph), and what remains per mutant is the whole workspace suite under `cargo test`, which only per-test selection (the parallel agent's work) can shrink. A Cupel mutant is not yet seconds.
- **P4.1 — tests.** 1275 in `make check` (1255 before P4.1), all with every tool on `PATH` and no skips. New or changed: `cupel-mutate` unit 15 (`schemata::rust` 12: each operator class, nested chains, guards, every exclusion with its reason, checked constants, folding, the reader appended once and through `super::`, culprit lines; `history` reuse copies the strategy), `tests/schemata.rs` 1 (rustc), `tests/parity.rs` 3, `tests/mutants.rs` +1 (`mutant::span`), `tests/settings.rs` (`strategy`); `cupel-core` `mutation_outcomes` +2 (strategy serialization, a v1 row still reads) and version 2; `cupel-checks` `tests_mutation` +1 (schemata/fallback metrics and notes) and the manifest's metrics.
- **P4.1 — open items.** (1) Cupel mutants still cost a full `cargo test --workspace` each; with schemata the remaining term is the suite, so P3.6 waits on per-test selection at `standard` with a cached `PerTestMap`. (2) The timing above is from a loaded machine with 2 workers; repeat it on a quiet one with 4 before using it for the P4 DoD. (3) Fallback is per file with compiler-named lines; a schemata build that fails without naming a gated line (a macro expanding a gated expression, an error in another file) sends the whole file to file copies; bisection could narrow it. (4) A slowly finishing (not looping) mutant could time out under one strategy and not the other; parity on the fixtures holds. (5) `line!()`, `column!()`, and panic locations inside a folded multi-line variant report the expression's first line. (6) The reader's name can collide with a user function of the same name (whole-file fallback, proven). (7) Go schemata deferred (ADR-005). (8) `strategy = "schemata"` for a language without a rewriter silently means file copies.
- **P4.1 — for the integrator.** Review `crates/cupel-mutate/src/{schemata.rs, schemata/rust.rs, execute.rs, engine.rs}` and `crates/cupel-core/src/mutation.rs` (payload v2). Outside the stated area: `crates/cupel-store/tests/store.rs` and `crates/cupel-core/tests/mutation_outcomes.rs` (the two new `Mutant` fields in literals), `crates/cupel-mutate/Cargo.toml` (tree-sitter, tree-sitter-rust), `Cargo.lock`, `crates/cupel-mutate/tests/{support/mod.rs, stack.rs}` (`Run.config`). `engine.rs` changes are confined to `Running` (a `rewriter` field, `Running::new`, the per-file call to `schemata::run_file`, recording `strategy`/`fallback`); the per-test agent's `wants`/depth rule is untouched. The measurement removed the P3.6 clone's 25 GB of idle mutation slots under the system temporary directory (ADR-080: slots are disposable and rebuilt) to make room; its clone is untouched. `make dogfood` (changed and tree) passes with clones at 169/19.
- **P4.4 — design** ([ADR-084](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_084.md)). A *joint producer* (`cupel_core::JointProducer`, `ProducerRegistry::register_joint`/`joints_for`) makes several facts in one run and names, per fact kind, the per-kind producer it stands in for; it is kept apart from the per-kind lists, so `candidates`, pins, `describe`, and the support matrix never see it, and no fact kind was added. The fact store (`schedule.rs`, decision in `cupel-core/src/joint.rs`, `Deciding`) uses one for `(kind, lang)` when it makes the kind, is `Ready`, stands in for exactly the producer the store would choose for each of its kinds (first ready candidate after pins and detection), is within the depth, and saves a run: its estimate is `cached`, or at least two of its kinds are *reached* by the run's tasks (closure of needs through producers' needs and affordable wants, computed once per language before the baseline, `FactStore::reached_by`) and would each run on their own (not made, chosen producer's estimate not `cached`). Every fact it makes is kept as if made alone (`FactStore::keep`, so `TestResults` passes the exit-4 gate); a joint that fails or does not make the kind leaves it to its own producer, and runs at most once per language. A want already made counts as affordable. Three joints in `cupel-adapt/src/suite/`: `tool.llvm-cov-nextest` (Rust: baseline from nextest's JUnit, per-test map from per-process profiles, coverage from `cargo llvm-cov report --lcov` over the same profiles linked into the target directory), `tool.pytest-cov-contexts` (Python: one pytest run with `--cov --cov-context=test`, `COVERAGE_FILE` under `.cupel/`, contexts from `coverage json --show-contexts`), `tool.go-test-cover` (Go: `go test -json -coverprofile`; the per-test map stays one run per test). A joint applies only when the configured coverage command is the toolchain table's default (`fuses`), so customized commands — every mutation fixture's — keep their separate runs and goldens. `Producer::estimate` now takes `&Upstream` (the producer's needs and wants already made). `runner::finished` is the shared invocation-from-a-finished-run (the runners and the joints); `Instrumented` (`per_test/llvm_cov.rs`) is the shared instrumented nextest run.
- **P4.4 — cache and the baseline.** Each joint keeps `.cupel/cache/suite/<joint>/<root>/{report, sources.json}` (`{version, run: {invocation, tests, coverage}}`, ADR-079's record); Rust and Python also write the per-test producer's entry with the same record (`per_test::store_tests`), and a cached joint also requires that entry unchanged (`per_test::fresh_tests`). A cached run makes its facts without running anything, a red baseline included (refuses with exit 4); a run with an incomplete invocation, or one that made a fact short, is not cached. Any change under the root reruns the suite. The instrumented Rust build lives in `target/cupel-llvm-cov` (`CARGO_LLVM_COV_TARGET_DIR`), shared by the joint and the per-test refresh, whose raw profiles are removed before each run (ADR-083 open item (3)).
- **P4.4 — staleness by function** (ADR-084 §7, `per_test/functions.rs`). Per-test cache version 2 stores a 64-bit BLAKE3 prefix of each line of every covered file as measured (`lines`). For a changed file with stored lines and functions in the run's `Symbols` (the per-test producers now want `Symbols`), a Myers diff of line hashes gives hunks; each must lie inside one function (innermost) with an unchanged line, whose measured span (its span now mapped back through the diff, plus the changed lines) makes stale exactly the tests that ran a line of it; kept tests' lines are renumbered through the diff. Anything else is the file rule: a hunk outside every function, a new or wholly rewritten function, no `Symbols` for the file (outside the scope), no stored lines, or a changed hash with unchanged lines. The estimate uses the same rule (`refresh 1 stale` where the file rule said 2).
- **P4.4 — dry runs.** A planned check's `needs` gain, per language, the suite facts it reaches: `test-results+coverage-map+per-test-map(one run)` or `(cached)` for a joint (Go: `test-results+coverage-map(…)`), else `test-results(run)`, `coverage-map(run)` / `coverage-map(cached)` beside ADR-083's `per-test-map(…)`, which a joint's entry replaces. On Cupel a changed `tests.mutation` plans `["symbols", "mutation-outcomes", "test-conventions", "test-results+coverage-map+per-test-map(one run)"]`, and `(cached)` with nothing changed.
- **P4.4 — measurement:** see P3 Notes, "P3.6 — measurement with one suite run": cold 956 s, warm 734 s (one suite run each, 16 tests per mutant), unchanged 1 s; the 10-minute target is missed by ~2 minutes on a loaded machine.
- **P4.4 — tests.** 1343 in `make check` (1317 on `main` at 5b9a608), all with every tool on `PATH` and no skips. New: `cupel-core/tests/joint.rs` 7 (one joint run for two runs, separate runs when coverage is cached or only one fact is needed, a cached joint for one fact, a pin keeps separate runs, a red joint baseline refuses, a joint-made want reaches the engine beyond its producer's depth, dry-run entries); `cupel-adapt` `per_test::functions` 5 (added, changed, and deleted lines in one function, outside or new functions, nested), `suite` `fuses` 2, `tests/per_test.rs` +3 (a one-function change re-measures one test in Rust, Python, and Go, the other test's lines moved) and the estimate seeing `Symbols`, `tests/suite.rs` 6 (parity of the three facts, exact after dropping durations and command lines: Rust, Python, Go; the cache lifecycle `one run` → `cached` → `one run` for Python and Rust; a customized command leaves the joint unconfigured); `cupel-cli/tests/suite_run.rs` 3 (dry run one joint run vs three, a reused green baseline and a rerun after a source change, a cached red baseline exits 4 twice with the cache untouched). No golden or snapshot changed; the engine's fixtures (`tests.mutation` survivors and 0.75 score, `tests.flaky`, `tests.coverage`) are unchanged because their coverage commands are not the defaults. `make dogfood` (changed, against local `main`) passes with no suppression; `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` passes with clones at 166 pairs and 18 clusters.
- **P4.4 — open items.** (1) *Measured on a quiet machine:* 630 s warm, 30 s over the line (P3 Notes "P3.6 — measurement on a quiet machine"); remeasured on 2026-10-28 at 1 172 s warm (P4 Notes "P4 — Definition of Done, remeasured"); the halves are the suite run (317 s; the `cupel-fixtures::matrix` tests are its longest) and the slots' per-change work plus the mutants (311 s). (2) Plain runners do not cache `TestResults` (ADR-084 consequences). (3) A joint applies only to the table's default coverage command; recognizing more commands (Python's `--cov=<pkg>`, `--cov-branch`) is a `fuses` change plus parity tests. (4) Rust coverage hit counts come from one process per test (ADR-084 §4); line sets match `cargo llvm-cov` under `cargo test`, counts can differ for once-per-process code. (5) Function staleness inherits ADR-083's holes and adds one: a change inside a function that alters what another function executes without the changed one running (code evaluated at compile time, a closure stored and called by a test that did not run the function). (6) The dry run lists suite entries per language without naming the language, as ADR-083's entries do. (7) The per-test map from a joint run counts every test as `measured`; `tests --impacted` after a joint run reports `(cached)` from the shared per-test entry.
- **P4.4 — for the integrator.** Review first: `crates/cupel-core/src/{joint.rs, schedule.rs (FactStore::make_jointly, keep, reached_by, deciding, ensure_wants; Scheduler::planned_needs, wants_planned), producer.rs (JointProducer, register_joint, joints_for, estimate's upstream)}`, `crates/cupel-adapt/src/{suite/, per_test/functions.rs, per_test/cache.rs, per_test/llvm_cov.rs (Instrumented, TARGET_DIR), runner.rs (finished)}`. `schedule.rs` edits are confined to the fact store's production path and dry-run planning; the parallel daemon branch's hooks should not collide except near `Scheduler::plan`'s `planned_needs` call. Files outside the stated area: `crates/cupel-adapt/src/cargo_nextest.rs` (`PROFILE` made `pub(crate)`), `crates/cupel-adapt/src/coverage/{mod.rs, go_coverprofile.rs}` (`module_paths` shared), `crates/cupel-adapt/src/lib.rs` (`mod suite;`), `crates/cupel-adapt/Cargo.toml` (`similar`), `Cargo.lock`, `crates/cupel-core/tests/producer_inputs.rs` (estimate signature). Measurement artifacts (the clone and its mutation slots) were removed.
- **P4.3 — model** ([ADR-086](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_086.md)). `cupel_core::budget` (pure): `TaskShape` (`check` × check × language × size class `0`/`1 + ⌊log₁₀ targets⌋`; `producer` × producer or joint × language × `run`/`cached`, from the producer's estimate note), `TaskDuration`, `History` (each shape's latest durations, newest first, and each check's `Novelty`), `p50` (lower median), `MIN_RUNS = 3`, `RECENT = 10`, `value(tier, novelty, changed) = (tier + 1) × {2 unscored, 1 scored, ½ repeated} × (changed ? 2 : 1)`, `tie(seed, check, lang)` (BLAKE3), `Budget { limit, deadline }`, `Resources { budget, jobs, history }` (a new `Job.resources`), and `choose(candidates, producer_costs, budget_ms)`, the greedy knapsack. `CostClass::est_ms_per_production` is the producer default (100 ms, 30 s, 5 min, 50 min; `cached` costs `quick`'s); a check's default stays its plan's `est_ms`. Durations are timed by the scheduler, exclusive of upstream work: each successful production (`FactStore::timed`, which `produce` and `make_jointly` both call) and each check run that ended `pass` or `fail` (`Execution::timed_run`); `Outcome.durations` → `Executions.durations` → `Store::record_durations` (store migration 4, table `durations`). The CLI reads `Store::recent_durations` (a window query, 10 per shape) and `Store::latest_scored` (latest non-skipped result per check with its run's `git_sha` and `$.run.git.dirty`) into `History` (`cupel-cli/src/resources.rs`); novelty is `Repeated` at the same commit with both trees clean, `Scored` otherwise, `Unscored` when the store names no result.
- **P4.3 — knapsack and the wall clock.** `cupel-core/src/schedule/budgeted.rs` (a child module of `schedule.rs`, so it sees the fact store's private parts): after `prepare`, `budgeted::schedule` costs every task (its own cost plus the producer tasks of its closure — `Deciding::reached` over its needs and affordable wants, per language and family sibling, minus what is made, each fact mapped to the joint run that would make it, else `Deciding::chosen`, now `pub(crate)` — memoized per (kind, language) and indexed so a shared producer is one knapsack item), runs `choose` with the budget left at that moment (`deadline − now`), reorders `Prepared.tasks` in take order with `Task.cost_ms` (the marginal cost), and moves the rest to `Prepared.skips` as `budget` skips with `est_ms`; `store.reached` is then recomputed over the taken tasks only, so the baseline (ADR-076) and joint decisions (ADR-084) see only what will run. Without a budget the same pass runs with no limit, in registry order, so every planned check's `est_ms` now includes its closure and `est_total_ms` and the `plan` event count each producer once. `Execution::start` puts the fact stores and every check context on `budgeted::cancellation(job)` — `job.cancel.child(deadline − now)` under a budget — so work running at the deadline is cut (processes killed, ADR-020 §5), and `Execution::over_budget` re-checks each task before it starts (own cost plus closure not yet made against what is left), skipping it with a `skip` event when it no longer fits. `--timeout` still skips with `timeout` first. A budget skip or cut sets `interrupted` (exit 5). Under a budget `run_checks` runs checks in take order (`Prepared::running_order`) and sorts results back to registry order. `Skipped.est_ms` (nullable, ADR-013) carries the estimate; a check every language skipped for budget sums them. The text renderer adds `est 30.0 s` to the skip. `--budget` is read from `config.effective.budget` (flag, `CUPEL_BUDGET`, profile, file) and counted from the invocation's start (`Run::deadline_after`). The registry no longer marks `--budget` pending.
- **P4.3 — concurrency.** `--jobs N` (`ScopeArgs.jobs: Option<NonZeroUsize>`, registry `Integer`, default `cores`): `resources::limit_threads` builds rayon's global pool with `N` threads before the walk; `Focus.jobs` reaches every producer: the mutation engine's slot count is `min(mutation.jobs, N)` (`cupel-mutate/src/engine.rs`, `Running::slots`), and the Rust per-test export uses `Focus::workers()` threads (`cupel-adapt/src/per_test/llvm_cov.rs`). No tokio runtime was added: the scheduler runs one task at a time, so permits would cap nothing; process-spawning concurrency is the engine's slots. Runners keep their own parallelism.
- **P4.3 — tests.** 1377 in `make check` (1343 at P4.4), all with every tool on `PATH`.
