(2) *README final-form per §2.7.* Opens with two sentences and the one-command quick start; shows captured output; every verb, check, flag, exit code, MCP tool, support cell, and the agent guide are generated from the binary (eight `generated:` sections, all held by `readme.rs`, which `make check` runs and which passed at every merge); the agent loop and exit-code table; Install, Usage, Configuration, For agents, Development, and License sections; links to both documents; no roadmap language (the Install table's availability column states facts about the routes, not promises).
- **P8 — v0.1.0, first attempt** (2026-10-19). The tag on `5d58dc1` built all four Unix archives, but the `release` job downloaded them into `dist`, which since P8.2 holds the installers, so `gh release create … dist/*` failed uploading `dist/homebrew` (a directory) after creating the release object. The release and the tag were deleted (nothing had consumed them), the job now stages under `target/dist` (where `make dist` writes), `release.rs` holds every line of the `release` job to never name a bare `dist` path (the test listed the six offending lines before the fix), and the tag was recreated on the fixed commit. The `secrets` and four `publish-*` jobs showed as skipped on the tag run, as ADR-128 says, since `release` had failed before them.
- **P8 — v0.1.0** (2026-10-19, tag on `ac3cf39`, release run 39336238952). `version`, the four Unix builds, `release`, and `secrets` green; the Windows build red without failing the run (ADR-124); `publish-npm`, `publish-pypi`, `publish-homebrew`, and `publish-crates` skipped, the `secrets` job logging `skipped: secret … not set` for each of `NPM_TOKEN`, `PYPI_API_TOKEN`, `HOMEBREW_TAP_TOKEN`, `CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN` (ADR-128). The release carries the four archives and `SHA256SUMS`. Verified against the published release: `dist/install.sh` from its raw URL installed and ran `cupel 0.1.0` in `ubuntu:24.04` (x86_64), in `alpine:3.20` (arm64, where `cupel init --dry-run` also ran), and on this Mac (arm64) into a scratch directory; `cargo binstall --git https://github.com/tysont/cupel cupel-cli` in `rust:1.92-bookworm` (x86_64) downloaded the musl archive from the release and the installed binary ran `cupel 0.1.0`, statically linked. The schemas the 0.1.0 envelopes name are served from GitHub Pages (ADR-127; run 39318866912 deployed them, and each sampled `$id` is served at its own URL). The tap repository `tysont/homebrew-cupel` exists and is empty until `HOMEBREW_TAP_TOKEN` is set.
- **P8 — open items** (carried from the sub-steps). Windows native build (ADR-124 Consequences); musl allocator speed under rayon; no build cache in the release workflow; the aarch64 Linux and x86_64 macOS binaries are not executed in CI; publishing steps and secrets for npm, PyPI, and the tap; the `cupel` package names on npm and PyPI are unverified; `cupel-cli` is not on crates.io, so binstall needs `--git`; `cupel.dev` is not set up; the wrappers' Windows paths are untested; a version bump edits three files; the agent guide's prose names flags and checks by hand, so a new flag the guide should mention is not caught; `#REQ-n` markers in test names as a secondary traceability source (P7 follow-up).
- **P8.4 — design** ([ADR-127](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_127.md)). `cupel.dev` has nameservers but no A record and serves nothing, and will not before 0.1.0, so `BASE_URL` (`crates/cupel-core/src/json_schema.rs`) is now `https://tysont.github.io/cupel/schema/v1/`, the GitHub Pages site of this repository (Pages enabled with `build_type: workflow`); the `/schema/v1/` path is kept so a later move to `cupel.dev` changes the origin alone. Every `$id`, `describe`'s schema URLs, and every envelope's `cupel.schema` change with it. `scripts/schemas.sh BINARY OUTDIR` (POSIX `sh` and `jq`, ShellCheck-clean) writes the site from the binary: the top-level schemas from `describe --all` (`schemas[].schema`), one per fact kind from `describe fact <kind>` for each kind in the `evidence.valid` of `describe fact`'s usage error (exit 2), each at the path its `$id` names under `https://tysont.github.io/cupel/` (35 files: 5 roots, 29 facts, and `index.html` linking them); a `$id` outside the site is exit 1 naming it. A script, not a `describe` option, so no flag reaches the registry, the README's flag tables, or the MCP tool schemas. `.github/workflows/pages.yml`: on push to `main` and `workflow_dispatch`, `build` (checkout, `rustup toolchain install`, `cargo build --locked --package cupel-cli`, the script into `_site`, `actions/upload-pages-artifact`), then `deploy` (`pages: write`, `id-token: write`, environment `github-pages`, `actions/deploy-pages`) only if `github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'`; `concurrency: pages` without cancelling; actions pinned by sha (`checkout` v7.0.1, `upload-pages-artifact` v5.0.0, `deploy-pages` v5.0.1). The README's sample envelope and a paragraph under the `describe` examples, and design §6.5 and §7.1, name the Pages base. ADRs naming `cupel.dev/schema` (ADR-087) are left as written; `cupel.dev/command` and `cupel.dev/event` are MCP `_meta` key names, not URLs, and are unchanged.
- **P8.4 — tests.** 2120 in all at `bae45f0` (+8 in this step): `cupel-cli/tests/pages.rs` 6 (the script on the built binary: every `$id` in `describe --all` and in `describe fact <kind>` for every `FactKind::all()` kind, 34 in all, is a file whose `$id` is the URL it is served at and whose `$schema` is draft 2020-12; the site is exactly those files and `index.html`; the five roots and each kind are under `schema/v1/`; the index starts `<!doctype html>` and links each file by its URL; a missing argument is exit 2 with the usage line; a stand-in binary emitting a foreign `$id` is exit 1 naming it), `release.rs` 2 (`pages.yml` uses exactly checkout, upload-pages-artifact, and deploy-pages, each pinned to a commit with its release; it triggers on `main`, and its deploy job has the `main`-only `if`, the `github-pages` environment, and both permissions); `release.rs`'s pin test now shares `actions`/`action_names` with the pages one. Goldens and snapshots: 254 files change, each only in the base URL (checked by applying the substitution to `main`'s copy and diffing: no other difference); 17 test sources that spelled the URL changed the same way.
- **P8.4 — dogfood.** `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exit 0 at `ca44e7f`: gate pass, `static.clones` 166 pairs (≤ 169) and 19 clusters (≤ 19), `static.complexity` 0 (cyclomatic max 10), `static.deps.cycles` 0, `static.dead-code` 0, `tests.quality` 0. The run at `4639c2d` failed `tests.quality` with 6 findings in the new tests (loops over assertions, `assert!(… .contains(…))`); they now compare whole sets and vectors with `assert_eq!`. Two earlier runs exited 4 on a red baseline that was load (load average 40–50 from parallel agents): the timing test `ten_thousand_symbols_are_searched_in_linear_work_and_under_a_second`, then `cupel-mutate`'s `a_mutant_that_never_finishes_times_out` (every mutant timed out); `.cupel/cache` and `.cupel/cupel.db` were moved aside so the red suite run was not reused. `make check` and `make parity` (103) green.
- **P8.4 — open items.** (1) Nothing is published until the merge reaches `main`; the integrator verifies after the push: `gh run list --workflow pages.yml --limit 1`, `gh run watch <id> --exit-status`, then `curl -sSf https://tysont.github.io/cupel/schema/v1/run.json | jq '.["$id"]'` (expect `"https://tysont.github.io/cupel/schema/v1/run.json"`), the same for `schema/v1/fact/symbols.json`, and `curl -sSf https://tysont.github.io/cupel/ | head` for the index. (2) The `github-pages` environment (`gh api repos/tysont/cupel/environments/github-pages`) has a custom deployment-branch policy allowing `main` alone, a second guard beside the job's `if`. (3) The schemas served are `main`'s tip, not a release's (ADR-127 Consequences). (4) `cupel.dev` remains unset; moving to it later is a `BASE_URL` origin change, the script's `site`, a golden regeneration, and a Pages custom domain so the `github.io` URLs redirect. (5) The script reads the fact kinds from a usage error's `evidence.valid`; `pages.rs` fails if that changes. (6) The pages build compiles without a cache (several minutes per push to `main`).
- **P8.4 — for the integrator.** New: `scripts/schemas.sh`, `.github/workflows/pages.yml`, `crates/cupel-cli/tests/pages.rs`, `docs/adr/CUPEL_DECISION_127.md`. Changed, owned by this step: `crates/cupel-core/src/json_schema.rs` (`BASE_URL`). URL only (`cupel.dev/schema/v1/` → `tysont.github.io/cupel/schema/v1/`): every golden under `crates/cupel-fixtures/golden/`, every `*.snap` that carried it, and the test sources `crates/cupel-cli/src/render/{fixture.rs, text/description.rs}`, `crates/cupel-cli/tests/{describe.rs, support/mod.rs}`, `crates/cupel-core/tests/{analyzer_report, contract, deltas, description, fact_catalog, footprint, gate, next_actions, property_results, schemas, suppressions}.rs`, `crates/cupel-fixtures/tests/redact.rs`, `crates/cupel-store/tests/store.rs`; any golden another branch adds before this merges still carries the old base, so after merging rerun the golden regeneration and check the diff is the URL alone (the substitution check in P8.4 — tests). Shared: `crates/cupel-cli/tests/release.rs` (`workflow(name)` beside `release_workflow()`, `actions`/`action_names` extracted from the release pin test, two pages tests and `setting` appended, the ABOUTME line); `README.md` (the sample envelope's `schema`; one paragraph after the `describe` examples); `docs/CUPEL_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` (§6.5 one sentence, §7.1 the sample envelope); this plan (the P8.4 sub-step and these notes). Not touched: `Makefile`, `ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, the README Install section.
- **P8.5 — design** ([ADR-128](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_128.md)). Publishing: a `secrets` job in `release.yml` (`needs: release`, tag pushes only) maps `NPM_TOKEN`, `PYPI_API_TOKEN`, `HOMEBREW_TAP_TOKEN`, and `CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN` into `env` and writes one boolean output per registry, with a `skipped: secret X not set` notice and step-summary line for each unset one; `publish-npm` (`npm publish ./dist/npm`; a bare `dist/npm` is read as a GitHub repository), `publish-pypi` (venv, `build` 1.6.1, `twine` 7.0.0), `publish-homebrew` (`SHA256SUMS` from the published release, `render.sh`, commit `Formula/cupel.rb` to `tysont/homebrew-cupel` `main`), and `publish-crates` (`cargo publish --workspace --locked`, which orders the crates and leaves out `cupel-fixtures`) each `needs: secrets` with a job-level `if` on its output. No new actions. Routes: `crates/cupel-cli/src/routes.rs`, a const table (name, command, platforms, `pending: Option`) of nine routes, live first: install script, cargo-binstall `--git`, release archive (`gh release download`), source (`cargo install --locked --git`); then Homebrew, npm, pipx, crates.io, cupel.dev, each with what it waits for. `CliDescription.install_routes` (`InstallRoute { name, command, platforms, live, pending, stability }`) carries them into `describe` and `describe --all` and an `install routes` text section; `doctor.version-unsatisfied`'s fix (the `install_tool` summary, also every grading verb's refusal) ends "Live install routes: `…`; …". The README's route table is the generated section `install-routes`, the ninth `readme.rs` renders.
- **P8.5 — tests.** 2119 in all at `eda02c4` (+7 over its base `a8e4ae2`): `release.rs` 3 (the `secrets` job runs on a tag after `release`, maps, probes, and notices each secret; every publish job needs `secrets`, is gated on its output, and reads its secret; no other job reads a secret), `describe.rs` 3 (four live and five pending routes, each pending reason starting "once "; every command one trimmed line run by `curl`, `cargo`, `gh`, `brew`, `npx`, or `pipx`, for platforms `dist/targets.toml` lists; every URL in a command is the repository, a file in it on `main`, or `cupel.dev`), `doctor.rs` 1 (the finding names every live route's command and no pending one). Changed: `doctor.rs`'s exact-summary test, `required_version.rs` (the refusal's summary equals doctor's), the text renderer's `describe --all` heading list. Goldens and snapshots regenerated, all from the new field or fix text: `golden/cli/describe.json`, `golden/doctor/required-version.json`, `describe__describe_without_a_subject_describes_the_cli.snap`, `format__format_text_renders_a_status_line_instead_of_json.snap`, the text renderer's CLI snapshot, `schemas__run_schema.snap`; the round-trip fixtures in `cupel-core/tests/description.rs` and `cupel-fixtures/tests/redact.rs` gain `install_routes`. `actionlint` 1.7.12 (with ShellCheck) reports nothing on `release.yml`.
- **P8.5 — dogfood.** `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exit 0 at `eda02c4`: gate pass, `static.clones` 166 pairs (≤ 169) and 19 clusters (≤ 19), `static.complexity` 0 findings (cyclomatic max 10), `static.deps.cycles` 0, `static.dead-code` 0, `tests.quality` 0. The first run (at `83e6e34`) failed on one `function.long`: `render_cli` had grown to 76 lines with the install-routes section; that section and the schema section are now functions of their own.
- **P8.5 — verified here and in CI only.** Here: `npm publish ./dist/npm --dry-run` packs `cupel@0.1.0` (two files); `python -m build dist/pipx` builds `cupel-0.1.0` sdist and wheel and `twine check` passes both (warnings: no long description); `cargo publish --workspace --dry-run --no-verify` orders `cupel-core`, `cupel-adapt`, `cupel-checks`, `cupel-lang`, `cupel-plugin`, `cupel-serve`, `cupel-store`, `cupel-mutate`, `cupel-cli` once the path dependencies carry versions (tried, not kept). Only in CI: none of the five new jobs has run. After the `v0.1.0` tag, `gh run view <run>` should show `secrets` green with four `skipped: secret … not set` notices, the four `publish-*` jobs skipped, and the run successful; a `workflow_dispatch` run skips all five.
- **P8.5 — open items.** (1) PyPI's `cupel` is another project's (0.1.88, `tolitius/cupel`): `publish-pypi` would be refused and `pipx install cupel` installs that project today (the route's reason says so); the distribution needs another name. (2) crates.io's `cupel-core` (and `cupel`) are other projects': `cargo package --workspace` here resolved `cupel-core 0.1.0` from crates.io and failed to verify `cupel-adapt` against it, so `publish-crates` fails at its first crate until the crates are renamed; it also needs a `version` on each `[workspace.dependencies]` path entry, left out until then. (3) npm's `cupel` is free. (4) Flipping a route live is a `routes.rs` change, `make readme`, and a release. (5) Between this merge and the `v0.1.0` tag, the four live routes are described as live slightly early. (6) The `publish-homebrew` push to an empty tap creates `main`; not exercised against a real repository here.
- **P8.5 — for the integrator.** New: `crates/cupel-cli/src/routes.rs`, `docs/adr/CUPEL_DECISION_128.md`. Changed, owned here: `crates/cupel-cli/src/{doctor.rs (version fix), lib.rs (mod routes), registry.rs (install_routes), render/text/description.rs (install routes and schema sections in their own functions)}`, `crates/cupel-core/src/{describe.rs (InstallRoute, CliDescription.install_routes), lib.rs (re-export)}`, `crates/cupel-cli/tests/{describe.rs, doctor.rs, required_version.rs}`, `crates/cupel-core/tests/description.rs`, `crates/cupel-fixtures/tests/redact.rs`, and the goldens and snapshots listed above (if another branch changes the run schema or the CLI description, rerun `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 INSTA_UPDATE=always cargo test` rather than hand-merging them). Shared: `.github/workflows/release.yml` (ABOUTME line 2, and five jobs appended after `release`), `crates/cupel-cli/tests/release.rs` (three tests appended), `crates/cupel-cli/tests/readme.rs` (the `install-routes` section; the array is now 9), `README.md` (Install: the paragraph before the table rewritten and the hand-written table replaced by the `install-routes` markers; one clause in the `required_version` paragraph; run `make readme` after merging), this plan (the P8.5 heading and these notes). Not touched: `Makefile`, `ci.yml`, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, the design doc, `json_schema.rs`.

### P9 — Dogfood to completion

**Goal:** Cupel grades its own codebase at full depth in CI and stays green while the follow-up list is burned down; Cupel grades other people's code and every finding an experienced maintainer would dispute is fixed as a check bug with a fixture case.

**Status:** complete (2026-10-28, c129fa3; tag `v0.2.0` on 2a811d0; P9 Notes "P9 — Definition of Done")

The owner's P9 ruling (2026-10-19) defines three tracks. Track (b), the deep gate, comes first so tracks (a) and (c) keep it green. Track (a) is the follow-up burn-down in the order below. Track (c) is external dogfood. The original P9 sub-steps fold in: the deep survey and acting on its findings into P9.0, raising the gate into P9.0 and P9.1, the performance baseline into P9.0's deep rules, and the agent instructions into P9.0 (the README's generated "For agents" section is the binary's own guide; P9.0 decides whether the repository needs a second copy).

#### P9.0 Cupel's own gate at `--depth deep` in CI

**Status:** complete (2026-10-20, 6c254ce; [ADR-129](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_129.md))

- Track (b). Measure a deep run on the tree and on a one-function change; put the changed gate at `--depth deep` on every push and the tree at `--depth deep` on `main` (per push, or scheduled with `--budget`, whichever the measurements justify); extend `[gate.changed]` and `[gate.tree]` with the deep checks' rules at the strictest values the tree sustains (`tests.mutation.score` floor 0.80); fix what the deep run finds; record the deep `survey` rankings verbatim as the baseline.

#### P9.1 Relative gate rules

**Status:** complete (2026-10-20, 06c527b; [ADR-130](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_130.md))

- Track (a) item 1. `"<= base"`, `">= base - 0.02"`, and the other comparisons against the reference value (baseline or base ref); a rule with no reference is skipped with a reason. Cupel's clone ratchets become `<= base`.

#### P9.2 Similarity-based symbol matching across refs

**Status:** complete (2026-10-20, 63357e9; [ADR-131](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_131.md))

- Track (a) item 2. Symbols matched across refs by `NormTree` similarity, one-to-many and many-to-one, with set-level deltas: a renamed, moved, split, or merged symbol no longer reads as resolved plus new, and a metric compared across a split is compared against the set.

#### P9.3 Protect-first ordering in `survey`

**Status:** complete (2026-10-20, c410db5; [ADR-132](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_132.md))

- Track (a) item 3. Each target paired with its mutation score; unprotected targets ranked for protection before simplification, and `survey`'s `next_actions` ordered the same way.

#### P9.4 `explain` hints

**Status:** complete (2026-10-20, 31ba5e0; [ADR-133](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_133.md))

- Track (a) item 4. `clone.cluster` → the common-subtree extraction candidate; `complexity.high` → the deepest nested block; `dep.cycle` → the cheapest edge to cut.

#### P9.5 `#REQ-n` markers as a secondary traceability source

**Status:** complete (2026-10-20, 49126e5; [ADR-134](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_134.md))

- Track (a) item 5. Configured requirement IDs stay primary; `REQ-<n>` markers in test names are the secondary source (ADR-114 amended).

#### P9.6 `References` fact and real dead-symbol detection

**Status:** complete (2026-10-20, eb9d38b; [ADR-135](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_135.md))

- Track (a) item 6. A `References` fact for every language; `static.dead-code` decides reachability per symbol (ADR-058 superseded).

#### P9.7 Clone type 1 vs type 2 in evidence

**Status:** complete (2026-10-21, b3b9ec2; [ADR-136](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_136.md))

- Track (a) item 7. A raw-token digest on `NormTree`; `clone_type` 1, 2, or 3 with the evidence saying what differs (ADR-050 amended).

#### P9.8 Windows `serve` over named pipes

**Status:** complete (2026-10-21, 457d8f3; [ADR-137](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_137.md))

- Track (a) item 8. The daemon's transport abstracted with a named-pipe implementation on Windows, the workspace compiling for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, and the Windows release job no longer best-effort.

#### P9.9 External dogfood

**Status:** complete (2026-10-27, 71a45b4; [ADR-138](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_138.md))

- Track (c). Two mid-size open-source repositories per native language (Rust, Python, TypeScript) and one per adapter language (Go, Java, Clojure): `survey` and `check --depth deep` on each, every finding triaged; a false positive is a check bug fixed with a fixture case; per-repository reports in `docs/dogfood/` with the false-positive rate per check.

#### P9.F1 Follow-up fixes: the mutation score, the suite cache, the mutation slots, aliased imports

**Status:** complete (2026-10-21, 18ce8c8; [ADR-141](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_141.md))

- Track (a), follow-up items 16, 17, 19, and 20 ([brief](plan/p9/p9.f1-engine-fixes.md)).
#### P9.F2 Follow-up fixes: the changed gate's scope, the release cache, the agent guide's flags, one version, the deferrals

**Status:** complete (2026-10-22, fce5f32; [ADR-142](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_142.md), [ADR-143](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_143.md))

- Track (a), follow-up items 10, 12, 13, and 18, and the deferrals of items 9, 11, 14, 15 and the P9.4–P9.7 open items ([brief](plan/p9/p9.f2-gate-and-release-fixes.md)).

#### P9.F3 Follow-up fix: `dead.export` under a range reads the whole language's import edges

**Status:** complete (2026-10-22, b2e8399; [ADR-144](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_144.md))

- Track (a), follow-up item 21 ([brief](plan/p9/p9.f3-dead-export-range.md)).

#### P9.F4 Follow-up fix: Cupel's own test suite is deterministic under load

**Status:** complete (2026-10-24, 2d6f1af; [ADR-145](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_145.md))

- The timing-sensitive tests of Cupel's own suite wait on events, bound work, and warm fresh executables, so their outcome no longer depends on the machine's load ([brief](plan/p9/p9.f4-flakes.md)).

#### P9.F5 Follow-up fix: the package is `cupel-cli` on every registry

**Status:** complete (2026-10-24, 3a7781b; [ADR-146](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_146.md))

- The owner's package-name ruling: `cupel-cli` on PyPI, npm, and crates.io, the command `cupel` ([brief](plan/p9/p9.f5-package-names.md)).
#### P9.F6 Follow-up fix: a hard cap on a mutant's run, and tests no runner can narrow

**Status:** complete (2026-10-24, c228134; [ADR-147](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_147.md))

- A mutant's run is capped by `max_mutant_timeout` (default 120 s) under `[checks."tests.mutation"]`, and a test slower than 60 s in the baseline is left out of every whole-suite mutant run with a `mutant.slow-test-excluded` note ([brief](plan/p9/p9.f6-mutant-timeout.md)).

#### P9.F7 Follow-up fix: a memory budget for mutant runs, and the slots' share of the cores

**Status:** complete (2026-10-25, edcebb3; [ADR-148](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_148.md))

- After the machine panicked under serde_json's mutants (2026-10-24, the compressor full and five swap files in use): every run of a production gets `max(1, cores / slots)` as the runner's parallelism (cargo nextest `--test-threads` and `--build-jobs`, cargo test `--test-threads` and `--jobs`), and an equal part of `max_mutant_memory` (default 50% of physical memory, under `[checks."tests.mutation"]`) as a budget on its process group, sampled through `ps`; a run over it is killed and its mutant is `out-of-memory`, reported as `mutant.out-of-memory` and left out of the score.

#### P9.10 Release v0.2.0

**Status:** complete (2026-10-28, tag `v0.2.0` on 2a811d0; [ADR-146](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_146.md) defers crates.io)

- The follow-up list is empty or every remaining item has a deferring ADR; `v0.2.0` tagged and the release verified as `v0.1.0` was.

#### P9 Definition of Done

- The follow-up list is empty, or every remaining item has an ADR deferring it with a reason.
- Cupel's own gate at `--depth deep` is green in CI.
- An external dogfood report exists for every language, with the false-positive rate per check.
- `v0.2.0` is tagged.

#### P9 Notes

- **P9 — recovery** (2026-10-19). The machine crashed with four P9 agents mid-step (P9.0–P9.3), each waiting on a dogfood behind a load average above 200: every agent had started its own tree dogfood at once, and eight shells of an already-merged P8 agent were still looping `make dogfood` in a deleted worktree. The scratch directory that held every tool the goldens depend on was lost with it. The owner's recovery ruling: (1) `make dev-setup` installs the pinned tool environment from the repository (ADR-139) before any agent restarts; the agent briefs live in `docs/plan/p9/` (one per step, `common.md` for the rules they share, recovery briefs for the four branches written from their commits, not from memory); `make dogfood` runs through `scripts/dogfood.sh`, which holds a machine-wide lock (`/tmp/cupel-dogfood.lock`, `CUPEL_DOGFOOD_LOCK` to move it) so one dogfood runs at a time across every checkout, and refuses to start from a working tree deleted under its shell; agents wait on the lock and never poll the load; the mutation engine's slots already default to half the cores, at most four. (2) The crashed agents are not resumed: fresh agents take each branch from its brief. (3) P9.0's scoping: deep on `--changed` for branch and pull-request CI, deep on the tree only on a push to `main`, local dogfood under `--budget` (ADR-129). (4) P9.0–P9.3 merge serially with the full procedure, then P9.4–P9.8 with at most two agents at once.
- **P9 — rulings for finishing** (2026-10-23). After a second session loss killed the two P9.9 agents and an F2 dogfood rerun, the owner ruled the order of the rest of P9: (1) Cupel's own test suite is made deterministic under load first (P9.F4, ADR-145: injected clocks, event-based waits, or baselines measured in-process; no retry and no quarantine; what cannot be made deterministic in an hour moves to a `make check-slow` target outside the merge gate, with the ADR saying why), merged before anything else runs. (2) The dogfood lock covers every heavy command on the machine: `scripts/dogfood.sh` became `scripts/heavy.sh` (`/tmp/cupel-heavy.lock`, `CUPEL_HEAVY_LOCK`), `make dogfood` and every external test-suite run of the P9.9 agents go through it, one at a time. (3) P9.F2 then P9.F3: tree dogfood under the lock, fast-forward, push, CI. (4) The P9.9 agents are not resumed; fresh agents take each branch with the exact remaining list (group 1: the two Rust repositories; group 2: the two Python and the Java); group 2's branch contains group 1's, so group 1 merges first; group 3 is cut to one repository per remaining language (TypeScript, Clojure); at most two group agents at once. (5) Groups merge serially with the full procedure, every false-positive fix with a fixture case, goldens additive. (6) The package name is `cupel-cli` on PyPI, npm, and crates.io; `dist/targets.toml`, the wrappers, the binstall metadata, and the README say so; publish jobs run when the secrets exist and skip cleanly otherwise. (7) P9.10: README examples recaptured from the binary, `make version V=0.2.0`, tag `v0.2.0`, the release verified as v0.1.0 was (the aarch64 Linux and x86_64 macOS binaries by hand per ADR-143, the release cache timing). (8) The final P9 report includes the external dogfood's false-positive rate per check per language.
- **P9 — rulings of 2026-10-24.** After group 1's full-tree deep run on serde_json held the machine five hours (each mutant that made a parser loop forever ran its integration binary to `timeout_factor × baseline`, about nine minutes): (1) the mutant timeout is a Cupel defect, fixed before group 1's next deep run (P9.F6, ADR-147): `max_mutant_timeout` (default 120 s) under `[checks."tests.mutation"]` caps a mutant's run above `timeout_factor`; a test no runner can narrow (trybuild, an integration binary, a runner that cannot run one test) whose baseline exceeds 60 s is left out of mutant runs with an `info` finding `mutant.slow-test-excluded`; which tests each runner can narrow is documented. (2) `make check` no longer takes the heavy lock (the flake fix made it load-safe); only tree dogfoods and external suite runs take it; the lock is a priority queue with the integrator's merge chains ahead of the group agents, who release it between repositories. (3) External dogfood runs `survey --depth standard` under a 30-minute budget, then `check --depth deep --paths` on the survey's top ten hot spots; no full-tree deep mutation on an external repository. (4) crates.io is not published for v0.2.0 (ADR-146 records the deferral; if revisited, the library crates take the `cupel-cli-` prefix); `publish-crates` stays a clean skip. (5) The sequence is unchanged: P9.F5, then groups 1 → 2 → 3 with the full procedure, then P9.10 and the final report.
- **P9 — disk ruling** (2026-10-24). With 13 GB free of 926 (five worktrees at 16 to 50 GB each, of which the coverage build was 14 to 18 GB and the incremental caches 5 to 14 GB; one agent's scratch held a second 20 GB `target`), the owner ruled that the processes clean up Rust artifacts: `make reclaim` removes the coverage build, the incremental caches, and the suite cache of a checkout; agents run it after every dogfood and before reporting, build into no second `target`, and delete each external clone's build outputs once its report is written; the integrator removes a worktree as soon as its branch is merged and reclaims idle ones; `scripts/heavy.sh` refuses to start a heavy command below 40 GB free (`CUPEL_HEAVY_MIN_FREE_GB`).
- **P9 — v0.2.0** (2026-10-28, tag on `2a811d0`, release run 40606071936). `version`, the five builds (Windows included since ADR-137), `release`, and `secrets` green; `publish-npm`, `publish-pypi`, `publish-homebrew`, and `publish-crates` skipped, the `secrets` job logging `skipped: secret … not set` for each of `NPM_TOKEN`, `PYPI_API_TOKEN`, `HOMEBREW_TAP_TOKEN`, `CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN` (ADR-128; crates.io deferred by ADR-146). The release carries the four Unix archives, the Windows zip, and `SHA256SUMS`. Verified against the published release: every checksum matches and `release.sh verify` accepts all five archives; `dist/install.sh` from its raw URL installed and ran `cupel 0.2.0` in `ubuntu:24.04` (x86_64), in `alpine:3.20` (arm64, the aarch64 Linux binary, where `cupel init --dry-run` also ran), and on this Mac (arm64) into a scratch directory; the x86_64 macOS binary ran `cupel --version` under Rosetta on this Mac (ADR-143's two by-hand checks); `cargo binstall --git https://github.com/tysont/cupel cupel-cli` in `rust:1.92-bookworm` (x86_64) installed a statically linked `cupel 0.2.0`. The release cache (P9.F2 open item 4): a `workflow_dispatch` dry run on `2a811d0` (run 40596917228) found no cache and built in 3 m 38 s (x86_64 musl), 4 m 23 s (aarch64 musl), 4 m 26 s (x86_64 macOS), 3 m 57 s (aarch64 macOS), 5 m 08 s (Windows); the tag run restored that cache for the macOS and Windows targets and built in 2 m 14 s, 2 m 58 s, and 4 m 39 s, but found no cache for either Linux target (3 m 37 s and 4 m 23 s), an open item. The README's check example was recaptured from the 0.2.0 release binary on the shop repository; at `--depth standard` every check now runs, so the example shows the whole check table and the mypy, `gen.property`, `static.api-surface`, and `tests.mutation` findings a standard run adds. Seen on the way: `tests.coverage` reports `lines 0` and passes for a changed file no test imports, because coverage.py's lcov has no row for a file never loaded (`cupel init`'s pytest command passes `--cov` without a source), while `tests.mutation` reports the same file's 25 mutants uncovered; the ubuntu `check` job of CI run 40596861632 failed once in `cupel-core/tests/process.rs` (`a_descendant_in_a_process_group_of_its_own_counts_against_the_memory_budget`: the detached child's pid file never appeared within 600 s), passed on rerun, and did not reproduce in 25 runs on this Mac (nextest and `cargo test`) nor 20 runs of the binary in `rust:1.92-bookworm`; and the two network-dependent baseline tests (`fix_with_install_installs_the_runner_into_a_scratch_venv_and_passes`, `each_wrapper_refuses_a_bad_checksum_downloads_once_and_forwards_the_exit_code`) fail a tree dogfood when the machine is offline or on battery, as during the 2026-10-25 DNS outage and again on 2026-10-27, and pass when rerun.
- **P9 — Definition of Done** (2026-10-28, against `main` at 2a811d0). (1) *Follow-up list:* items 1–8 closed by P9.1–P9.8 (ADR-130–137), 10, 12, 13, 16–21 closed by P9.F1–P9.F3, items 9, 11, 14, 15 deferred by ADR-143, crates.io by ADR-146. (2) *Cupel's own gate at `--depth deep` green in CI:* the `dogfood` job passed on every run of P9.9's merges and on 2a811d0 (run 40596861632). (3) *An external dogfood report for every language with the false-positive rate per check:* Rust (serde_json, itertools), Python (pyflakes, more-itertools), TypeScript (ts-pattern), Go (cobra), Java (commons-cli), Clojure (cljfx), in `docs/dogfood/` and ADR-138 §8–§10. (4) *`v0.2.0` tagged:* above.

the Homebrew tap token; `cupel.dev`.
- **P9 — development environment** ([ADR-139](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_139.md)). `make dev-setup` runs `scripts/dev-setup.sh`, which installs or verifies, under `${CUPEL_DEV_TOOLS:-~/.cache/cupel/dev-tools}`, every tool `ci.yml`'s `check`, `dogfood`, and `installers` jobs install at their pins, zig 0.16.0 and cargo-zigbuild 0.23.4 and the release targets for `make dist`, and actionlint 1.7.12; each tool goes into a directory named for its version and a stable link is switched to it once complete, so agents running tests are never handed half an install, and a second run installs nothing. `scripts/dev-env.sh` is the one source of the tools' `PATH`, `JAVA_HOME`, `MAVEN_ARGS`, `GRADLE_USER_HOME`, `CLJ_CONFIG`, `CLJ_CACHE`, and `KANI_HOME`; the Makefile exports them for every target (nothing without the directory, so CI is unaffected) and `make dev-env` prints them. Pins added to `ci.yml`: `cargo-nextest@0.9.148`, `cargo-llvm-cov@0.9.1`, `pytest==9.1.1`, `pytest-cov==7.1.0`, `vitest@5.0.5`, `@vitest/coverage-v8@5.0.5`, `jest@30.5.2`, Node `22.23.3`, Python `3.12.10` (the newest 3.12 `setup-python` has for macOS). `crates/cupel-cli/tests/dev_setup.rs` fails while a version pinned in `ci.yml` or `release.yml`'s build job is missing from the script, or `ci.yml` installs a package unpinned. JVM network finding: not the network but LuLu, the owner's per-application firewall, which allows Homebrew's `openjdk@21` 21.0.12.1 java by path and held back a freshly downloaded Temurin java ("No route to host" from Maven); the script links that keg when it is exactly the pinned release, and no proxy is needed. The rustup `nightly` here was the 2026-03-27 nightly, not CI's pin; it is now the link to `nightly-2026-03-26` that `ci.yml` makes. Verification: `make dev-setup` twice (the second installed nothing), `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 INSTA_UPDATE=always make test` left `git status --porcelain` empty, `make check` and `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` green; the only skipped real-tool tests were atheris (Python `gen.fuzz`, as in CI) and ThreadSanitizer, whose runtime crashes on this macOS (ADR-105).
- **P9.0 — design** ([ADR-129](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_129.md)). The owner's scoping: CI's `dogfood` job runs `make dogfood DOGFOOD_BUDGET=` (`check --changed --depth deep --gate`, mutating every function of every changed file) on every branch push and pull request, and `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree DOGFOOD_BUDGET=` (`--depth deep` on the tree without `tests.mutation`) on each push to `main`; a local `make dogfood` adds `--budget 30m` (`DOGFOOD_BUDGET`), so it ends in bounded time, lists what the budget left out, and exits 5 when it left anything out. Both skip `tests.flaky` (its engine does not rerun cargo-nextest, so the check would end in `error`) and `conc.race` (Miri cannot run Cupel's process-spawning tests). The job installs cargo-nextest 0.9.148 and cargo-llvm-cov 0.9.1 with `llvm-tools-preview`, frees the runner's disk, builds without debug info, and has 90 minutes. Gate rules added: both gates `static.analyze` `none`, `static.crap.max <= 20`, `gen.property.falsified == 0`, `spec.contracts` `none`; the tree `tests.coverage.line >= 0.96`; the changed gate `tests.coverage` `none` and `tests.mutation.score >= 0.80`. No rule on `gen.fuzz` (no target, and no cargo-fuzz in the dogfood job, so a rule would be skipped and fail CI's gate) or `perf.bench` (no benchmark). Fixed from the deep run: the 112 `static.analyze` findings in the three Python scripts Cupel ships (the PIT plugin, the metamorphic harness, the Hypothesis plugin template, now valid Python before the case budget is written in; the harness's four catch-alls of the project's own exceptions carry `# noqa: BLE001`); `metrics::weighted_mean` and `outcome::weighted` scored a scope with nothing to weigh 0, so `tests.mutation` reported 0 for a change with no mutant though each language reports 1, and now keep the languages' own values; two surviving mutants in `outcome.rs` now killed. The matrix test is a `libtest-mimic` harness, one golden-or-skip and one declared-facts trial per (check, language), so a mutant reruns only the pairs that execute it. No second agent guide: the README's generated "For agents" section is the one copy.
- **P9.0 — measurements** (2026-10-20, the owner's 8-core Mac, `make dev-setup`'s tools, through the dogfood lock; not idle: three other agents' suites kept the load average at 40–75 during the tree run and 16–132 during the one-function run). Tree, `--depth deep` without mutation, 567 files and 6 953 symbols: 2 170 s, exit 0; the instrumented suite run for the coverage map 1 219 s, the property engine 889 s, clippy 36 s, mypy and ruff 1.3 s, the language producers 15 s; checks `static.crap` 2.3 s, `static.clones` 2.2 s, `tests.coverage` 1.6 s, every other under 1 s. One edited function (`metrics::symbol_coverage`, `--base HEAD`, its file's 12 symbols): 2 307 s, exit 0; the mutation engine 1 395 s for 12 mutants (all schemata, all killed), the per-test coverage run 875 s, clippy 12 s, criterion 1.3 s. The tree's values: line coverage 0.968 (45 uncovered symbols), CRAP max 20 and mean 2.14, 20 properties none falsified, 167 clone pairs in 19 clusters, cyclomatic max 10, 175 sparse tests and 31 sparse files.

- **P9.0 — open items.** (1) CI's deep runs are unmeasured on GitHub's runners; the 90-minute limit rests on the local numbers. (2) The 45 uncovered symbols (several reachable only through a terminal prompt or an uninstalled tool such as atheris), held by the tree's line rule and failed by the changed gate's `none` whenever a change touches one. (3) No fuzz target, no benchmark, no contract library: `gen.fuzz`, `perf.bench`, and `spec.contracts` measure nothing on Cupel. (4) 175 sparse tests and 31 sparse files (`tests.assertion-density`), with no gate rule. (5) `outcome::plan_symbols` estimates 0 targets for a scope whose `symbols` is `None` (every symbol in its files); the scheduler always lists the symbols, so the branch is not reached and its `0 → 1` mutant survives. (6) The one unreproduced `tests.mutation` overlay baseline failure above. (7) `scripts/dogfood.sh` keeps the lock on a descriptor every child inherits, so a dogfood's long-lived descendant would hold the lock after the dogfood ends; none was seen. At the merge (2026-10-20): the first dogfood job on GitHub (run 39463677925) failed its baseline with 26 tests red, since the job installed only cargo-nextest and cargo-llvm-cov while the suite drives every tool; the `check` job's installs moved into the composite action `.github/actions/tools`, which both jobs use, and `dev_setup.rs` reads the action's pins as it read ci.yml's. The second run (39473943742) failed one baseline test, `thread_sanitizer_reports_the_race_of_the_fixture_where_its_runtime_runs`: the job's `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0` reached the fixture's sanitized build, whose race frames then had no file or line; `tool.tsan` now sets `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=line-tables-only` for its own build (in the cache, so nothing of the project's), since a race without a place is no report (`tsan.rs`, with a unit test; `Process::environment` exposes a child's variables).

No snapshot or schema changed. - **P9.1 — design** ([ADR-130](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_130.md)). Grammar: after `>= > <= < ==` the operand is a number (optionally `%`) or `base`, optionally `base + k`/`base - k` with `k` a non-negative finite number in the metric's unit; `GateCondition::Relative { op, offset }` (`cupel-core/src/config/gate.rs`) displays as `<= base`, `>= base - 0.02` and parses back to itself; errors name the fix (`base - 2%` → "write `base - 0.02`"); the config schema's pattern and description carry the form. Reference (`cupel-cli/src/reference.rs`, resolved only when the evaluated table has a `base` rule and the envelope is graded): `--against` (ADR-046's resolution, `against::compare` now returns the run it compared with); else a range scope recomputes its base: the scope resolved again, the changed files that existed at the base (ADR-056) as `--paths`, the `base` rules' checks as `--only`, run by `check::run` in a copy of the base commit that `Git::export` writes through a private `GIT_INDEX_FILE` into a temporary directory outside the repository (inside `.cupel/` the repository's ignore files hide every file); else the `default` baseline; else `no baseline is set`. `evaluate_gate` takes a `GateReference` (`Run(&Envelope)` or `Missing(why)`); a rule with no reference value is `skipped` with `actual` set and reason `no reference: <why>`, and fails the gate. `summary.gate.rules[]` gains `bound` (threshold, reference value plus offset, or 0 for presence rules) and `reason` (every skipped rule now says why); the text renderer prints the reason and, for a `base` rule, the bound; a no-reference rule's next action is `configure` with `cupel baseline set --write`. ADR-046's status line records that §5's "nothing is ever re-run" is narrowed for `base` rules on a range.
- **P9.1 — tests.** 2139 in all at `1e33f53` (+12): `cupel-core/tests/config_gate.rs` +4 (every comparison against `base` parses; display round trip; eight malformed forms with their exact reasons; a `base` rule needs a metric), `cupel-core/tests/gate.rs` +3 (bound and reason on evaluated and skipped rules; `base` plus offset for all five operators; no reference from an unset baseline and from a reference run lacking the metric, failing the gate), `cupel-core/tests/git.rs` +1 (`export` writes a commit's files from a subdirectory root and leaves the index, work tree, and its temporary index alone), `cupel-cli/tests/gate.rs` +4 on the real binary (a stored baseline with `[gate.tree] "static.clones.pairs" = "<= base"`: skipped with the configure action, then 1 against bound 1 passes, then 3 against 1 fails; `--changed` with `params_max <= base` and `cyclomatic_max >= base - 0.5` recomputed at the base commit, and `--against run:ID` taking a stored run instead; a range of only new files has no reference; the Python mutation fixture with `"tests.mutation.score" = ">= base - 0.02"`: a change to `refund` covered only by a test that pins no value scores 0.0 against bound 0.73 (base 0.75, recomputed by rerunning the suite and mutants at the base) and fails, and pinning the value scores 1.0 and passes; at the merge with P9.0 this case was rewritten from an uncovered change, which since ADR-129 §5 scores 1 (follow-up item 16)). Changed: `exit_codes.rs` and `gate.rs`'s pinned rule objects gain `bound`/`reason`. Snapshots regenerated, each from the two fields, the reason in text, the schema pattern, or the agent guide's sentence: `schemas__run_schema.snap`, `schemas__config_schema.snap`, the four render snapshots and the SARIF one in `cupel-cli/src/`, `agent__the_guide_is_about_sixty_lines_of_markdown.snap`.
- **P9.1 — dogfood.** The gate's checks on the tree (`check --gate --only static.complexity static.clones static.deps static.dead-code tests.quality`) exit 0 at `75c849e`: complexity 0 findings (cyclomatic max 10), clones 166 pairs and 18 clusters, deps cycles 0, dead-code 0, tests.quality 0. The first run found three complexity findings in this step's code (`gate::evaluate_rule` 66 lines and cognitive 17, `reference::run_at` six parameters), fixed by evaluating comparison and presence rules in their own functions and building the base run's flags apart. The base ratchet on Cupel itself: after `cupel baseline set --write`, the same run with a copy of `cupel.toml` whose clone rules read `<= base` passes with `static.clones.pairs <= base actual 166 bound 166` and `clusters … actual 18 bound 18`. The full `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` at `8537f0f` exited 4 on a red baseline: `cupel-checks`' timing test `ten_thousand_symbols_are_searched_in_linear_work_and_under_a_second` (23.6 s under load average 90–200 from the parallel agents; 15.6 s alone, passing); `make check` stopped on the same test and `make parity` on a cold-versus-daemon difference in `conc.model-check` where the cold run could not open `.cupel/cupel.db` (store contention, no gate involved). The recovery run, at `06c527b` (this branch with `main` at `17cd3cb` merged), one dogfood at a time under the lock: `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` exits 0 (gate.tree pass: complexity 0 findings, cyclomatic max 10, clones 163 pairs and 17 clusters under the 169/19 ratchets, deps cycles 0, dead-code 0, tests.quality 0). `make dogfood` (changed, against local `main` at `17cd3cb`) exits 1 only on `tests.weakening`'s eight `snapshot.rewritten`, the eight snapshots listed under tests, each a deliberate contract change of this step (the `bound`/`reason` fields, the reason in text, the schema pattern and description, the agent guide's sentence; the only removed lines are those lines' old forms), as P4.3, P5.1, and P5.3 recorded for theirs; it passes once the step is on `main`. Every other changed rule passes (complexity 0 findings, cyclomatic max 8, clones 0, deps 0, dead-code 0, tests.quality 0). Its first run at the merge found two clone pairs, fixed in `06c527b`: `a_rule_against_the_reference_value_needs_a_metric` shaped like `error_message_quotes_the_rule_and_lists_the_operators` (both now assert through `assert_parse_error`), and in `exit_codes.rs`, which this step touched, the pre-existing `exit_2_for_a_misspelled_flag_suggests_the_right_one`/`exit_2_for_conflicting_flags_names_both` (both now through `usage_action`). `make check` green at the merge `f47bf4f` and at `4fd8972` (2151 tests, the timing test passing); one attempt between them, under load average 80 from the other agents, failed only that timing test (28.8 s). `make parity` green at `06c527b` with the machine shared but no dogfood running: the `conc.model-check` cold-versus-daemon difference did not recur. A first parity run failed `survey_matches_its_goldens_for_every_language_fixture` with `fact store: disk I/O error` because the disk was full (165 MB free across four worktrees' ~30 GB targets); this worktree's `target/cupel-llvm-cov` and incremental cache were removed and the rerun used `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`.
- **P9.1 — open items.** (1) Cupel's own clone ratchets stay literals (`<= 169`, `<= 19`): a fresh CI checkout has no fact store, so `<= base` in `[gate.tree]` would be skipped (`no reference: no baseline is set`) and fail the dogfood job on `main`. To switch, the dogfood job (P9.0 owns `ci.yml`) must give the tree run a reference: restore `.cupel/` with `actions/cache` keyed by commit with a `main` restore key and run `cupel baseline set --write` after a green `main` run, or commit an exported baseline and `cupel baseline import FILE --write` before the run (which ratchets only as often as the file is refreshed); then change the two rules in `cupel.toml` to `"<= base"`. (2) On a range, the base side scores the base versions of the changed files whole while the current side scores what the range touched (for mutation, the changed lines' mutants); comparing hunk with hunk needs the base side of each hunk. (3) Every base run is cold: the copy keeps no store or build directory, so a `tests.mutation` `base` rule reruns the base suite and every mutant from scratch on each `--changed` check; keeping base runs in the repository's store keyed by base commit and files would make repeats free. (4) A stored run read back from before this step has `bound`/`reason` null.

Not touched: `cupel.toml`, `Makefile`, `ci.yml`, `delta.rs`, the ranking modules.
- **P9.1 — the committed baseline** ([ADR-140](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_140.md), 2026-10-20, the integrator). P9.1's open item (1) closed: `[gate.tree]`'s clone rules read `<= base`; the reference is `.cupel/baseline.json`, a tree run on `main` (`303cfb6`: 164 pairs, 17 clusters) that `make baseline` exports and `make dogfood DOGFOOD_SCOPE=tree` imports before grading, so a fresh store, local or CI's, holds it. A cache of the store across CI runs was rejected: the reference would differ between a local run and `main`, and an evicted cache would fail the gate until bootstrapped by hand. `dogfood.rs` +3 tests (the tree recipe imports the file; `make baseline` sets and exports; the file is a clean tree run on `main` with the clone metrics; the rules read `<= base`).
- **P9.2 — design** ([ADR-131](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_131.md)). `cupel_core::matching` matches the base symbols whose structural hash the head lacks with the head symbols whose hash the base lacks, by ADR-050's subtree fingerprints (`cupel_core::similarity`, moved from `static.clones`, which imports it): candidates at containment ≥ 0.8 (the smaller set's share), trees under 30 nodes excluded; each symbol keeps its best candidate (containment, Jaccard, name Dice coefficient, parameter count, same file, position); the connected components of the kept pairs are the matches (one to one, split, merge), a member whose removal raises the set similarity dropped, and a match under 0.5 Jaccard over the joined sides rejected. The scheduler matches per language under a range only, from both sides' `Symbols` and `NormTree` (`Outcome::matches`); a tree run against a stored baseline compares by identity as before. `deltas_matched` carries a base finding across a match when the head set has a finding of the same check and kind (neither new nor resolved); per-symbol metrics are written to the store's `metrics` table on every run (`Store::record_metrics`, read back by `Store::symbol_metrics`) and compared over the set as a sum, or as a node-weighted mean for a score (`Check::averages`: `static.crap`'s `crap`, `tests.quality`'s `mock_density`). The envelope's only change is `checks[].delta.sets` (optional, absent when empty): per match the base and head symbols, the similarity, the carried finding IDs, and each metric's `{base, head}`; text renders each set under its check. `tests.weakening` (version 2, now needing `symbols` and `norm-tree`) matches the tests that name pairing left over by body similarity: a renamed, moved-and-edited, split, or merged test is compared, not reported deleted. Deferred with reasons in the ADR: `static.api-surface` renames, mutation outcome reuse, and matches in a tree run against a baseline.
- **P9.2 — tests.** 2139 on `main` (17cd3cb) → 2165 at 63357e9 (+26), counted with `cargo test --workspace -- --list` at the branch's merge of `main` (2166) less the dogfood commit's net −1; the branch's diff against `main` adds 27 `#[test]` and removes none before that commit. `matching.rs` 11 (a renamed and edited function, a moved and edited one, a split, a merge, unrelated and unchanged symbols left alone, trees under 30 nodes not matched, identical twins paired by name, a deleted function not matched to a small function built from its parts, a member that only repeats the true successor left out, candidates joined by key, name similarity as shared letter pairs); `deltas.rs` 5 (a carried finding is neither new nor resolved; another kind or a symbol outside the match stays new/resolved; sums and node-weighted means; sets land on their check's delta; a delta without sets serializes as before); `against.rs` 1 (a renamed symbol's finding carried and metrics compared); `scheduler.rs` 2 (a range matches removed to added symbols; no range, no matches); `tests_weakening.rs` 4 (a renamed test compared, not deleted; renamed with the same body is no weakening; a split compared against the sum of its parts; a deleted test unlike any added one is still deleted); `store.rs` 1 (per-symbol metrics read back by check, without scores); `registration.rs` 1 (the metrics compared as weighted means are exactly `static.crap`'s `crap` and `tests.quality`'s `mock_density`, replacing one test each in `static_crap.rs` and `tests_quality.rs` that the dogfood found to be clones; it fails when `averages` names another metric); `symbol_matching.rs` 1 on the real binary (the `symbol-matching` fixture's rename and split carry their findings and compare their metrics as sets, golden `symbol-matching/changed.json`). Golden review: `git diff main...HEAD` over goldens and snapshots removes or changes four pre-existing lines, all justified: the JSON envelope snapshot's `"value": -0.12` gains a trailing comma for the new `sets`; the JSONL snapshot's header line is the old line plus `delta.sets` (parsed and compared with `sets` removed: equal); `tests.weakening/describe.json`'s `needs` gains `symbols` and `norm-tree` (the line `"test-frames"` changes only by its comma) and `version` goes 1 → 2, both ADR-131 §6. Every other change is an added line (`sets` in the SARIF, JSON, JSONL, and two text snapshots; `SetDelta`, `SetFindings`, `SetMetric` in the run schema). `c6e454f`'s claim of an additive regeneration holds, the version bump being the one changed value.
- **P9.2 — dogfood.** `make dogfood` (changed, against `main` at 17cd3cb) at 63357e9 exits 2, failing only on `tests.weakening`'s six `snapshot.rewritten`, each the deliberate `delta.sets` contract change above (the SARIF, JSON, JSONL, and two text render snapshots, and the run schema), as P5.1's and P5.3's were, and on five `static.clones` findings, all in code this branch did not write: `static_clones::metrics`/`tests_quality::metrics`, `StaticCrap::new`/`TestsQuality::new`, the `StaticCrap::plan` cluster, two `suite` helpers inside pre-existing `tests_weakening.rs` tests, and `deltas.rs`'s `check`/`store.rs`'s `check` (each in the changed scope only because its file changed). Complexity (`cyclomatic_max` 9, `length_max` 60), deps, dead code, and `tests.quality` pass. The first run, at 404e765, also found what the branch added: five clone pairs (matching's `Rng` and `statement` copied from similarity's tests, now `similarity::synthetic`; the weakening tests' body builder shaped like the scheduler test's, now assertions; the two score tests, now one registry test), `TestsWeakening::run` and `fixture::checks` at 65 lines (now `paired` and `split_set`), and three duplicate-setup findings in the matching and store tests; all fixed in 63357e9. Three intermediate runs were exit 4: the disk had 148 MiB free (four worktrees' `target/` at 30–36 GiB each), so the suite's tests failed with `tar: Write error` and SQLite `disk I/O error`, and the next runs reused that red suite from `.cupel/cache/suite` (same thread IDs in the failure output) until the cache was removed. `make parity` green (104).
- **P9.2 — open items.** (1) A suite outcome recorded while the machine was failing (disk full) is reused by later runs until `.cupel/cache/suite` is removed: a red baseline should not be cached, or its key should not survive an environment failure. (2) The five pre-existing clone findings above fail the changed gate for any branch that touches those files. (3) The thresholds (0.8 containment, 0.5 set similarity, 30 nodes) are constants (ADR-131); no external repository has tested them yet (P9.9). (4) The deferred consumers in ADR-131 §7.

- **P9.3 — design** ([ADR-132](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_132.md)). `MetricSpec` gains an optional `protection: mutation-score | coverage | tests` (absent on every other metric, so no existing manifest's JSON changes); a protection metric is a per-symbol dimension where lower is weaker. `tests.mutation` measures each function's `mutation_score` (killed / (killed + survived), equivalent survivors left out; unmeasured when no mutant was scored) and `tests` (distinct tests run against its mutants); `tests.coverage` measures `line_coverage` (covered / instrumented lines); `survivors` and `uncovered_lines` are unchanged. Every ranking dimension carries `worst: highest | lowest` (`lowest` for protection metrics) and lists its worst values first. `rankings` gains `recommended` and `targets`: the symbols an unsuppressed finding asks to protect (`write_test`, `strengthen_assertion`) or simplify (`reduce_complexity`, `refactor_extract`), each with `recommend: protect | simplify | both`, `protection {mutation_score, coverage, tests}`, its value and rank in every dimension that measured it, and its findings; ordered protect before simplify, then weakest protection (mutation score, else coverage, unmeasured weakest), then best rank in any non-protection dimension, then name and place; capped at `top`. `survey` applies suppressions before ranking, and its `next_actions` list each target's protect action in target order, then each target's simplify action, then the rest as ADR-061 ranks them; `check`'s are unchanged. Text shows `targets  N recommended, protect first` with one line per target and its values and finding IDs beneath; `describe <check>` shows `protection <metric>`. Thresholds are the checks' own settings: `survey` has no new setting. Code: `cupel-core/src/{ranking.rs, rank.rs, actions.rs, check.rs, lib.rs}`, `cupel-checks/src/{tests_mutation.rs, tests_coverage.rs, manifest.rs}`, `cupel-cli/src/{survey.rs, check.rs, registry.rs, render/text.rs, render/text/description.rs}`; design §6.5 and the README's `survey` section describe the targets.
- **P9.3 — tests** (2139 → 2151, `make check` at c410db5). `cupel-core/tests/rankings.rs`: `a_protection_dimension_ranks_its_lowest_values_first`; `an_unprotected_complex_symbol_outranks_a_protected_worse_one` (protection before complexity); `the_mutation_score_is_the_protection_and_coverage_stands_in_where_it_is_absent`; `equal_protection_falls_back_to_the_metric` (best rank in any dimension, then name); `targets_are_symbols_with_a_protect_or_simplify_finding_capped_at_top` (`recommended` counts all, `both` joins a symbol's protect and simplify findings). `cupel-core/tests/next_actions.rs`: `a_survey_lists_every_target_protect_action_before_any_simplify_action`; `a_target_whose_finding_is_suppressed_gets_no_action_for_it`. `cupel-checks/tests/tests_mutation.rs`: `each_function_is_measured_for_its_killed_ratio_and_the_tests_run_against_it` (equivalent survivors left out, tests de-duplicated); `a_function_with_no_scored_mutant_has_no_killed_ratio`. `cupel-cli/tests/survey.rs`: `a_standard_survey_protects_the_untested_function_before_simplifying_the_complex_one` (real pytest and mutation run on the Python mutation fixture: `refund` protect with coverage 0 and 0 tests, `discount` both with mutation 0.75, the `mutation_score` dimension `lowest`, and the first three `next_actions` two `write_test` then `reduce_complexity`). `cupel-cli/tests/describe.rs`: `describe_check_says_which_metrics_are_protection_values`. `cupel-cli/src/render.rs`: `text_lists_the_targets_protect_first_with_their_protection`. Extended: the `tests.mutation` and `tests.coverage` manifest and measurement tests assert the new metrics and their `protection`. Golden review (`git diff main...HEAD -- 'crates/cupel-fixtures/golden/**' 'crates/**/snapshots/**' | grep -E '^-[^-]'`): every removed line in the `static.{clones,complexity,crap,deps}/survey-*.json` and `tests.quality/survey-*.json` goldens (the two `static.complexity` lines per language are the `cyclomatic` and `cognitive` dimensions) and in the SARIF and JSONL render snapshots is a dimension's last field, `summary`, re-emitted with a trailing comma because `worst` now follows it; no value changed. The `survey` verb summary (`describe.json`, the `describe` and `format` CLI snapshots, the text description snapshot) gains ", protect-first". The run schema snapshot's rewritten descriptions are the ranking's documented meaning changing with ADR-132 §2–3: "1 is the highest value" → "1 is the worst value", "The highest values first" → "The worst values first", `Rankings`' description now names the targets, `top` now caps `targets` too, and `summary` is no longer the last required field (`worst` is). Everything else is added: `worst` on every dimension, `recommended`/`targets` in `survey/<lang>.json`, the protection metrics in `tests.{mutation,coverage}/describe.json`, `protection` in the plugin schema.

A branch that adds a `MetricSpec` literal must add `protection: None`; one that touches the run schema, the fixture envelope, or a survey golden regenerates on top of this (`UPDATE_GOLDENS=1 INSTA_UPDATE=always make test`, then `make readme`) and keeps `worst`, `recommended`, and `targets`. A branch that changes how findings name symbols (P9.2's matching) changes which symbols become targets only through the findings, not through this code.
- **P9.4 — design** ([ADR-133](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_133.md)). The checks compute each hint when they report the finding and store it in `action.hints` under a fixed key typed in `cupel_core::hint`: `common_subtree` on `clone.cluster` (the first member's largest non-leaf subtree whose shape occurs in every other member, its location there and in each other member, and the identifier and literal positions whose text differs, as parameters), `deepest_block` on `complexity.high` (the structural decision of greatest nesting, then most decisions inside, then first; its location, nesting, construct path, and the cyclomatic and cognitive weight inside it), `cheapest_cut` on `dep.cycle` (among the reported cycle's edges: one whose removal leaves the component acyclic first, then fewest unnamed import edges, then fewest names; its specifiers, names, unnamed count, and `breaks_component`). The facts gained optional fields, versions unchanged: `NormNode.span` and `.text` (marker leaves), `Decision.span`, `ImportEdge.names` (Rust path item, Python `from` name, Java single import, JS/TS named and `default` bindings via the new `@import.default` and `@import.names` captures; none for Go and Clojure). `structural_hash` hashes `NormNode::shape_json`, byte-identical to the old serialization, so no hash or finding ID changed; `static.clones` and `tests.quality` compare shapes, not JSON. The run schema's `action.hints` names the known keys (each optional, map still open); `FindingKindSpec.hints` and `FindingDescription.hints` (`x-cupel-optional`) carry each hint's schema for `describe`. Text renders a hint on one line under its finding and in full under `explain` (`render/text/remedy.rs`). A hint whose facts are missing is absent.
- **P9.4 — tests** (2592 → 2615, `make check`'s `cargo test` pass count at 31ba5e0; 23 added, none removed). `cupel-core/tests/syntax_facts.rs` +4: a decision's span, a node's span and marker text, shape equality and `shape_json` ignoring both (and equal to the old bytes), an edge's names omitted when empty. `cupel-lang`: `rust_norm` every node has a span and markers their text; `rust_decisions` each decision carries its construct's span; `import_names` the names of Rust, Python, and JavaScript edges (submodules, wildcards, aliases, defaults, re-exports, `require`). `cupel-checks`: `static_clones` +4 (the shared subtree and differing parameters of a three-member cluster; no hint without spans; none on a pair; the manifest declares it), `static_complexity` +4 (the deepest block, tie broken by what it holds, with path and weights; only `complexity.high` carries it; none without spans; declared), `static_deps` +4 (fewest names wins; component-breaking beats fewer names; statements merged with unnamed counted; declared). `cupel-cli`: `explain` +3 (each hint in JSON and in full text), `describe` +1 (`describe finding` shows each kind's hint schema). Goldens: all seven languages' `static.clones`, `static.complexity`, `static.deps` goldens carry the hints, the fact goldens the new fields; a script confirmed every changed golden JSON differs from 303cfb6 only by `span`, `text`, `names`, the three hint keys, and `FindingKindSpec.hints`.

Shared: `crates/cupel-core/src/{syntax.rs (NormNode, Decision, ImportEdge fields), finding.rs (hints schema), check.rs (FindingKindSpec.hints), describe.rs, lib.rs}`, `crates/cupel-checks/src/{tests_quality.rs (shape grouping), manifest.rs, finding.rs}`, `crates/cupel-cli/src/{render/text.rs, render/text/description.rs, catalog.rs, oracle/cap.rs}`, literal-site field additions in `crates/cupel-checks/tests/{static_dead_code.rs, tests_coverage.rs, tests_quality.rs, static_crap.rs, meta_oracle_independence.rs, support/mod.rs}` and `crates/cupel-core/tests/{scope.rs, config_registry.rs, analyzer_report.rs, extension.rs}`, `crates/cupel-lang/tests/{ecma_norm.rs, support/mod.rs}`, README (explain prose), the core schema snapshots, and the fact, check, survey, symbol-matching, and `cli` goldens. A branch that builds `NormNode`, `Decision`, `ImportEdge`, or `FindingKindSpec` literally must add the new fields; P9.7 (raw-token digest on `NormTree`) touches the same struct and fact goldens and can derive its digest from `NormNode.text`; a branch regenerating fact or schema goldens after merging must keep these additive fields.
- **P9.5 — design** ([ADR-134](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_134.md), amending ADR-114 §1–§2). `TestCase` gains `doc` (TestFrames version 3): the case's doc comment or docstring as written, read by a new `@test.doc` capture with `@doc.target` on the node it documents, so a doc pattern may match any function and documents only a case another pattern found (roots and every other reader of `@test.case` are unchanged). Per language: Rust's `///` lines or `/** */` above the attributes, Go's `//` lines above `func TestX`, the Python docstring, a JSDoc `/** */` above the `it`/`test` statement, Javadoc above the method, and Clojure's `;` lines above `deftest`/`defspec`. A test names a requirement when its name or doc carries the ID as whole words (ADR-114's rule); its body is not read. A marker is the word `req` then a word of digits (`#REQ-12`, `req_12`, `TestParse_REQ_12`, not `REQ12`, `testReq12`, `prereq_8`), written `REQ-<digits>`. Requirements have a `source`: `config` (listed) or `marker` (a marker on a test the range added that no listed ID matches; markers on pre-existing tests add nothing). A marker requirement is listed as the new kind `spec.unlisted-requirement` (`info`, `configure`), with evidence `{requirement, source, base, tests}`; `spec.untested-requirement`'s evidence gains `source`. Status and `untested` are unchanged; the score's `n` counts both sources. `spec` is not a source value: ADR-114 offers no `--spec FILE`. Manifest version 2.
- **P9.5 — tests** (2592 → 2599 by `cargo test --workspace -- --list` at 303cfb6 and at the tip; `make check` at the tip ran all 2599, none ignored). `cupel-lang/tests/test_docs.rs`: `each_language_reads_its_own_doc_form_above_a_test` proves each of the seven languages' doc forms is read as written, multi-line docs joined, and a plain comment or a documented non-test function documents no case. `cupel-checks/tests/meta_traceability.rs`: `a_configured_requirement_an_added_test_names_only_in_its_doc_is_traced`; `a_marker_on_an_added_test_lists_a_requirement_the_configuration_does_not` (the `info` finding, its ID, title, evidence with `source: marker`, pass status, `n` 2); `without_configuration_the_markers_of_added_tests_are_the_requirements` (a marker on a pre-existing test adds nothing); `a_marker_is_req_then_a_word_of_digits_and_a_configured_id_is_matched_in_any_case`; `markers_only_on_tests_that_existed_before_leave_nothing_to_trace` (the note stays). Changed: the untested evidence asserts `source: config`, the whole-token test now also expects REQ-120 listed and the status `fail` for its one untested requirement, the manifest shape has both kinds. `cupel-fixtures/tests/meta.rs`: `every_language_traces_a_doc_marker_lists_a_name_marker_and_reads_a_fixture_doc` runs the real binary with `--base` on a copy of each of the seven language fixtures, adding a test whose doc names configured REQ-2 (traced) and whose name carries REQ-3 (listed, `source: marker`), with REQ-1 untested and its evidence naming the fixture's documented test (`added: false`). Conformance: the `test-frames` inventory gains `doc`, mapped in every language's fixture (a `REQ-1` doc on one existing test each). `syntax_facts` asserts `doc` serializes and TestFrames is version 3.

- **P9.6 — design** ([ADR-135](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_135.md), superseding ADR-058 and amending ADR-059). A new fact kind, `References` (`references`, version 1, syntactic, with `conformance/references.toml`), appended to `FactKind`: per reference its file, span, name, kind (`name`, `member`, `text`, `attribute`, `implicit`), innermost symbol (`from`), and target (`local` or `imported` with the symbols, or `unresolved`); per dynamic access its file, span, `from`, reach (`members` or `names`), and text. The fact covers every file of the language in the repository (the walk's rules: `.gitignore`, `[scope] exclude`, the ecosystem's generated directories, through `walk::language_files`), whatever the scope, so callers outside a `--changed` scope count. The producer (`cupel_lang::references`, `lang.<id>`) is registered with each language's `ModuleGraph` producer by `references::register_resolved`, since it calls the same resolver over the language's files; it reads a new query file per language, `references.scm` (contract: `@reference.name` and `@reference.member` required; `@reference.text`, `@reference.attribute` with `@attribute.name`, `@dynamic.members`, `@dynamic.names` optional; one capture per node, the earliest pattern winning) and `tables.toml [references]` (`inert` attributes, `implicit` method names, `path_imports`). A plain name resolves to the same file's functions and closures, else to the imported file's through an import that binds it (the names a statement lists, Python; or every path's last segment where `path_imports`, Rust), else stays unresolved; members and strings are unresolved; a definition's own name is never a reference. `static.dead-code` version 2 needs `references`; the module pass is unchanged; the symbol pass reports, as `dead.symbol` at 0.6 (0.3 where a dynamic access in the language could reach it: any `names` site, a `members` site for a method), every symbol in scope that the module pass keeps and that is not a root for any reason, not nested, not a constructor (a method named like its type), and named by no reference outside its own body. Evidence gains `reach` (`module` or `references`) and `dynamic` (up to ten `file:line`). Per language, as ADR-135 §5 records: Rust and Go record no dynamic access (0.6); Python's `getattr` family with a non-literal name, `globals`, `eval`, and kin, JavaScript and TypeScript's computed subscripts and `eval`, Java's reflection lookups, and Clojure's `resolve` and kin lower their findings. On Cupel's own tree the check reports nothing over 7 224 symbols (8.8 s in a debug build, the references of 567 files); two unused functions appended to `rank.rs` as a probe were both reported at 0.6 (the probe was removed).
- **P9.6 — tests** (2595 → 2616, `make check` at eb9d38b: 2616 passed, less the 21 this branch adds; the run before the branch stopped at a test this branch's new query files tripped mid-run, so it gave no total). `crates/cupel-lang/tests/references.rs` (12): `a_plain_name_resolves_to_the_function_of_that_name_in_its_own_file`, `a_plain_name_an_import_binds_resolves_to_the_function_in_the_imported_file` (Rust `use`, Python `from … import`), `an_es_import_binds_no_name_by_its_specifier_so_the_name_stays_unresolved` (`@ledger/shapes/square` binds `side`, not `square`), `a_name_reached_through_a_value_type_or_package_is_an_unresolved_member` (six languages), `a_definition_s_own_name_is_not_a_reference` (six languages), `an_attribute_names_the_definition_it_is_on_unless_the_language_calls_it_inert`, `a_decorator_names_the_function_or_method_it_decorates` (Python, Java), `a_string_whose_text_is_a_path_of_names_is_a_text_reference_to_its_last_segment`, `identifiers_in_a_macro_are_members_since_the_token_tree_does_not_say_what_they_are`, `a_constructor_the_runtime_calls_gets_an_implicit_reference_to_itself`, `a_name_computed_at_run_time_is_a_dynamic_access_with_what_it_can_reach` (a literal `getattr` name is text, not dynamic; Rust and Go have none), `references_come_from_every_file_of_the_language_whatever_the_scope`; `references.rs` unit tests (2): what a string names, an attribute's last segment. `crates/cupel-checks/tests/static_dead_code.rs` (15 → 21): `a_symbol_nothing_names_in_a_reached_module_is_dead_by_references` (0.6, its title, detail, and evidence), `a_reference_resolved_to_one_symbol_does_not_keep_its_namesakes_alive`, `an_unresolved_reference_keeps_every_symbol_of_its_name_alive`, `a_reference_inside_its_own_body_is_recursion_but_one_in_its_signature_counts`, `roots_nested_symbols_and_constructors_are_never_reported_by_references`, `a_lookup_by_name_lowers_every_finding_and_a_computed_member_only_methods`; seven existing tests gained the references their module-pass cases imply, so each still asserts what it named. `crates/cupel-fixtures/tests/conformance.rs` (+1): `a_reference_is_located_by_its_file_and_name_and_a_dynamic_access_by_its_text`. The matrix test's trials are unchanged (398); every language's `references` golden and inventory mapping are new. Fixture cases per language (`references.*` and `rates.*` in each repository): an exported rate, one reached only through an import (only Rust and Python can import a non-root; elsewhere the import needs an export or a package), one reached only dynamically (a table that names it in Rust, Go, JavaScript, TypeScript, so live; a computed name in Python, Java, Clojure, so reported at 0.3), and one nothing names (reported). Golden review against `main`: every pre-existing finding is kept with only `reach` and `dynamic` added; the new `dead.symbol` findings are the fixtures' unreferenced private functions (Rust `scale`, `audit_limit`; Go's unexported clone, decision, and twin functions; Java's package-private ones; the private methods `#isValid`, `isValid`, `#cache`; Python's `audit_limit` and `_audit`), each checked to have no caller; plus Clojure's `ledger.rates/doubled` as `dead.export` (nothing requires the new `rates.clj`). Changed pre-existing values, all from the two new files per fixture: `run.scope.files` and `.symbols`, every check's `score.n`, the survey rankings' `measured` and complexity ranks, and `next_actions` where the new warnings outrank the old ones; `static.dead-code`'s `version` 1 → 2, `needs`, and manifest text (ADR-135). Snapshots: the run and plugin schemas and `describe language` text gain `references`; the `module-graph` expectations in four `*_modules.rs` tests gain the new files' edges.

The gate fails on three `tests.weakening` `snapshot.rewritten` (the run schema, the plugin schema, and the `describe language` text snapshot, each the deliberate catalog addition above); four `static.clones` findings, all pre-dating the branch in files it touched (follow-up item 18): the test helpers `private`/`public` in `static_dead_code.rs`, `Ecma::javascript`/`Ecma::typescript`, the `register` cluster of the Go, Rust, and Java languages, and the `the_fixture_graph_is_exactly_…` tests of `python_modules.rs` and `rust_modules.rs`; and two `tests.coverage` uncovered functions, both pre-existing and unchanged in files it touched: `Imports::root` and `SpecError::fmt`. At 027a412 the tree gate passes (exit 0, 1 568 s): pairs 164, clusters 17, coverage 0.970, CRAP max 20, complexity, deps, dead code, quality, analyzers, properties, and contracts clean. `make check` at 027a412: 2645 passed.
- **P9.6 — open items.** (1) Reachability is not followed from roots: a function only dead code calls is reported once that code is gone, and two private functions calling only each other stay unreported. (2) Unresolved names match across the language, so a common name (`new`, `len`, `run`) used anywhere keeps every symbol of that name live: missed findings, never invented ones. (3) Go, Java, and Clojure plain names resolve only within their file: Clojure `:refer` lists are not captured as `@import.name`. At the merge with P9.4, ES named imports resolve through ADR-133's `ImportEdge.names` (the `references.rs` test on `square` now expects `imported`), but by the imported name, so an aliased import (`import { square as side }`) leaves `side` unresolved (follow-up item 20). (4) A JavaScript `eval` lowers every JavaScript finding, though direct `eval` reaches only its own scope. (5) The producer parses every file of the language on each run, including `--changed` (6 s for Cupel in a debug build); a store of per-file references keyed by content would make it incremental. (6) The module pass still reads only the scope's import edges, so under `--changed` an unchanged importer is invisible and pub items read as `dead.export` (ADR-058); the symbol pass does not have this problem. (7) The four pre-existing clone findings and two uncovered functions above fail the changed gate for any branch that touches those files.
