- **P9.7 — design** ([ADR-136](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_136.md), amends ADR-050's Type bullet). `cupel_core::similarity::token_digest(root)` folds every label with its child count and every `NormNode.text` in pre-order (FNV-1a labels, `combine`/`mix`), `None` when a `$id` or `$lit` leaf lacks its text; it is computed by `static.clones` from ADR-133's texts, so the `NormTree` fact, its version, and every symbol hash are unchanged. A pair is type 1 when both digests are present and equal (and the shapes equal), 2 when only the shapes are, 3 otherwise; a cluster is typed by every member against the first. Evidence of both kinds gains `clone_type` (new on `clone.cluster`), `renamed` (type 2: the `common_subtree` hint's `parameters` walk over the whole tree, `values` one text per member, left then right for a pair), and `edits` (type 3: a top-down alignment, labels differing → removed and inserted, children aligned by a longest common subsequence of equal shapes, equal-length unmatched runs compared pairwise again, others removed and inserted; each edit `change`, `symbol`, `nodes`, and `location` when the node has a span). `manifest::kind` now generates evidence schemas with `inline_subschemas`, so a referenced type (`hint::Parameter`, `Location`) is written in place for the evidence validator (ADR-043); every existing evidence schema is byte-identical. The manifest summary names the three types and keeps one limitation: normalization's erasures (layout, comments, the function's own name, Python annotations and decorators, Rust attributes and visibility, Java modifiers) count as type 1, and two lookup tables differing only in literals are type 2.
- **P9.7 — tests** (2618 → 2631, `make check`'s `cargo test` pass count at b3b9ec2; 13 added, none removed). `cupel-core` `similarity` +3: digests agree exactly for equal texts and differ for a renamed identifier, a changed literal, a text moved between markers, and a missing statement; spans do not change a digest; a marker without text gives none. `cupel-checks/tests/static_clones.rs` +9: equal tokens are a type-1 pair with nothing renamed or edited (title `type-1`); a renamed copy lists the identifiers and literals as left/right values with occurrences; markers without text stay type 2 with nothing renamed; a type-3 pair's inserted statement with its location; a statement missing from the second member is `removed`; a changed statement narrows to the changed operator (one removal, one insertion); clusters of type 1, type 2 (one text per member), and type 3 (the changed member's edits). The manifest-limitation test now pins the new limitation. `cupel-fixtures/tests/clones_family.rs` +1: Go and Java fixtures each hold a type-1, a type-2 (with `renamed`), and a type-3 (with `edits`) pair through `cupel check`. Fixtures: `runningRefunds` and `runningRefundsAgain` (the same body laid out differently, a comment added) in `repos/go/clones_settlement.go` and `repos/java/.../ClonesSettlement.java`. Goldens: every `static.clones` golden gains `clone_type` on clusters and `renamed`/`edits` on both kinds; no existing pair changed type (no fixture pair had equal texts). Changed pre-existing values, each from the two new Go and Java symbols: `symbols`/`n`/`measured` 78→80 (Go) and 98→100 (Java) across the check, survey, and fact goldens; Go and Java `static.clones` pairs, findings, warn, and `next_actions` priorities after the inserted pair shift by one; `survey/{go,java}.json` `recommended` +1 and the ranks of cyclomatic-1 symbols +2. The `static.clones` describe golden's summary and `clone_type` description changed with the decision.

(2) `edits` repeat a removed subtree once per cluster member that lacks it. (4) `similarity::seeds`' coverage finding needs a coverage exclusion or a runtime call; it is not this step's code.

- **P9.8 — design** ([ADR-137](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_137.md), amending ADR-124's Windows consequences). `cupel_serve::transport` holds a `Listener` (bind, accept within a wait, whether its endpoint is still its own) and a `Connection` (connect to an endpoint path, `Read`/`Write` also through `&Connection`, `try_clone`, wait for the peer to hang up), each with a Unix and a Windows implementation chosen by `cfg`; the server loop, the client, and the protocol name neither. Unix is ADR-085 unchanged (socket at the path, `0600`, inode identity, `poll` for 200 ms). Windows: the pipe `\\.\pipe\cupel-<16 hex of BLAKE3 of the endpoint's absolute path>` (`transport::pipe_name`, so with the default socket it is fixed by the repository path and `--socket` still selects its own), byte-mode duplex, `PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS`, the first instance with `FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`, the default security descriptor; then an endpoint file at the socket path holding the pipe's name and a line unique to the daemon (its identity: Windows file tunnelling carries a deleted file's creation time over to its replacement). Accepting polls a nonblocking (`PIPE_NOWAIT`) instance every 5 ms for up to the loop's 200 ms and switches a connected one to blocking mode; a run's hang-up is a `PeekNamedPipe` poll every 50 ms, because a blocked read on a synchronous handle would hold back the run's `stderr` writes; a client retries for 5 s while every instance is busy. Every rule above the transport (`.cupel/serve.sock`, `CUPEL_NO_DAEMON`, stale endpoints, `doctor`, `serve --stop`, parity) is the same on both. The workspace's `unsafe_code` lint is `deny` instead of `forbid`, allowed only in `transport/windows.rs`, each Win32 call with its safety stated. `nix` is a Unix-only dependency of `cupel-serve`; `windows-sys` 0.61.2 (already in the lock) a Windows-only one; `blake3` names the pipe. Unix-only code outside the transport compiles only on Unix: `cupel-cli`'s `install.rs`, `installers.rs`, `oracle.rs`, `pages.rs`, `release.rs`, and `serve.rs` carry `#![cfg(unix)]` (as `plugins.rs` did), the `plugin` test helper is `cfg(unix)`, and the parity harness's tree copy makes Windows directory or file links. CI's `windows` job (`windows-latest`, make 4.4.1 from Chocolatey) runs `make windows-check` (`cargo check --workspace --all-targets --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`) and `make windows-test`: `cargo test --package cupel-serve` (its server tests over real named pipes) and `cargo test --lib` for `cupel-core`, `cupel-checks`, `cupel-lang`, and `cupel-mutate`, the crates whose unit tests spawn no process and touch no file. `release.yml`'s Windows build loses `continue-on-error`; the README's install section lists the `.zip` and what Windows lacks; the design's §10 and ADR-124's consequences drop "best-effort". `dist/targets.toml`'s comments never said best-effort and are unchanged.
- **P9.8 — tests** (2625 → 2630 by `make check`'s `cargo test` count; 5 added, none removed on macOS). `transport::tests::the_pipe_is_named_for_the_endpoint_s_absolute_path` (the name's form, stable per path, distinct across paths) and `a_relative_endpoint_names_the_pipe_of_its_absolute_path` (a client and daemon given the same endpoint relatively and absolutely meet); `release::every_release_build_holds_back_the_release_when_it_fails` (no `continue-on-error` or `best-effort` in the build job) and `ci_compiles_and_tests_the_windows_target` (the `windows` job's runner, make, and both targets); `server::an_endpoint_that_cannot_be_made_is_an_io_error_naming_it` (a socket path under a regular file is `ServeError::Io` naming it; covers `server::failed`). `crates/cupel-serve/tests/server.rs` drives the transport's `Connection` instead of `UnixStream`, with a per-platform stale endpoint, so its 13 tests compile and run on Windows. `dev_setup::workflow_pins` leaves ci.yml's `windows` job out, as it did release.yml's Windows step.

The named-pipe code, the `windows` CI job, and the Windows release build have not run; the first push runs the job, and the first tag (or a `workflow_dispatch` of `release`) the build, now without `continue-on-error`.
- **P9.8 — dogfood.** `make dogfood` (changed, `--depth deep --budget 30m`, through the lock) at 457d8f3: exit 5 (`partial`), gate `fail (gate.changed)` with every evaluated rule passing (complexity none, cyclomatic max 6, clones none, deps none and 0 cycles, dead-code none, tests.quality none, static.analyze none, tests.coverage none, CRAP max 12.7, gen.property 0 falsified, spec.contracts none, tests.weakening none) and `tests.mutation.score >= 0.8` skipped because the budget left `tests.mutation` `partial` (860 s). Warn-level findings outside the gate: `meta.oracle-independence` (2 of 46 changed functions exercised only by tests this branch wrote), `tests.assertion-density` (pre-existing sparse tests in touched files: `llvm_cov`'s executables test, `dev_setup.rs`, `release::every_publish_job_is_gated_on_its_secret`), and `static.api-surface` `api.widened` (info) for the new public transport items.
- **P9.8 — open items.** (1) The named-pipe transport has not run anywhere; the Windows CI job is its first test. (2) The Windows job runs neither `cupel-cli`'s integration suites, the golden harness, nor `make parity`; ADR-124's other Windows items stay open (`sh -c` producers, no Job Object for timeouts, `/`-separated paths in findings and goldens, symlinks, plugin discovery's untested `not(unix)` branch). (3) The pipe keeps the default security descriptor (other local users may open it read-only and cannot send a request) where the Unix socket is `0600`. (5) `make check` here twice failed one test under a load average of 85–170 from other agents (`plugin_conformance`, a fresh plugin's `describe` past 10 s; `installers::each_wrapper_refuses_a_bad_checksum…`, npm's local fetch failing), both untouched code; each passed on rerun.

Shared: `Cargo.toml` (`unsafe_code = "deny"`; `windows-sys` in `[workspace.dependencies]`), `Cargo.lock` (two edges on `cupel-serve`), `crates/cupel-serve/{Cargo.toml, src/lib.rs, src/server.rs, src/client.rs, tests/server.rs}`, `crates/cupel-fixtures/src/parity.rs` (the `symlink` helper), `crates/cupel-adapt/src/per_test/llvm_cov.rs` (one test import moved under `cfg(unix)`), `crates/cupel-cli/tests/{support/mod.rs, install.rs, installers.rs, oracle.rs, pages.rs, release.rs, serve.rs, dev_setup.rs}`, `.github/workflows/{ci.yml, release.yml}`, `Makefile` (`windows-check`, `windows-test`), `README.md` (install section, prose only), `docs/CUPEL_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` §10, and ADR-124's status line and Windows consequence. A branch adding a `cupel-cli` integration test that uses `std::os::unix` adds `#![cfg(unix)]` or keeps `make windows-check` compiling; one that adds `unsafe` anywhere but `transport/windows.rs` is refused by the `deny` lint as before.
- **P9.F1 — design** ([ADR-141](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_141.md)). (16) `tests.mutation.score` is killed / (killed + survived + uncovered), 1 when the scope has no killed, surviving, or uncovered mutant; `Score.n` is that denominator, so languages combine weighted by it; each function's `mutation_score` (ADR-132) is the same ratio, so a function whose mutants are all uncovered scores 0 and ranks first for protection. Status, findings, timeouts, unviable mutants, and possibly equivalent survivors are unchanged; the manifest summary and both metric descriptions state the formula. (17) A joint suite run (`cupel-adapt/src/suite/mod.rs`) is cached in `.cupel/cache/suite`, with its per-test entry, only when it completed, made its facts, and no test failed or errored; the entry version is 2, so a red entry written before reruns once (ADR-084 §6 amended). (19) `cupel-mutate/src/slots.rs` (slot locations moved out of `overlay.rs`): each repository's slot directory holds `repository` (its path; its modification time is the last use), `size` (written when a production ends), and `lock` (a shared `File::lock` every production holds for its duration). A claim removes another repository's unlocked directory when its repository is gone or it is idle for more than 7 days, then, while every repository's slots hold more than 32 GiB, the least recently used unlocked ones that have a lock file; a directory with no lock file was made by an earlier Cupel that may be running, so only the gone and idle rules apply to it, and no lock file is ever created in another repository's directory. A file whose mutants end in an error (budget, cancellation, red reference) gets its original written back into every slot it used (`Execution::restore`); a worker whose reference left the renewed file restores it (`Holds::Renewed`); a sync replaces a slot file that held other bytes with a copy dated now, never a hard link. `cupel doctor` reports `doctor.mutation-slots` (info, `review`): the total, and each repository's path, existence, bytes, and last use, when the slots directory holds any. Every test invocation of the binary (`support::run_with` unless the test sets `TMPDIR`, `cupel_fixtures::run_cupel`, both parity servers) gets its own `TMPDIR`, so no test or golden sees another's slots or the machine's. (20) `ImportEdge.aliases` (`{name, local}`, absent when empty): ES import specifiers' `alias` fields (not `export … from`), a default import (`default` → its local name), and Python's new `@import.alias` capture (`from x import a as b`; a submodule imported under another name records none). `References` binds each name of an edge to a file under its local name and resolves it among the target's functions by the imported name, following `re-export` edges (each file once) when the target only re-exports it; an edge with no names binds as before. This also resolves every ES named import, which before resolved only where the name equalled the specifier's last segment: the P9.6 note's "ES named imports resolve through `ImportEdge.names`" held only for `square`, by way of the next line's `@ledger/shapes/square`.
- **P9.F1 — goldens.** Mutation goldens, `score`, `metrics.score`, and `score.n` only (`git diff` shows nothing else): Python, Go, JavaScript, TypeScript, Rust 0.75 → 0.6 (n 4 → 5); Clojure 0.6 → 0.5 (n 5 → 6); Java 0.7142857 → 0.5555556 (n 7 → 9). `tests.mutation/describe.json` and the SARIF render snapshot: the summary and metric descriptions. The survey test: `refund`'s `mutation_score` null → 0.0, and the lowest `mutation_score` entry 0.75 → 0.0. Module graph (JavaScript, TypeScript, Python) and its schema snapshot: `aliases` added (default imports, `square as side`, Python's `exported_rate as tripled`) and Python's new `rates.py` edge; nothing removed. References: JavaScript 51 and TypeScript 49 references `unresolved` → `imported` (`add`, `range`, `parseCents`, `classify`, `label`, `toCents`, `importedRate`, `doubled`, `discounted`, `tally`, `Badge`, `square`, `side`; every one names the file the import leads to), Python four references added (the alias line and `TRIPLED = tripled`). Python api-surface: `ledger.rates.TRIPLED` added. No `static.dead-code` golden moved.
- **P9.F1 — tests** (2645 at 027a412 by P9.6's note; 2661 at 4218d08 by `make check`'s pass count). `cupel-checks/tests/tests_mutation.rs`: `the_score_is_killed_over_killed_surviving_and_uncovered` (renamed, 2/4), the timeouts test now scores (0.0, 1), `a_scope_of_only_timeouts_and_unviable_mutants_scores_one`, `a_function_whose_mutants_no_test_runs_has_a_killed_ratio_of_zero` (the no-ratio test now uses a timeout). `cupel-cli/tests/suite_run.rs`: `a_red_baseline_is_not_cached_and_refuses_every_run_with_exit_4` (was: a cached red baseline exits 4), `a_suite_that_failed_once_runs_again_and_passes_on_an_unchanged_tree` (the real binary twice; a test asserts a file outside the repository is absent, the file is removed between runs; red on 2e75dbb's code, 4 and 4). `cupel-core/tests/syntax_facts.rs`: `an_import_edge_lists_the_names_its_statement_binds_under_another_local_name`. `cupel-lang/tests/references.rs`: the import test gains the alias cases (ES `side`, Python `tripled`), and the ES test now expects `importedRate` imported. `cupel-mutate/tests/slots.rs` (new; two tests moved from `overlay.rs`): gone, idle (8 days against 7), total size (least recently used first, own kept), own kept at any size, a held claim never removed and removed once released, size recorded at the end, lock-less directories only by the gone and idle rules and left without a lock file, `usage`. `overlay.rs`: `a_sync_replaces_a_file_left_mutated_with_a_copy_dated_now`. `engine.rs`: `mutants_that_end_in_an_error_leave_the_slots_holding_the_original_file` (red reference in the slot; failed without `restore`, the slot holding the renewed file). `cupel-cli/tests/doctor.rs`: the slot report and its absence; `mutation.rs`: `a_mutation_run_removes_the_slots_of_gone_and_idle_repositories_and_records_its_own` (the real engine, `TMPDIR` in a temporary directory). `gate.rs`: P9.1's original first phase is restored, an untested change scoring 0.0 against bound 0.58 (the base now 0.6). `python_modules.rs`: the new edge.
- **P9.F1 — dogfood.** `make check` green at 4218d08 and at 18ce8c8 (2661 passed each; at 18ce8c8 the overlay's build-outputs test, dropped by mistake when the slot tests moved, is back, and the alias cases joined the import test); `make parity` green at 4218d08 (507 passed). `make dogfood` (changed, `--depth deep --budget 30m`, against `main` at 2e75dbb), three runs. At 4218d08, first: exit 4, the baseline red on `similarity::tests::ten_thousand_symbols_are_searched_in_linear_work_and_under_a_second` (47–50 s against its 1 s limit at load averages of 60–170; it passes at load 10; `similarity.rs` is P9.7's, left alone), after the lock had been held for over ten minutes by four orphaned, spinning mutant test processes (PPID 1) of the ended `p9-windows-serve` dogfood, which were killed (P9.0 open item (7) seen). At 4218d08, second: exit 5, and it found in this branch's code `slots::reclaim` (cyclomatic 13, cognitive 21), weak or repeated assertions in five slot tests, the engine test's loop, a mutation-check test pair and the doctor test, a clone pair of the two reference import tests, and the deleted overlay test; all fixed at 18ce8c8. At 18ce8c8: exit 5 (partial), 1 812 s, 34 files and 693 symbols; the budget left out `gen.fuzz` and `perf.bench` and left `tests.mutation` partial after 572 s, so its rule was skipped and Cupel's uncovered count is still unmeasured. The gate fails only on what the notes above justify or pre-dates the branch: `tests.weakening`'s two `snapshot.rewritten` (the SARIF render snapshot's summary, the module-graph schema's `aliases`); five `static.clones` findings in touched files, none written here (follow-up item 18: `static_dead_code.rs`'s `private`/`public`, `support::finding`/`survey::dimension`, the two `ecma::resolve` clusters, the Go and Rust `paths_normalize` tests); six `tests.coverage` functions in touched files, none written here (`doctor`'s test `Needs::plan` and `Needs::run`, `Imports::root`, `java::resolve::is_descriptor`, `engine::io`, `execute::panicked`). Complexity (cyclomatic max 10), deps, dead code, `tests.quality`, analyzers, CRAP (max 11.0), properties, and contracts pass. Not gated: `static.api-surface` reports `overlay::slot_dir` and `overlay::claim` removed (moved to `slots`, an internal crate's API). `$TMPDIR/cupel-mutate` (`du -sh`): nothing after the restart; 656 KB before the first run; 48 GB before the first dogfood and 47 GB after it (all `p9-windows-serve`'s, made by a Cupel without locks, so left to the gone and idle rules); 47 GB + 49 GB (this branch's) = 97 GB after the second; 47 GB before the third (this branch's 49 GB were removed by its own `make check`, whose in-process engine tests claim under the real rule, as designed: idle, locking, and over 32 GiB); 83 GB after it (47 GB `p9-windows-serve`, 35 GB this branch).
- **P9.F1 — open items.** (1) The changed-gate measurement of `tests.mutation.score` with uncovered mutants, and Cupel's uncovered count, wait on a dogfood with a green baseline (a quiet machine); if the score falls below 0.80 the rule should stay and the uncovered functions get tests, since the rule now means what it says. (2) The similarity timing test fails every baseline at a load above ~60; P9.7 owns it. (3) Mutant test processes outlive a budget-stopped or timed-out run (orphans with PPID 1) and hold the dogfood lock; the runner kills its child, not the child's process group. (8) Cupel's own in-process engine tests claim slots under the machine-wide rule, so a `make check` evicts an idle checkout's large slots (the next dogfood rebuilds them); tests could claim under a lenient rule. (9) Slots made by a Cupel without locks are never removed for size, so two checkouts on different versions can still hold more than the cap (83–97 GB here) until both run this code. (4) Rust `use a::b as c`, Clojure `:refer`/`:as`, and ES namespace imports bind no local names. (5) No conformance construct for aliased imports in `references.toml`. (6) The 7-day and 32 GiB values are constants. (7) During this branch's work the `p9-clone-types` slots (14 GB) disappeared while an early version of `slots.rs`, which applied the size rule to lock-less directories, ran in tests against the real temporary directory; that version could have removed them (the committed one cannot), so that worktree's next mutation run rebuilds its slots.

Changed: `cupel-checks/src/tests_mutation.rs`; `cupel-adapt/src/suite/mod.rs`; `cupel-mutate/src/{engine,execute,overlay,lib}.rs`; `cupel-cli/src/{doctor,run}.rs`; `cupel-core/src/syntax.rs` (`ImportEdge.aliases`, `ImportAlias`); `cupel-lang/src/{imports,references,contract}.rs`, the ES and Python `imports.scm`, every language's `resolve.rs` (`aliases`); the Python fixture's `rates.py`; tests listed above; ADR-080/084/129/133/135 status lines; README (score, suite cache, slots, doctor), design §5 table row. Shared: `cupel-cli/tests/support/mod.rs`, `cupel-fixtures/src/{run,parity,mcp_parity}.rs` (`TMPDIR` per invocation), the module-graph schema snapshot, the mutation, module-graph, references, and api-surface goldens. A branch that constructs `ImportEdge` adds `aliases: Vec::new()`; one that adds a mutation fixture or golden expects uncovered mutants in the score; one that adds a JS or TS fixture import expects it `imported`; P9.7 (`similarity.rs`) owns the timing test.
- **P9.F2 — design** ([ADR-142](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_142.md), [ADR-143](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_143.md)). (18) Under `--changed` or `--base`, the scheduler's symbol resolution keeps a symbol only when one of its file's changed lines (ADR-077 §5's `ChangedLines`, already computed for the mutation engine) overlaps its span: `cupel_core::scope::touched`, applied by `schedule::within_range` after `--symbols` selection, so `CheckCtx::scope`, `scope.symbols`, and dry-run targets all see the touched set. A file in scope with no changed lines is untracked (git's diff omits it) and counts whole. Findings outside the range are left out, not flagged, as `--paths` and `--symbols` leave them out (design §6.4); every check that already filters by `Scope::includes` rules on the range unchanged: a clone group is reported when any member was touched, every per-symbol finding (complexity, CRAP, coverage, dead code, test quality, mutants) when its symbol was. File-granular checks (`static.deps`, `static.analyze`, contracts, generative) keep file granularity because their findings have no span that says what caused them; diff-native checks (`tests.weakening`, `static.api-surface`) already rule on the range. No check, producer, fact, schema, or renderer changed. Measured first: a full `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` with the narrowing moved three targets only (the symbol-matching golden, the agent snapshot, the README sections), because every other range test writes whole functions. (10) `release.yml`'s build job runs `Swatinem/rust-cache@d1527c8f67ef9af357290475d4af4b0fab8f12e0 # v2.9.3` keyed `${{ matrix.target }}` between the toolchain and `make dist`; GitHub scopes a cache to the ref that saved it, so a tag run restores what a `workflow_dispatch` dry run on `main` saved (a tag's own cache is visible only to that tag). Before/after times of the v0.2.0 tag run: _not yet measured; the integrator records the build jobs' durations from the run before the cache existed (v0.1.0's) and from the v0.2.0 run here._ (12) `agent::tests` reads the long flags of `check`, `survey`, `explain`, and `serve` from `cli::Cli::command()` (clap), not a list; the guide now names `--head`, `--tier`, `--jobs`, `--against`, `--oracle`, `--cases`, `--scaffold`, `--watch`, and says a range rules on the touched functions; the allow-list is `--since-baseline` (pending, unimplemented), `--socket` and `--stop` (daemon management an agent's loop never does), each with its reason in the test, and the test fails if an allowed flag disappears or becomes named. (13) `scripts/release.sh version --set X.Y.Z` (ShellCheck-clean; `sed` without `-i`, so BSD and GNU behave alike, run here with macOS's BSD `sed` only) rewrites the workspace `[workspace.package]` version, each `cupel-*` crate's entry in `Cargo.lock` (the fourth file: a bump without it breaks `--locked` builds), `dist/npm/package.json`, and `dist/pipx/cupel.py`, refuses anything but `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, and prints the new version; `make version V=X.Y.Z` calls it and the README's Makefile table lists it. ADR-143 defers items 9, 11, 14, 15 and the P9.4–P9.7 open items that are limits, not bugs, each with what reopens it; P9.6 (6) is a bug and becomes follow-up item 21.
- **P9.F2 — tests** (2658 → 2666, `cargo test` pass counts: 2658 is the 2666 `make check` passed at eb25740 less the 8 this step adds, none removed). `cupel-core/tests/scheduler.rs` `under_a_range_the_symbols_in_scope_are_those_its_changed_lines_touch`: a change at a symbol's last line keeps it and one past its span drops it, a file without changed lines keeps every symbol, and `--symbols` intersects with the range (failed on the old scheduler with both files' symbols in scope). `cupel-cli/tests/check.rs` `a_range_reports_a_clone_group_only_when_it_touched_a_member`, on a real git repository and the real binary: an edit to an unrelated function reports none of the pair that existed at the base (the tree run reports it), a third copy is reported with the two it copies, and an edit inside one member reports the pair (failed on the old scheduler, which reported the pair on the unrelated edit). `cupel-cli` `agent::tests::the_guide_names_every_flag_of_the_verbs_an_agent_runs` (failed listing the nine flags above). `cupel-cli/tests/release.rs`: `the_build_job_caches_each_targets_build` (the cache step sits between the toolchain and `make dist`, keyed by the target), `release_workflow_pins_every_action_to_a_commit` now expects `Swatinem/rust-cache`, `every_file_that_names_the_release_carries_the_crate_version` (all 13 versions: the workspace, ten lock entries, the two wrappers), `version_set_rewrites_the_version_in_every_file_that_names_it` (on a copy: exactly those 13 lines change, each only in its version), `version_set_refuses_what_is_not_a_version` (`v1.2.3`, `1.2`, `1.2.3-rc.1`, empty: exit 1 with the message, nothing written), `make_version_sets_the_version_through_the_release_script` (`make -n` and the README row); the three version tests failed against the old script. Goldens: `golden/symbol-matching/changed.json` changes two pre-existing values, `run.scope.symbols` 6 → 5 and `static.complexity`'s `score.n` 6 → 5: the fixture's head commit leaves `describe` (its last function, below every hunk) untouched, so it leaves the range's scope (ADR-142); no finding, metric, or other value moved. The agent snapshot and the README's "For agents" section change by the guide's new sentences; the README also gains the scope paragraph, the mutation-scoring sentence, and the `make version` row.
- **P9.F2 — dogfood.** `make dogfood` (`check --changed --depth deep --budget 30m --gate --no-install --skip tests.flaky --skip conc.race`, against `main` at 9e6ed93, through the lock) at fce5f32 (run_01M5H7M290GQ26G47X963JXSH9, 83 s): exit 2 (`cupel check` exit 5, partial), gate `changed` failing on one rule only, `tests.weakening` 1: `snapshot.rewritten` of `agent__the_guide_is_about_sixty_lines_of_markdown.snap`, the guide's deliberate new sentences (item 12; three lines extended, one added, none of its old text removed), as earlier steps' snapshot rewrites were; not suppressed. Every other rule evaluated passes: `static.clones` 0 (216 symbols compared, 0 pairs), complexity 0 (cyclomatic max 4), deps 0, dead-code 0, `tests.quality` 0, `static.analyze` 0; the budget left out the suite-based checks in that run (each estimated at 1 441–1 474 s). They ran in two other runs through the lock: the same `make dogfood` at 675aec7 (run_01M5GVYKHADAXBMKNS49H0FDGZ, 1 801 s; the same code but for the `within_range` refactor) passed `tests.coverage` (line 1.0, 0 uncovered), CRAP (max 3), `gen.property` (0 falsified), and `spec.contracts`, and found `schedule::selected_keys`' six parameters (fixed in fce5f32 by `within_range`); and `scripts/dogfood.sh cargo run --quiet --package cupel-cli -- check --changed --depth standard --no-gate --no-install --only tests.mutation --only tests.coverage --only static.crap --format text` at fce5f32 (2 534 s, exit 0): `tests.mutation` 1.0 (5 of 5 killed; under ADR-142 `standard` and `deep` score the same touched functions' mutants), `tests.coverage` 0 uncovered (line 0.985 over 132 lines), CRAP max 4.0. Two earlier `make dogfood` runs ended exit 4 on a red baseline that is load, not code, both in tests this step did not touch, both passing in `make check`: three `cupel-plugin` tests whose shell plugin's `describe` did not answer within 10 s, and `similarity::tests::ten_thousand_symbols_are_searched_in_linear_work_and_under_a_second`, while another worktree's deep `survey` held the load average at 40–80; `.cupel/cache/suite` was deleted before each rerun because this branch predates ADR-141 §2. Before and after on Cupel's own history, `static.clones` alone with this branch's binary: P9.5's range (`check --base 303cfb6` at 797fd22) reports 0 findings where P9.5's dogfood reported 4, and P9.6's (`--base 3c64d05` at eb9d38b) reports 1 where it reported 4: the Go/Rust/Java `register` cluster, whose Go member P9.6 edited, so it is in the range. On this branch, a temporary copy of `scope::select_symbols` added to `scope.rs` was reported by `check --changed --only static.clones` as two type-1 pairs (the function and its inner `hash_of`) although the original was untouched, and gone once the copy was removed. `make parity` passes at 45536e1 (501 tests, 0 failed). At the merge (7c51d01, `main` at 1bd8001), the tree dogfood failed `static.deps` on one new two-module cycle, `producer.rs → scope.rs → producer.rs`, which `scope::touched`'s import of `ChangedLines` closed; the changed gate had missed it because `producer.rs` was outside the range (ADR-142 §5's limit). The `ChangedLines` alias now lives in `scope.rs` (`producer.rs` and the crate root import it from there), the cycle is gone, and the tree dogfood passes (cd6bcc1).
- **P9.F2 — open items.** (1) Follow-up item 21 (P9.6 open item 6): under a range the dead-code module pass reads only the scope's import edges, so a touched `pub` item an unchanged file imports is an `info` `dead.export`; a bug, left for the integrator or a later step. (2) At `deep`, the mutation engine still mutates every function of the changed files while `tests.mutation` now scores only the touched functions' mutants (ADR-142 Consequences); narrowing the engine's deep plan to `Focus::changed` is `cupel-mutate`'s (P9.F1's module), and would cut the changed dogfood's mutation time. (3) A finding a change causes on a symbol it did not touch (a function left uncovered by a removed test, a symbol left dead by a removed caller) is graded by the tree gate, not the changed gate. (4) The release cache's before/after times await the v0.2.0 tag run. (5) `cargo publish --workspace` (the `publish-crates` job) needs a `version` on each path dependency in `[workspace.dependencies]`, which has none; `make version` would then rewrite those too. Seen, not changed: the crates.io names are the owner's (not on this list).

Shared: `.github/workflows/release.yml` (one step in `build`; `main` since dropped `best-effort` for Windows, ADR-137: no overlap, both apply), `Makefile` (`version` target and `.PHONY`; `main` added `windows-check windows-test` to the same `.PHONY` line: keep both), `README.md` (scope paragraph, mutation sentence, Makefile row, regenerated "For agents"; rerun `make readme` after merging), `crates/cupel-cli/tests/snapshots/agent__the_guide_is_about_sixty_lines_of_markdown.snap`, `crates/cupel-fixtures/golden/symbol-matching/changed.json`, this plan (the P9.F2 heading after P9.9, which `main`'s P9.F1 heading also follows: keep both; the follow-up list line, which P9.F1 also edits: keep its strikes of 16, 17, 19, 20 and these of 9–15, 18, and item 21). `main`'s `release.rs` gained `#![cfg(unix)]` (ADR-137); the tests added here are Unix-only too. ADR-143's link to ADR-137 resolves only after the merge. Any branch that adds a range test now gets the touched symbols in scope: a golden whose head commit leaves a function of a changed file untouched counts one symbol fewer, and its findings for that function disappear.
- **P9.F3 — design** ([ADR-144](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_144.md), amending ADR-135 §1). Option (b) of the brief: the `References` producer already resolves every file of the language with the same `Resolve` as `ModuleGraph` (`register_resolved`) and dropped the graph; it now keeps its edges as `References.imports` (`Vec<ImportEdge>`, optional and omitted when empty, fact version unchanged, as ADR-133's additions), and `static.dead-code`'s module pass reads the edges of both `ModuleGraph` (the scope) and `References.imports` (the language): a module is entered when either holds an edge into it from a file outside it, and suspect specifiers come from both. An importer outside the scope has no symbols, so no module namespace, and always counts as outside the module it imports. Option (a) (`ModuleGraph` over every file) was rejected: `static.deps` filters nothing by scope, so under a range it would report the tree's cycles, distances, and layering violations, and the mutation engine and `tests --impacted` would change through the fact; each would need its own scope filter in modules this step leaves alone. The check's needs, kinds, evidence, and version are unchanged; nothing else reads `References`. Blast radius measured before committing: on a tree scope the two facts hold the same edges, so no check golden moved.
- **P9.F3 — tests** (2679 → 2683, `make check` pass counts; 2679 is 2683 less the four added, none removed). `crates/cupel-fixtures/tests/dead_export_range.rs` (new) `a_python_export_only_an_unchanged_file_imports_is_not_dead_under_a_range` and `a_go_export_only_an_unchanged_package_imports_is_not_dead_under_a_range`: a real git history (`crates/cupel-fixtures/repos/dead-export-range/{python,go}/{base,head}`), the head commit editing only `shop.prices.price` (`prices.Price`), which only the unchanged `checkout.py` (`main.go`) imports; `check --base main --only static.dead-code` with the real binary has the function in scope and reports nothing (both failed before the fix with `dead.export` on it), with goldens `golden/dead-export-range/{python,go}.json`. `cupel-checks/tests/static_dead_code.rs` `an_import_from_outside_the_scope_that_only_references_carries_enters_the_module`: an edge only in `References.imports` enters its target, an unresolved one there lowers confidence as a suspect, and an export nothing imports is still `dead.export` (failed before the fix). `cupel-lang/tests/references.rs` `import_edges_come_from_every_file_of_the_language_whatever_the_scope`: scoped to `src/references.rs`, the Rust fixture's fact holds the `mod references;` and `use crate::references::imported_rate` edges of the two files outside the scope (failed before the producer kept them). Goldens: the seven `golden/facts/<lang>/references.json` gain `imports`, each exactly its `module-graph.json`'s edges (33–49 per language, compared programmatically); `schemas__fact_references.snap` gains the optional `imports` with the `ImportEdge`, `ImportTarget`, `ImportAlias`, and `EdgeKind` definitions. - **P9.F4 — design** ([ADR-145](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_145.md)). A performance test bounds its work, not its wall clock (`similarity`'s search: every planted pair compared, at most 2n exact comparisons). A test that acts once the other side is ready waits for that side's sign: a child or plugin writes `ready` after setting its SIGTERM trap and is cancelled then; the daemon's scripted handler counts runs begun (hang-up and stop tests), goes past its first streamed line only once the client holds it, and answers four concurrent runs only once all four are in flight; the dogfood lock test releases the first run once the second has said it waits. A ceiling is a hang guard (`HANG`, ten minutes, or an hour), equal to the length of what the test cut; lower bounds that are deterministic stay (a budget-cut run lasts at least its budget, an ignored SIGTERM is killed no sooner than the grace period after the cancellation, a wait lasts until its deadline). Budget scheduling tests leave the clock all the room their estimates allow (30 s under the costly check's 30.2 s default; an hour with a 3 599.6 s estimate for the overrun case). Every plugin test runs a freshly written plugin once (`warm`) before the host's 10 s `describe`/`plan` limit (ADR-088 §4) applies: the first execution of a new file on a loaded macOS took 2.1–4.0 s at a load average of ~100 (0.01–0.14 s for the second), the cause of the `plugins.rs` failures. No injected clock was needed (Cupel has none; the tests are about real deadlines), no production code changed, and no test went to a `make check-slow`, which does not exist.

- **P9.F4 — open items.** (1) `tests.flaky` stops at a red baseline (exit 4), so one test that flakes in the baseline hides every other from the engine; on a suite with a known flake the engine measures nothing. (2) The brief's config (`runner = "cargo"`) is what a user would write and pins nothing; `runner` accepts only a producer's id with or without `tool.` (`cargo-test`), and the error is a bare `unconfigured`. (3) Each rerun of Cupel's suite one test at a time took 1–2 hours under load; three seeds before and after cost about 30 machine-hours. (4) `tests.coverage` reports `similarity::seeds`, a `const fn` evaluated only in a `static` initializer at compile time, as never run: no run can cover it. (5) `serve::unwatched` (the `--watch` failure) has no test. (6) On macOS the slowness under load is partly `XprotectService`, `syspolicyd`, and `mds` checking and indexing new files, which the load average does not show; a loop that relinks binaries or writes many new executables slows every agent's process starts. (7) The store's 5 s writer-lock wait and the process spawn's ETXTBSY wait (`BUSY_WAIT`) are production constants a loaded machine could outlast; no test failed on them here.

PyPI: `dist/pipx/pyproject.toml` `name` `cupel-cli` with `[tool.flit.module] name = "cupel"` (flit otherwise looks for `cupel_cli.py`), console script `cupel`; route `pipx install cupel-cli`. crates.io: route unchanged (`cargo install --locked cupel-cli`), pending reason now names the library crates and `cupel-core`'s owner; the library crates keep their names, and ADR-146 lists the owner's options (`cupel-cli-` prefix, another prefix, vendoring). `publish-crates` gains a `names` step that asks the crates.io API about each publishable crate and skips (notice, step-summary line, exit 0) when one is held by a crate whose `repository` is not ours; the dry run and the publish run only when none is. - **P9.F6 — design** ([ADR-147](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_147.md)). A mutant's timeout is `min(max_mutant_timeout, max(2 s, timeout_factor × reference))` (`Settings::timeout`). The cap is a `tests.mutation` setting (`[checks."tests.mutation"]`, per-language override as any setting); because the design forbade a producer to read `[checks.*]`, a check's setting may now declare the fact it configures (`SettingSpec::configures`), and the fact store hands the configured values of every registered check's such settings to that fact's producers as `ProduceCtx::settings` (unconfigured: absent, the producer's default applies; joint producers and plugin producers get none). Design §6.7 and §9.3 and ADR-080's status line are amended. "Cannot be narrowed" is per mutant run: a mutant whose tests a per-test map selected runs them all; one that runs the whole suite (no map for the runner, or a site the map names no test for) leaves out every baseline test over 60 000 ms, as do the file's reference and schemata validation runs, where the runner can leave a test out: `TestSelection::Except` and `RunnerSpec::exclude` (`cargo nextest -E 'not (…)'`, `go test -skip`, `pytest --deselect`; every other runner cannot, and its slow tests stay in under the cap). `MutationOutcomes.excluded` (optional, version unchanged) lists them; the check emits one `mutant.slow-test-excluded` (info, review) per test. A whole-suite mutant's cache key holds the excluded IDs only when some are excluded, so stored outcomes stay valid. ADR-147 §5 is the per-runner table (also in the README). The brief says Cupel's Clojure runner cannot run one test by name; it can (`tests_or_files`, by var), but it has no per-test map, so its mutants always run the whole suite and it has no exclusion flag.
- **P9.F6 — tests** (2691 before by `make test`'s pass count at the branch point, 2707 after by `make check` at c228134; +16). New: `producer_inputs::a_producer_gets_the_settings_checks_declare_for_its_fact_as_configured_for_its_language` and `an_unconfigured_setting_for_a_fact_is_left_to_the_producer` (the scheduler forwards declared settings with per-language overrides, only those that configure the fact); `plugin_protocol::a_setting_that_configures_a_fact_names_its_kind`; `selection::the_whole_suite_but_some_tests_becomes_the_runners_flag_that_leaves_them_out`, `a_runner_that_cannot_leave_a_test_out_gives_no_command_for_one`, and real runs `cargo_nextest_runs_every_test_but_one`, `go_test_runs_every_test_but_one`, `pytest_runs_every_test_but_one`; `cupel-mutate` `settings::a_cap_that_is_not_a_duration_is_refused`, `a_mutant_gets_the_factor_times_its_reference_at_least_two_seconds_and_at_most_the_cap`, `the_cap_a_check_declares_for_the_engines_fact_defaults_to_the_engines_own`; `tests_mutation::the_cap_on_a_mutants_run_is_its_one_setting_and_configures_the_engines_fact`, `a_cap_that_is_not_a_duration_is_a_configuration_error`, `each_slow_test_left_out_of_whole_suite_runs_is_one_info_finding_naming_its_duration` (`any_setting_is_a_configuration_error` kept, now listing the one valid setting); `cupel-fixtures` `mutant_timeout::a_mutant_that_loops_is_cut_at_max_mutant_timeout` (the real binary on `repos/mutant-timeout/loop`, Python, `timeout_factor = 100000`, cap `30s`: both looping mutants `timeout`, the check within a 10-minute hang guard; golden `mutant-timeout/loop.json`) and `a_slow_test_no_per_test_selection_narrows_is_left_out_of_every_mutant_with_a_finding` (`repos/mutant-timeout/slow`, Go, a 61 s `TestSlowIntegration`: one finding with its baseline duration, and `Half`'s mutants survive with `covering_tests` the fast test alone; run without daemon parity, since the duration is measured). The cap was first `3s`; at load average ~180 every pytest run of the fixture exceeded it, so it is `30s`. Goldens (additive): `tests.mutation/describe.json` (the kind and the setting), the `run`, `plugin`, and `mutation-outcomes` schema snapshots (`configures`, `excluded`), the README's check and settings tables; the SARIF render snapshot gains the rule and so the one later rule's `ruleIndex` moves 6 → 7, the only changed pre-existing value. Tests and code were written together for the core, adapt, and engine pieces (each test seen passing, not first failing); the fixture tests found two real mistakes (a too-tight cap, `go test` naming no file).

(2) `go test -skip`/`-run` match by name across a root's packages. (3) A stored `timeout` is reused under a larger cap (the cap is not in the cache key, as `timeout_factor` is not). (4) A slow test that alone kills a mutant makes it survive in a whole-suite run; the note names the test. (5) 60 s is a constant.

- **P9.F7 — design** ([ADR-148](adr/CUPEL_DECISION_148.md)). The 2026-10-24 panic (`watchdog timeout: no checkins from watchdogd in 92 seconds`, the compressor at 100% of its limit, five swap files; serde_json's `regression` and `test` binaries the busiest threads) was memory, which ADR-147's cap does not bound: four slots each ran `cargo nextest` with a test process per core (thirty-two on eight cores), and nothing watched what a run held. Now `Execution::options` hands every run of a production `parallelism = max(1, cores / slots)` (`settings::threads`) and `memory = max_mutant_memory / slots` (`Settings::memory_per_slot`, physical memory from `hw.memsize` or `/proc/meminfo`); `cargo nextest` takes the parallelism as `--test-threads` and `--build-jobs` (its own `Varying`, `FIXED` removed), `cargo test` as `--test-threads` and `--jobs`. `RunOptions::memory` reaches `Process::memory`, whose wait loop reads the child's process group from a `ps -A -o pgid= -o rss=` sample shared by every running process and renewed when older than 200 ms (`cupel-core/src/memory.rs`, no `unsafe`), kills the group over the budget, and returns `Error::OutOfMemory { limit }`; the engine makes that `MutantOutcome::OutOfMemory` for a mutant and a production error naming the setting for a reference or validation run. `tests.mutation` declares `max_mutant_memory` (`MemoryLimit`: `3g`, `512m`, or `50%`; `configures = mutation-outcomes`, as the cap), the kind `mutant.out-of-memory` (info, review, `Site` evidence), and the metric `out_of_memory`. A run over the budget overshoots it by what it allocates in one sample interval; Windows has no `ps`, so no budget is enforced there.
- **P9.F7 — tests** (2716 before by summing `make test`'s result lines on `main` at 8c35d24, 2726 after; the renamed `the_cap_on_a_mutants_run_is_its_one_setting_and_configures_the_engines_fact` becomes `the_cap_and_the_budget_of_a_mutants_run_are_its_settings_and_configure_the_engines_fact`). New: `config_settings::memory_limits_parse_whole_numbers_with_a_unit_or_a_share_of_physical_memory` and `memory_limits_reject_everything_else`; `process::a_child_whose_group_holds_more_than_its_memory_budget_is_killed_with_the_group` (a background `perl` holding 256 MiB under a 64 MiB budget, the shell waiting on it: the group dies), `a_child_within_its_memory_budget_runs_to_its_end`, `the_machines_physical_memory_is_what_the_platform_reports` (against `sysctl hw.memsize` or `/proc/meminfo` read by the test); `selection::cargo_nextest_takes_the_runs_parallelism_as_its_test_threads` (a real run whose command ends `--test-threads 1 --build-jobs 1` and whose two tests pass) and `a_run_whose_processes_hold_more_than_its_memory_budget_is_stopped_as_out_of_memory` (a real `cargo nextest` under a one-byte budget); `cupel-mutate` `settings::a_budget_that_is_neither_a_size_nor_a_share_is_refused`, `each_slot_gets_an_equal_part_of_the_budget_and_of_the_cores`, `the_budget_a_check_declares_for_the_engines_fact_is_the_engines_default`; `tests_mutation::a_budget_that_is_neither_a_size_nor_a_share_is_a_configuration_error` and `a_mutant_killed_over_its_memory_budget_is_a_note_left_out_of_the_score`; `cupel-fixtures` `mutant_memory::a_mutant_that_loops_while_allocating_is_killed_over_its_memory_budget` (the real binary on `repos/mutant-memory/grow`, Python: two mutants of `grow` loop holding four more MiB every ten milliseconds, one slot with a 128 MiB budget and a 10 s cap as the backstop; both `out-of-memory`, four killed, score 1; golden `mutant-memory/grow.json`). Goldens (additive): every `tests.mutation` envelope and `mutant-timeout/loop.json` gain `out_of_memory: 0`, `tests.mutation/describe.json` the kind, the metric, and the setting, `schemas__fact_mutation-outcomes.snap` the outcome; the SARIF render snapshot gains the rule, so the one later rule's `ruleIndex` moves 7 → 8, the only changed pre-existing value. Each test was seen failing before its code (the fixture test first failed against a stale `cupel` binary, which `cargo test -p cupel-fixtures` does not rebuild; `make test` does).
- **P9.F7 — dogfood.** `make dogfood` (changed, `--depth deep --budget 30m`, through the lock) at 37db733: exit 5 (partial), the gate failing on `tests.weakening` (the two snapshot rewrites above, each this step's deliberate contract change, as P9.F6 recorded for its own), `static.complexity` (`tests_mutation::manifest` at 74 lines, `Settings::read` at cognitive 16), `static.clones` (`TimeLimit::from_str` with `MemoryLimit::from_str`, the unit-suffix matches with `Format::as_str`, the two declared-default tests), and `tests.quality` (three weak assertions); all fixed at aaa6b1d (one `number_with_suffix` parser for both limits, the memory units as a table, `settings()` and `Settings::declared` extracted, every assertion pinned). The second run at aaa6b1d: exit 5 (partial), the gate failing only on `tests.weakening`: the two snapshots, and the merged declared-default test counted as deleted, so the cap's test is back under its name at edcebb3 and the budget's is its own (`cupel check --changed --only tests.weakening --only static.clones` then shows the two snapshots alone). Complexity (cyclomatic max 8, cognitive max 13), clones, deps, dead code, `tests.quality`, `tests.coverage` (0.983), CRAP (max 9), properties, and contracts pass; `tests.mutation` partial under the budget both runs (650 s, then 519 s). Outside the gate: `static.api-surface` `api.breaking` on `runner::FIXED` (removed on purpose: nothing varies nothing any more) and nine `api.widened`; `meta.oracle-independence` on two self-authored tests. During the second run the machine's memory pressure level stayed at 1 (normal) in every two-minute sample, and one slot's whole process group held about 840 MiB while it rebuilt with two jobs, within its 3 GiB share. `make check` green at edcebb3 (2726 tests). `make reclaim` run afterwards.
- **P9.F7 — open items.** (1) The budget is sampled: a run allocating at several GB/s overshoots its share by up to 200 ms of allocation; `/proc` on Linux or a safe `proc_pidinfo` wrapper on macOS would measure finer and cheaper. (2) No budget on Windows. (3) The runners without a parallelism flag (pytest, unittest, Surefire, `clojure.test`) still run at their own parallelism per slot. (4) A stored outcome is reused under a changed budget, as under a changed cap. (5) Cupel's own schemata build in a slot links with the share's jobs; whether the default budget admits every Cupel mutant on a 16 GB machine is unmeasured.
- **P9.F7 — for the integrator.** Mine: `docs/adr/CUPEL_DECISION_148.md`, `crates/cupel-core/src/memory.rs`, `crates/cupel-fixtures/repos/mutant-memory/`, `crates/cupel-fixtures/tests/mutant_memory.rs`, `crates/cupel-fixtures/golden/mutant-memory/grow.json`. Changed: `cupel-core` (`config/settings.rs` `MemoryLimit`, `config/mod.rs`, `error.rs` `Error::OutOfMemory`, `process.rs` `Process::memory`, `producer.rs` `RunOptions::memory`, `mutation.rs` `MutantOutcome::OutOfMemory`, `lib.rs`), `cupel-adapt` (`runner.rs` the budget into `Process` and `FIXED` removed, `cargo_nextest.rs` its `Varying`, `cargo_test.rs` `--jobs`), `cupel-mutate` (`settings.rs`, `execute.rs`), `cupel-checks` (`tests_mutation.rs`), their tests, the README (mutation section and generated tables), design §6.7, §8.3 table, and §9.3, the status lines of ADR-080 and ADR-147. Shared snapshots: `cupel-core/tests/snapshots/schemas__fact_mutation-outcomes.snap`, `cupel-cli/src/render/snapshots/…sarif…the_fixture_envelope_renders_as_its_snapshot.snap`, `golden/tests.mutation/describe.json`, every `golden/tests.mutation/*.json`. A branch that builds a `RunOptions` literal adds `memory`; one that matches on `MutantOutcome` exhaustively adds `OutOfMemory`; one that touches those snapshots regenerates them after merging this.

## 4. Cross-phase checklist

Run this at the end of every step, before committing:

- [ ] Failing test was written and observed failing before implementation
- [ ] `make check` green (fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, tests, docs, clean tree)
- [ ] No `todo!()`, `unimplemented!()`, commented-out code, or stray files
- [ ] Every new file has an `// ABOUTME:` header
- [ ] `describe` reflects any new verb, flag, check, finding kind, fact, or producer; `describe matrix` is what you expect
- [ ] No language name appears in `cupel-checks`; no check name appears in `cupel-lang`, `cupel-adapt`, or `cupel-mutate`
- [ ] Goldens updated deliberately (`UPDATE_GOLDENS=1`) and the diff reviewed, never blindly
- [ ] Status line and Notes in this document updated
- [ ] ADR written if a decision was made
- [ ] README reflects what exists (at phase boundaries at minimum)
- [ ] Committed
